# Judicial Case Index

Cases decided by the Standing Judicial Commission (SJC) and its predecessor the Committee on Judicial Business (CJB), grouped by Assembly. For canonical case IDs, proceeding types, review standards, editorial summaries, BCO provisions, and topic tags, see **[JUDICIAL-CASES.md](JUDICIAL-CASES.md)**.

This index is **structure-first**: every case listed links to a full-text page re-extracted verbatim from the volume (with its opinions). After the decided cases, an Assembly may list extra rows from the underlying case table: *decided at Nth GA* — the case was only listed here (deferred to a later Assembly, or cited from an earlier one) and links to where it was actually decided; *reference / no separate decision* — a cross-reference, roll-up, or out-of-order/withdrawn matter with no published decision; *not yet re-extracted* — the volume is still pending.


## 3rd General Assembly (1975)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [3-12](../cases/ga03_1975__case1.md) | Rev. Harold L. Webb & Rev. Thomas Miller v. Pacific Presbytery | Dismissed | Ministers complained that Pacific Presbytery conducted nonroutine business without the constitutionally required quorum of three teaching and three ruling elders. The Assembly agreed that the August 4, 1975 actions were irregular, but dismissed the complaint because Presbytery later met with a quorum and ratified those actions. The dismissal therefore reflected correction of the defect, not approval of proceeding without a quorum. | [full text](../cases/ga03_1975__case1.md) |

## 4th General Assembly (1976)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [4-12](../cases/ga04_1976__case1.md) | Session of Trinity Presbyterian Church, Slidell, Louisiana v. Grace Presbytery | Remanded | Trinity Church complained that Grace Presbytery's July 9, 1976 substitute motion, delaying Adrian Pater's ordination until he was called to PCA work, was an out-of-order reconsideration. The Judicial Commission did not reach the merits: because the BCO 43-5 record was not complied with and minutes were inadequate, it returned the whole matter to the lower court for a new hearing. The Assembly accepted that judgment and separately adopted, 238–131, a recommendation that the BCO does not envisage ordination expressly for pastoral service in another denomination. | [full text](../cases/ga04_1976__case1.md) |
| [4-13](../cases/ga04_1976__case2.md) | Session of Eastland Presbyterian Church, Memphis, Tennessee v. Covenant Presbytery | Not sustained | A session complained that Covenant Presbytery violated PCA standards by approving the summer employment of a divorced and remarried man in a ministerial capacity. The Judicial Commission found the complaint invalid, holding that McKeown was not employed in a ministerial capacity. It expressly did not decide whether such employment would violate the Constitution. The General Assembly adopted the report as its action and judgment, leaving the presbytery's action undisturbed. | [full text](../cases/ga04_1976__case2.md) |

## 5th General Assembly (1977)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [5-13 / 5-87](../cases/ga05_1977__case1.md) | Rev. Paul L. Karlberg and Ruling Elder James G. Luce v. Central Georgia Presbytery | Not sustained | Complainants Karlberg and Luce challenged Central Georgia Presbytery’s commission as biased—it heard the Faith Church minority but not the majority—and challenged its judicial designation and recommendations. After Presbytery rescinded recommendations 2–6, complainants dropped those reasons, leaving only the bias claim. A General Assembly judicial commission heard both sides and unanimously declined to sustain. It reasoned both parties acted in good faith, mistakes were inadvertent, and remand would not further Christ’s cause. The rescinded recommendations and designation were not adjudicated. | [full text](../cases/ga05_1977__case1.md) |
| [5-14 / 5-86](../cases/ga05_1977__case2.md) | Rev. [REDACTED] (Kim) v. Presbytery of Texas | Case deemed abandoned |  | [full text](../cases/ga05_1977__case2.md) |
| [5-15 / 5-83](../cases/ga05_1977__case3.md) | Rev. Thomas Barnes et al. v. Evangel Presbytery | Not sustained | The Fifth General Assembly approved its Judicial Commission’s refusal to sustain a complaint challenging Presbytery’s approval of Mercer for ordination as an evangelist without a call and for parachurch work outside its bounds. No reason for rejecting the complaint was stated. The ruling nevertheless required Presbytery to have—and itself issue—a written call before implementing the ordination decision, and requested referral of the broader question defining a valid ministerial call to the Permanent Committee on Judicial Business. | [full text](../cases/ga05_1977__case3.md) |

## 6th General Assembly (1978)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [case](../cases/ga06_1978__case1.md) | James H. Campbell v. Mid-Atlantic Presbytery | Remanded | Ruling Elder James H. Campbell complained that Mid-Atlantic Presbytery approved a commission report finding charges against Rev. Robert Schoof inadmissible rather than proceeding on them. The General Assembly adopted the Committee on Judicial Business recommendation ordering Presbytery to hold a new hearing on the original charges under BCO 44-5, to conform to the BCO, and to keep the principals informed; it also appointed a judicial commission for any further complaint or appeal. The adopted report gives no further reasoning and does not expressly rule on the late-filing request. | [full text](../cases/ga06_1978__case1.md) |
| [case](../cases/ga06_1978__case2.md) | Luther L. Allin Jr. et al. v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery | Partially sustained; Denied; Remanded | Session members challenged Presbytery’s order to hold a congregational meeting on officer rotation and its handling of the complaint hearing. The Judicial Commission held that ten days ran from the notice’s postmark and that Bristow and Pastor Vitullo could not participate as members of the reviewing court. It nevertheless upheld Presbytery’s authority to require the meeting: a sufficient petition left the Session no option to refuse, while the congregation retained authority to decide rotation. The complaint was sustained in part and remanded for a properly noticed hearing. | [full text](../cases/ga06_1978__case2.md) |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| [7-57](../cases/ga07_1979__case1.md) | Ruling Elder James H. Campbell v. Presbytery of the Mid-Atlantic | Complaint partially sustained; Commission sustained complaint on grounds 1, 2, and 3; Presbytery directed to proceed with trial |  | [full text](../cases/ga07_1979__case1.md) |

## 8th General Assembly (1980)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [3](../cases/ga08_1980__case1.md) | Teaching Elders Byron Snapp and James M. Hope v. Calvary Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | Teaching Elders challenged Calvary Presbytery’s use of the extraordinary clause to examine and ordain a candidate who had completed only one quarter of Hebrew. The Assembly sustained the complaint’s single specification because Presbytery had not demonstrated either extraordinary circumstances or the man’s proven extraordinary gifts. It found the ordination contrary to the Book of Church Order and remanded for correction. Rather than answer the broader question about proper use of the clause, it ordered a report to the Ninth General Assembly. | [full text](../cases/ga08_1980__case1.md) |
| [4](../cases/ga08_1980__case2.md) | Ruling Elder James H. Campbell et al. v. Presbytery of Mid-Atlantic | dismissed (absent complainant/counsel) |  | [full text](../cases/ga08_1980__case2.md) |
| [5](../cases/ga08_1980__case3.md) | Teaching Elder Carl W. Bogue et al. v. Presbytery of the Ascension  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | Complainants challenged Presbytery's sustaining of ordination trials for a candidate who affirmed continuing revelation through interpreted tongues and its approval of a campus-ministry call lacking required terms. The Assembly sustained both counts, holding that the examination left the candidate's views unclarified and failed adequately to protect the Standards' teaching on the finality and sufficiency of Scripture; Presbytery conceded the call's omission. It remanded the matter to obtain a BCO 21-1-compliant call and re-examine the candidate. | [full text](../cases/ga08_1980__case3.md) |

## 9th General Assembly (1981)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [case](../cases/ga09_1981__case1.md) | Ruling Elder Cooke Lewis, et al. v. Grace Presbytery | Not sustained | Members of a congregation challenged Grace Presbytery's formation of an administrative commission, its method of appointing the commission, and its refusal to dissolve the pastoral relationship after a 26-16 congregational vote. The General Assembly adopted its Judicial Commission's judgment declining to sustain each item or the complaint as a whole because none of the challenged actions violated the BCO. It also urged all parties to continue efforts toward reconciliation. | [full text](../cases/ga09_1981__case1.md) |
| [case](../cases/ga09_1981__case2.md) | Ruling Elder Robert H. Zeigler v. Presbytery of Texas | Not sustained; Affirmed | A ruling elder complained that the Presbytery of Texas, handling a complaint against a session, appointed a committee rather than the requested commission and had that committee meet with the pastor, session members, and congregants before meeting the complainants. The Assembly adopted the Judicial Commission's report and unanimously confirmed the lower court's judgment (10-0). No adopted reasons or issue-level merits appear, so the case's value is a sparse example of a confirmed judgment without explanation. | [full text](../cases/ga09_1981__case2.md) |
| [case](../cases/ga09_1981__case3.md) | Teaching Elder Rodney King, et al. v. Presbytery of Western Carolinas | Not sustained | A complaint challenged Presbytery's one-year delay before accepting a petition to organize a mission congregation. The General Assembly adopted the Judicial Commission's report and did not sustain the complaint. It reasoned BCO 5-1(1) gives Presbytery discretion over the time for organization, the group had sought only mission status, and the dispute was best handled at Presbytery, where complainants could seek reconsideration. A dissent recommending sustain and annulment was admitted but not adopted. | [full text](../cases/ga09_1981__case3.md) |

## 10th General Assembly (1982)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1](../cases/ga10_1982__case1.md) | Stephen M. Lee, et al. v. Gulf Coast Presbytery | Partially sustained | A Session challenged Gulf Coast Presbytery’s refusal to sustain Raymond Bradford Fell’s ordination examination, contending that his theonomic view was improperly treated as disqualifying and that prejudicial materials affected the process. The General Assembly sustained specifications 1, 2, and 5, but not 3 and 4. It ordered re-examination after direct guidance on specific biblical and confessional deficiencies, while expressly refusing to dictate the result. It also urged that no view of the judicial law’s present application should itself define orthodoxy or heresy. | [full text](../cases/ga10_1982__case1.md) |
| [2](../cases/ga10_1982__case2.md) | Charles H. Davis, et al. v. Presbytery of Central Georgia | Sustained | A teaching elder complained that Central Georgia Presbytery wrongly refused to sustain his complaint against a session that closed a disciplinary matter involving unbiblical separation, divorce, and remarriage before any judicial action. The General Assembly's Judicial Commission sustained the complaint, treating the session's "closing of the matter" as the central issue. It did not grant the requested reproof, suspension, or excommunication; it requested the presbytery to aid the session in corrections and heed WCF 24:6 and SC 87 on divorce and repentance. | [full text](../cases/ga10_1982__case2.md) |
| [3](../cases/ga10_1982__case3.md) | David H. Bryson, et al. v. Presbytery of Central Georgia | Sustained | Teaching elders challenged Central Georgia Presbytery’s refusal to sustain a complaint against Liberty Presbyterian Church’s Session for closing a disciplinary matter before taking judicial action. The General Assembly sustained the complaint, adopting findings that counseling, Session discussion, and instruction on discipline were underway before the Session closed the matter and refused to reopen it. It requested Presbytery to assist the Session with appropriate corrections or further action and said both courts should heed confessional teaching on marriage and repentance. The complainants’ requested affirmations were not expressly ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga10_1982__case3.md) |
| [4](../cases/ga10_1982__case4.md) | Vaughn E. Hathaway, Jr., et al. v. Grace Presbytery | Not sustained | Complainants challenged Grace Presbytery's refusal to ask the Assembly to assume original jurisdiction over allegations against a minister in Western Carolinas Presbytery. The Assembly did not sustain the complaint because Western Carolinas had not refused jurisdiction. It nevertheless directed that presbytery to proceed at its next stated meeting and held the one-year limitation inapplicable because its commission had unduly delayed formal process. The ruling was conditional, not an unqualified endorsement of the handling below. | [full text](../cases/ga10_1982__case4.md) |
| [5](../cases/ga10_1982__case5.md) | James K. Workman, et al. (Session of Peace Presbyterian Church) v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Sustained | The Session challenged Eastern Carolina Presbytery’s refusal to rescind its resolution calling voluntary total abstinence from alcohol the proper Christian lifestyle. The General Assembly unanimously sustained the complaint, adopting the commission’s conclusion that the resolution in practice bound consciences and exceeded Scripture’s requirements contrary to the Westminster Confession. The judgment did not expressly rescind the resolution, and it distinguished improper conscience-binding from permissible teaching and counseling against alcohol abuse and intemperance. | [full text](../cases/ga10_1982__case5.md) |
| [6](../cases/ga10_1982__case6.md) | Session of First Presbyterian Church, Stanley, North Carolina v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained | The Session challenged Presbytery’s refusal to receive complaints and associated voting, executive-session, elder-emeritus, interpretive, and disciplinary actions. The General Assembly sustained items 1, 2, and 5, but not 3, 4, and 6. Receiving a complaint under BCO 43-1 was not subject to a vote; when heard, complainants and respondents could not participate. Executive session was permissible, an elder emeritus retained full privileges, and item 6 was improper in this court. No reason was given for item 5. Presbytery was instructed to receive and hear the complaints properly. | [full text](../cases/ga10_1982__case6.md) |

## 11th General Assembly (1983)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1](../cases/ga11_1983__case1.md) | Robert L. Reymond et al. v. Illiana Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | A teaching elder confessed adultery, but Presbytery charged him only with ninth-commandment violations, admonished him, and required him to reconsider his call. The complaint alleged Presbytery failed to take BCO 34-7 action and improperly reduced the charges. The Commission sustained both items and remanded for a new trial, reasoning that the confessed sins were not considered and the censure was not commensurate with the offenses charged; it did not itself convict or sentence him. It adopted a recommendation directing Presbytery to follow BCO disciplinary procedures. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case1.md) |
| [13](../cases/ga11_1983__case10.md) | Donald A. Codling et al. v. Presbytery of Eastern Canada | Partially sustained | Presbytery members challenged Eastern Canada Presbytery’s denial of Ron Harris’s licensure. The complaint was sustained regarding the improperly adopted supermajority rule and the insufficiency of objections concerning counseling, evangelistic preaching, and sovereign grace; claims that pertinent data was excluded and requests to hear Harris preach were blocked were not sustained. Citing the fragmented examination and sharply differing views, the Assembly urged a complete reexamination at one meeting. It did not order Harris licensed or formally remand the matter. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case10.md) |
| [2](../cases/ga11_1983__case2.md) | Val H. Barleman et al. v. Presbytery of Ascension | Sustained; Annulled | The General Assembly sustained a complaint against Ascension Presbytery for sustaining a candidate's ordination trials despite exceptions to PCA constitutional standards. The Commission upheld specifications that his Lord's Supper and baptism views denied required visible-church membership and that his women-deacons view conflicted with the BCO; it rejected specifications on Sabbath recreation and immersion. The Assembly annulled the Presbytery's sustaining action, but not the ordination itself, so the candidate remained on the roll. It directed correction of the trial and pastoral instruction, with possible discipline if his views persisted. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case2.md) |
| [7](../cases/ga11_1983__case3.md) | Stark Wilbor et al. v. North Georgia Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled | Complainants challenged North Georgia Presbytery’s approval of John Parrish’s transfer examination after he denied that the first day of the week is the Christian Sabbath. The General Assembly sustained the specification of error and annulled Presbytery’s action, characterizing the teaching as a fundamental element of doctrine. Presbytery was directed to instruct Parrish and, only if his view conformed to Scripture, the doctrinal standards, and the BCO, re-examine him in that area. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case3.md) |
| [6](../cases/ga11_1983__case4.md) | "Determined Minority" of New Life Presbyterian Church v. Judicial Commission of Central Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained | Members of New Life Presbyterian Church challenged Central Carolina Presbytery's commission over its handling of membership dismissals and compliance with earlier Assembly directions. The General Assembly commission held that the presbytery commission exceeded BCO 57-5 membership requirements and sustained a related item in part, but did not sustain the remaining claims, including that the commission had failed to act or caused the entire conflict. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case4.md) |
| [8](../cases/ga11_1983__case5.md) | Jack Eubanks et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained | Complainants challenged Presbytery’s called meeting and its actions concerning a judicial commission and church membership. The adopted adjudication sustained the objection to dissolving the commission by a 14–12 vote because Robert’s Rules required a two-thirds vote, and sustained without stated grounds the objection to ordering the dissolved commission to reassemble and revote. It rejected the challenges to the called meeting, Presbytery’s membership action, and its consideration of David Alexander’s complaint; the membership claim failed because church membership is not itself a court. No express remedy was ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case5.md) |
| [10](../cases/ga11_1983__case6.md) | David Lockwood Jr. and Evan Holyfield v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained; Not sustained | David Lockwood Jr. and Evan Holyfield challenged Central Carolina Presbytery's commission for allegedly failing to act and mishandling discipline and reconciliation at New Life Presbyterian Church. The General Assembly commission rejected the failure-to-act claims, citing twenty-three meetings, and found that the complainants had not followed the reconciliation plan; it nevertheless sustained one overlapping membership-discipline item in part by reference to its ruling in Case 6. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case6.md) |
| [12](../cases/ga11_1983__case7.md) | Jack Eubanks et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained | Complainants challenged a called Presbytery meeting and its actions concerning a judicial commission and church membership. The adjudication sustained objections to dissolving the commission by a 14–12 vote and ordering the dissolved commission to reconvene and revote. A commission with power could be dismissed only by a two-thirds vote under Robert’s Rules; no separate ground was stated for the reconvening issue. Challenges to the meeting, Presbytery’s membership action, and its receipt of another complaint were not sustained. No relief was specified. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case7.md) |
| [15](../cases/ga11_1983__case8.md) | W. Ted Smith Jr. v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Not sustained | A presbytery was challenged for allegedly failing to carry out General Assembly directives restoring dismissed New Life congregation members and for allowing the pastor and session to evade compliance; the complainant asked the Committee on Judicial Business to dissolve the pastoral relation and impose censure. The CJB did not sustain the complaint. The supplied record states that disposition but contains no adopted reasoning, findings, or remedial order, so the basis for the ruling cannot be stated. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case8.md) |
| [9](../cases/ga11_1983__case9.md) | Session of Village Chapel Presbyterian Church v. Presbytery of Eastern Carolina | Abandoned; Affirmed | The Session of Village Chapel Presbyterian Church complained that Eastern Carolina Presbytery improperly lifted its matter from the table, alleging refusal of reasonable indulgence, improper evidence, and haste. The Commission adjudged the complaint abandoned: after public notice and a two-day recess, the complainant neither appeared nor sent counsel and gave no satisfactory explanation, as BCO 42-10 requires for complaints. Separately, it confirmed the SCJB's Reference 8 advice—six questions belonged in complaint or review and control; the seventh only required referring the applicant to the BCO. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case9.md) |
| [1983-12](../cases/ga11_1983__case7.md) | Jack Eubanks et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained | Complainants challenged a called Presbytery meeting and its actions concerning a judicial commission and church membership. The adjudication sustained objections to dissolving the commission by a 14–12 vote and ordering the dissolved commission to reconvene and revote. A commission with power could be dismissed only by a two-thirds vote under Robert’s Rules; no separate ground was stated for the reconvening issue. Challenges to the meeting, Presbytery’s membership action, and its receipt of another complaint were not sustained. No relief was specified. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case7.md) |
| [1983-14](../cases/ga11_1983__case14.md) | RE Truman Swain et al. v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Abandoned | A session's complaint against Eastern Carolina Presbytery for lifting from the table a matter concerning the church was abandoned because neither the complainant nor counsel appeared before the Commission within two days, despite public notice and a recess, and no satisfactory explanation was given. The Commission also confirmed and adopted the SCJB's interim advice on Reference 8: six questions were properly declined as redressable by complaint or review and control, while the seventh only required referring the applicant to the BCO. | [full text](../cases/ga11_1983__case14.md) |

## 12th General Assembly (1984)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [12-80](../cases/ga12_1984__case1.md) | Elder Roy J. Wuckovich v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Not sustained; Affirmed | A ruling elder complained that Central Carolina Presbytery violated the BCO in examining and approving Rick Downs for ordination, citing five specifications about the call, Downs’s Sabbath exception, the revote after an initial disapproval, lack of Scriptural search, and ordination questions. The judicial commission held points 1, 2, 4, and 5 not sustained 11–0 and point 3 not sustained 10–1; the general complaint was not sustained 11–0, and the lower court decision was confirmed. Its explanatory opinion said the commission disagreed on the Sabbath/subscription question and acted largely because the specifications did not substantiate the complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga12_1984__case1.md) |
| [12-81](../cases/ga12_1984__case2.md) | Russell D. Toms and TE William L. Thompson v. Central Florida Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | Central Florida Presbytery received a minister while he had a pending call to labor in another presbytery. A Judicial Commission sustained the specification of error, holding that BCO 13-2,5 barred receiving him under those circumstances; the vote was 9–4. It also voted 13–0 to remand the entire matter for proper procedures regarding his admission to the PCA. The Commission commended the presbytery's aim of receiving him but ruled the process erroneous, leaving the admission to be handled through proper procedures. | [full text](../cases/ga12_1984__case2.md) |
| [12-82](../cases/ga12_1984__case3.md) | Warren Gardner, et al. v. Presbytery of North Georgia | Not sustained | A complaint challenged North Georgia Presbytery's transfer of Trinity Mission, Elberton, Georgia, to Central Georgia Presbytery, alleging insufficient debate and failure to examine discipline issues. The Judicial Commission unanimously did not sustain the specification of error. Its adopted explanation found that two years of contact and discussions had done as much as possible to clear misunderstanding. It nevertheless noted the presbytery's inadequate explanation and the complainants' failure to secure adequate information; no relief was granted, and the listed questions about congregational choice and transfer power were not answered. | [full text](../cases/ga12_1984__case3.md) |
| [12-83](../cases/ga12_1984__case4.md) | Session of Faith Presbyterian Church, Akron, Ohio, et al. v. Presbytery of Ascension | Partially sustained | A Session and other complainants challenged Presbytery’s May 1984 resolution as continued noncompliance with directions from an earlier ordination case. The continued-noncompliance specification was sustained, but allegations of constitutional violation and contumacy were not sustained. Remedial recommendations were adopted, although the supplied excerpt provides neither their terms nor the deciding body’s reasons. The mixed ruling therefore distinguishes noncompliance from the complainants’ more serious characterizations of Presbytery’s conduct. | [full text](../cases/ga12_1984__case4.md) |
| [12-83](../cases/ga12_1984__case5.md) | Carl W. Bogue, et al. v. Presbytery of Ascension | Partially sustained | Ascension Presbytery was challenged for resolving not to comply with prior General Assembly directions concerning an ordination examination. The case was sustained as to continued noncompliance and constitutional violation, but not as to contumacy; remedial recommendations were adopted. The supplied text contains the complaint rather than the operative judgment, so it does not disclose the adjudicative reasoning or the specific remedial measures. | [full text](../cases/ga12_1984__case5.md) |
| [12-83](../cases/ga12_1984__case6.md) | Robert C. Peterson, et al. v. Presbytery of Ascension | Sustained | Complaint challenged Ascension Presbytery's March 10, 1984 refusal to obey the Eleventh General Assembly's directive to correct constitutional defects in Kurt Lutjens's ordination trial, and its purported BCO 41 reference of the matter to the Twelfth General Assembly for retrial. The CJB sustained specifications of continued non-compliance and constitutional violation and adopted recommendations for remedial action. The complaint's asserted distinction—that BCO 41 cannot return a prior General Assembly decision for retrial—marks the case's practical limit. | [full text](../cases/ga12_1984__case6.md) |

## 13th General Assembly (1985)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [5](../cases/ga13_1985__case1.md) | S. Michael Preg, Jr., et al. v. Missouri Presbytery | Sustained | Can a presbytery require an annual committee visit to every session and congregation absent a request or evident problem? The judicial commission sustained the complaint against Missouri Presbytery 16-0, holding BCO 11-4 limits higher-court jurisdiction to express constitutional provisions and BCO 13-9 contains no clear authorization for such required visits. It instructed presbytery to conform its Standing Rules and visitation procedures to the BCO, while encouraging voluntary pastoral oversight; several commissioners recorded negative votes without stated reasons. | [full text](../cases/ga13_1985__case1.md) |
| [1985-04](../cases/ga13_1985__1985-04.md) | Mayfield v. Siouxlands Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The Assembly found Jack Mayfield's appeal not in order under BCO 42-4 because he filed it nearly a year and a half after the matter had been adjudicated, and his reasons did not justify disregarding the filing requirement. The ruling did not reach the underlying merits and directed him instead to BCO 37-4's procedure for removing censure. | [full text](../cases/ga13_1985__1985-04.md) |

## 14th General Assembly (1986)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1](../cases/ga14_1986__case1.md) | Kenneth L. Gentry, Jr. et al. v. Calvary Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled; Remanded | Ministers challenged Calvary Presbytery's approval of William Hyer's examination and reception despite his belief that noncanonical, nonbinding revelation could continue. The Assembly sustained the complaint because PCA standards foreclose continuing revelation beyond illumination and providence. It annulled only the examination portion concerning revelation, suspending his reception, and instructed Presbytery to counsel and re-examine him, then report to the next Assembly. The majority deemed the record sufficient despite a dissent emphasizing Hyer's affirmation of Scripture's finality and the absence of an examination transcript. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case1.md) |
| [2](../cases/ga14_1986__case2.md) | Vaughn E. Hathaway, Jr. et al. v. Grace Presbytery | Not sustained | A complaint challenged Grace Presbytery's approval and installation of a candidate who had been divorced for his own infidelity and had remarried; the complainants argued Scripture, the Westminster Confession, BCO 16-3, and the Seventh General Assembly's divorce report barred ordination. The Judicial Commission did not sustain any of the four specifications and upheld Presbytery's action. Its adopted reason was that the PCA's constitutional statements do not explicitly address divorce and remarriage for church officers; noting wide interpretive disagreement, it called for study. The ordination and installation remained undisturbed. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case2.md) |
| [3](../cases/ga14_1986__case3.md) | Henry D. Knight, Jr. v. Palmetto Presbytery | Partially sustained; Remanded | A ruling elder challenged Palmetto Presbytery’s publication, as an appendix to meeting minutes, of a letter never discussed at that meeting, and its handling of his complaint. The Judicial Commission sustained specifications 1 and 3: Presbytery had not clearly declared the publication erroneous, and it risked harming Covenant’s Session. Specifications 2 and 4 failed because Presbytery timely referred the complaint, though its record was inaccurate, and acted in good faith. Despite Presbytery’s later deletion of the letter, the Commission remanded for a new hearing on specifications 1 and 3. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case3.md) |
| [4](../cases/ga14_1986__case4.md) | Edward A. Steele III v. Great Lakes Presbytery | Abandoned | An appeal challenged Great Lakes Presbytery's decision to divest TE Edward A. Steele III without censure and without judicial process, which he alleged followed an accusation that he was not engaged in his official duties. Steele alleged constitutional and discriminatory error and sought investigation, rescission, or erasure of his name. Because no one appeared to represent the appeal, the Committee on Judicial Business judged it abandoned, leaving the merits and requested relief undecided. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case4.md) |
| [5](../cases/ga14_1986__case5.md) | D. Keith Abee et al. v. James River Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed; Remanded | Five appellants challenged censures imposed after a Presbytery commission treated a Session's request for assistance as authority to initiate judicial process. The Assembly sustained all six specifications, holding that the commission lacked authority to proceed before charges were approved and that original jurisdiction remained with the Session. It reversed the censures, ordered their public removal, and remanded for the Session to address any properly specified charges. The ruling expressly did not determine the appellants' guilt or innocence. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case5.md) |
| [6](../cases/ga14_1986__case6.md) | Carl W. Bogue et al. v. Presbytery of the Ascension | Sustained | Presbytery’s response claiming that Lutjens had changed his sacramental views was challenged as factually false and as a retroactive rewriting of minutes Presbytery had previously refused to interpret. All five specifications were sustained, including illegitimate interpretation of BCO 58-4. Because the earlier examination transcript was unavailable through mechanical failure, Presbytery was ordered to reexamine Lutjens, provide a complete transcript, and report by November 10, 1986. Discipline remained conditional on continued nonconformity after pastoral counsel. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case6.md) |
| [8](../cases/ga14_1986__case7.md) | Dye et al. v. Missouri Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled; Remanded |  | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case7.md) |
| [1986-07](../cases/ga14_1986__case7_shell.md) | William A. Shell v. Great Lakes Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The Committee on Judicial Business found Case 7, the six complaints of RE Dye et al. against the Presbytery of Missouri, to be in order and recommended that a single commission be appointed to adjudicate all six complaints. | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case7_shell.md) |
| [1986-08](../cases/ga14_1986__case7.md) | Dye et al. v. Missouri Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled; Remanded |  | [full text](../cases/ga14_1986__case7.md) |

## 15th General Assembly (1987)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [15-73](../cases/ga15_1987__case1.md) | Erwin Morrison, TE Donald Stone, TE David Brewer, TE Steve Christian, RE John Harley, RE David Lachman, RE George Harris v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Sustained | Philadelphia Presbytery ordained Lester Porter as a Ruling Elder of Presbytery to serve a cross-cultural mission church with no particular-church members. The Assembly, adopting the Judicial Commission's adjudication, sustained all three specifications and upheld the complaint: the ordination lacked BCO compliance and the Presbytery mishandled the complaint. It refused to annul Porter's ordination—reasoning Presbytery may ordain Ruling Elders in organizing churches—but directed Presbytery to find a BCO-conforming temporary government for Church Without Walls, stop ordaining Ruling Elders not duly elected by a particular church, and bring Porter's ordination into conformity. | [full text](../cases/ga15_1987__case1.md) |

## 16th General Assembly (1988)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2](../cases/ga16_1988__case1.md) | Carl Fox v. Mississippi Valley Presbytery et al | Partially sustained; Remanded | Fox challenged a Session’s acceptance of his resignation while censured, refusal to restore him, denial of relevant records, and allegedly uneven discipline in his marital dispute. The Assembly adopted the commission’s judgment sustaining the first, third, and fourth complaints: he remained a member, so his removal and nonrestoration were improper and he was entitled to relevant records. Those matters were remanded for rectification. The uneven-discipline claim was neither sustained nor denied because the evidence was insufficient; it too was remanded for investigation and necessary action. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case1.md) |
| [3](../cases/ga16_1988__case2.md) | David C. Lachman et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled | Complainant elders challenged Philadelphia Presbytery's tie-vote refusal to sustain its judicial commission's judgment that the Calvary Session wrongly treated physical cruelty as biblical grounds for divorce. The General Assembly adopted the commission's judgment sustaining all three specifications: the Session's action contravened Matthew 5 and 19, WCF 24:6, and the church's disciplinary role. It annulled Presbytery's refusal and instructed Presbytery to advise the Session that physical cruelty is not biblical or confessional grounds for divorce. The explanatory opinion urged pastoral alternatives, such as separation and rehabilitation, before any divorce determination. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case2.md) |
| [5](../cases/ga16_1988__case3.md) | Robert G. Rayburn et al. v. Missouri Presbytery | Not sustained; Affirmed | Missouri Presbytery instructed its Candidates and Credentials Committee to examine candidates without treating a qualified minority view on prophecy and tongues as disqualifying. Complainants argued this conflicted with the 1986 Gentry decision. The Judicial Commission, adopted by the 16th General Assembly, did not sustain the complaint and confirmed the lower court 7-4. Although earlier similar cases had annulled lower-court actions, it cited the absence of a specific candidate, safeguards, inconclusive debate, and a preference for grass-roots resolution, encouraging Overture 25. The dissent called the view fundamentally out of accord. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case3.md) |
| [7](../cases/ga16_1988__case4.md) | Robert Jamison v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Not sustained; Affirmed | A ruling elder complained that Western Carolina Presbytery wrongly left a confessing bisexual Teaching Elder under indefinite suspension rather than deposing him, excluding him from the sacraments, and cutting him off from fellowship. The General Assembly's adjudicating commission did not sustain the specification of error and confirmed Presbytery's action, holding it complied with BCO 30-3, 4 and 34-7. Review was limited to the constitutionality of the disciplinary process; the commission cited Presbytery's restorative purpose and evidence of repentance. The Assembly was advised Presbytery later deposed the Teaching Elder. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case4.md) |
| [8](../cases/ga16_1988__case5.md) | John Neville v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Withdrawn | The complaint was withdrawn after the matter was resolved at a called meeting of Western Carolina Presbytery on June 6, 1988. The published adjudication records no decision on the complaint's merits. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case5.md) |
| [10](../cases/ga16_1988__case6.md) | James Serio et al. v. Palmetto Presbytery | Sustained | The 16th General Assembly approved the adjudicating Commission’s judgment sustaining a complaint against Palmetto Presbytery. The Commission found that Presbytery erred by sustaining John Tomlinson’s theology examination and licensing him despite his belief in continuing noncanonical revelation through prophecy, tongues, dreams, visions, and direct speech, contrary to WCF I.1 and I.6 and BCO 7-1. It directed Presbytery to annul the theology examination, suspend its licensing action, counsel and re-examine Tomlinson, exercise greater care, and report to the next Assembly. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case6.md) |
| [1988-11](../cases/ga16_1988__case11.md) | Korean Church of Washington Matter | In order | Korean Church of Washington complaint. Cross-referenced to 16-12, p. 114 and 16-57, p. 133, and Appendix V, p. 539, with no further detail recorded here. | [full text](../cases/ga16_1988__case11.md) |

## 17th General Assembly (1989)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1 (from 16th GA)](../cases/ga17_1989__case1.md) | McCready et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Denied; Affirmed | Complaint by Douglas McCready et al. against Philadelphia Presbytery was found in order and referred to a commission for adjudication. No full case record or opinion appears in this window. | [full text](../cases/ga17_1989__case1.md) |
| [4 (from 16th GA)](../cases/ga17_1989__case2.md) | TE Erwin Morrison v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Partially sustained; Annulled | Philadelphia Presbytery ordered Christ Church, Oreland’s Session to transfer three ruling elders while their judicial cases were on appeal. The General Assembly adopted its special Judicial Commission’s judgment rejecting the improper-debate specification for insufficient documentation but sustaining the constitutional challenge: BCO 12-5 and the separation of church-court powers reserve membership control to the Session, which correctly withheld transfer during pending judicial process. The judgment annulled Presbytery’s instruction and imposed three-year constitutional-inquiry safeguards, plus Digest purchase and officer review. | [full text](../cases/ga17_1989__case2.md) |
| [6 (from 16th GA)](../cases/ga17_1989__case3.md) | TE John T. DeBardeleben v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled; Guilty; Not guilty | A complaint challenged Philadelphia Presbytery’s approval of proceedings that cleared teaching and ruling elders without properly trying serious public accusations arising from Christ Church’s division. All three specifications were sustained because plea bargaining, dropped charges, and failure to hear necessary witnesses left the accusations unresolved. Presbytery’s action was annulled and de novo trials produced mixed convictions and acquittals. Harold Burkhart received six months’ suspension, Douglas McCready an admonition, and six former ruling elders three-month suspensions. The decision stressed taking such conflicts to Presbytery or judicial process rather than the congregation. | [full text](../cases/ga17_1989__case3.md) |
| [9 (from 16th GA)](../cases/ga17_1989__case4.md) | Morrison et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Partially sustained; Annulled | Philadelphia Presbytery treated a letter as a complaint although it had not first been filed with the Session, judged the Session without proper process, and asked its clerk to facilitate membership transfers despite the Session's authority over dismissal letters. Those and a constitutional-limits specification were sustained; claims that Presbytery neglected underlying questions and acted for the Session were not. The March 12 actions were annulled, specified language expunged, and Presbytery directed to adopt safeguards for constitutional inquiries and review relevant church-law materials. | [full text](../cases/ga17_1989__case4.md) |
| [case](../cases/ga17_1989__case5.md) | D. Stine v. Southern Florida Presbytery | Partially sustained; Denied; Affirmed | The CJB found the complaint by member D. Stine against Southern Florida Presbytery to be in order and recommended that a commission be appointed to adjudicate the case. No further details of the substance of the complaint are provided in this report. | [full text](../cases/ga17_1989__case5.md) |
| [case](../cases/ga17_1989__case6.md) | Session of Grace Presbyterian Church v. No substantive details of the complaint are provided. | [full text](../cases/ga17_1989__case6.md) |

## 18th General Assembly (1990)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1](../cases/ga18_1990__case1.md) | First Presbyterian Church of Rowlett, Texas v. North Texas Presbytery | Not sustained; Denied; Affirmed | A church challenged North Texas Presbytery’s approval of Rev. C. Don Darling, who disclosed that he could not conscientiously accept or conduct remarriage after divorce. The Standing Judicial Commission did not sustain and denied the complaint, affirming Presbytery. Ministerial acceptance ordinarily rests within a presbytery’s jurisdiction, and Darling’s exception neither concerned a fundamental of the doctrinal system nor struck at the vitals of religion or threatened much injury. Overriding Presbytery would therefore improperly impair liberty of conscience. | [full text](../cases/ga18_1990__case1.md) |
| [2](../cases/ga18_1990__case2.md) | First Presbyterian Church of Rowlett, Texas v. North Texas Presbytery | Abandoned; Affirmed | First Presbyterian Church of Rowlett appealed after North Texas Presbytery reversed the Session's indefinite suspension of two members who had begun attending other churches. The SJC did not reach the asserted procedural errors because the Session advised that it would start over and would not pursue the appeal. Under BCO 43-7, the appeal was abandoned and Presbytery's judgment was affirmed. | [full text](../cases/ga18_1990__case2.md) |
| [1990-01](../cases/ga18_1990__case1.md) | First Presbyterian Church of Rowlett, Texas v. North Texas Presbytery | Not sustained; Denied; Affirmed | A church challenged North Texas Presbytery’s approval of Rev. C. Don Darling, who disclosed that he could not conscientiously accept or conduct remarriage after divorce. The Standing Judicial Commission did not sustain and denied the complaint, affirming Presbytery. Ministerial acceptance ordinarily rests within a presbytery’s jurisdiction, and Darling’s exception neither concerned a fundamental of the doctrinal system nor struck at the vitals of religion or threatened much injury. Overriding Presbytery would therefore improperly impair liberty of conscience. | [full text](../cases/ga18_1990__case1.md) |
| [1990-02](../cases/ga18_1990__case2.md) | First Presbyterian Church of Rowlett, Texas v. North Texas Presbytery | Abandoned; Affirmed | First Presbyterian Church of Rowlett appealed after North Texas Presbytery reversed the Session's indefinite suspension of two members who had begun attending other churches. The SJC did not reach the asserted procedural errors because the Session advised that it would start over and would not pursue the appeal. Under BCO 43-7, the appeal was abandoned and Presbytery's judgment was affirmed. | [full text](../cases/ga18_1990__case2.md) |

## 19th General Assembly (1991)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1990-03](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-03.md) | Grace Covenant Presbyterian Church v. New River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | Grace Covenant Presbyterian Church complained against New River Presbytery's action sustaining a complaint that a Sunday school teacher holding theistic evolution and other doctrinal exceptions should not teach. The SJC partially sustained the complaint, declining to refer the matter back to the church with instructions but advising that the teacher should not teach while he holds exceptions that could require him to teach contrary to the fundamentals of the system of doctrine. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-03.md) |
| [1990-04](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-04.md) | Chappell v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Sustained | Ruling Elder Chappell complained against Eastern Carolina Presbytery's Commission, which had suspended the session of Fuller Memorial Presbyterian Church and constituted itself as a 'Session Pro Tem' without due process or congregational consent. The SJC sustained the complaint, holding that the BCO grants no authority for a presbytery to depose a session and act in its place, that suspension requires due process, and that the congregation has an inalienable right to be governed by officers it has chosen. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-04.md) |
| [1990-04a](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-04a.md) | Marshall v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Sustained | Teaching Elder Wallace Marshall complained against the same Eastern Carolina Presbytery actions at Fuller Memorial Presbyterian Church that were the subject of Case 90-4, filed separately as a Teaching Elder complainant. The SJC sustained this complaint on essentially the same grounds as Case 90-4, finding that the presbytery lacked authority to suspend the session without due process and to constitute itself as a 'Session Pro Tem.' | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-04a.md) |
| [1990-05](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-05.md) | Melton et al. v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery | Denied | Complainants challenged the ruling of the session of Westminster Presbyterian Church (Chattanooga) disallowing proxy votes at a December 10, 1989 congregational meeting that voted not to rescind the earlier dissolution and property transfer to New City Fellowship. The SJC denied all specifications, holding that proxy voting was not required under BCO 24-4 or Robert's Rules, and that the congregation's decision to dissolve was within its rights under BCO Chapter 25. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-05.md) |
| [1990-07](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-07.md) | Stringer v. Mississippi Valley Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | Randy Stringer appealed his excommunication by Mississippi Valley Presbytery after being deposed and then excommunicated for failure to respond to the presbytery. The SJC set aside the excommunication and remanded the case to MVP, holding that after deposition the presbytery was required under BCO 46-8 to assign Stringer to membership in a particular church rather than proceed to excommunication. Dr. Morton H. Smith dissented, arguing that the presbytery had proper grounds to proceed to excommunication after Stringer's contumacy. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-07.md) |
| [1990-08](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-08.md) | Bowen v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Sustained | Bowen challenged Presbytery’s attempt, two years after installation, to rescind its earlier ruling and annul local officers’ elections and ordinations without trial. The SJC sustained the complaint: ordination is perpetual, deposition requires judicial process, and rescission was out of order after implementation; Presbytery likewise could not suspend a local elder without due process. But it affirmed that infant baptism and limited atonement are fundamentals admitting no officer exception. The officers remained in office but doctrinally out of accord; voluntary demission or proper process was advised. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-08.md) |
| [1990-09](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-09.md) | Hathaway v. Grace Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | TE Vaughn Hathaway complained that the Session of Waynesboro Presbyterian Church failed to institute discipline against seven members who had not attended worship for over a year. The judicial panel denied the complaint on the grounds that the Session retained discretion to determine the timing of discipline proceedings, and procedural errors did not result in material prejudice. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-09.md) |
| [1990-10](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-10.md) | Hathaway v. Grace Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Moot | A pastor complained that Presbytery upheld his Session’s refusal to give him financial reports prepared for Session members, while also alleging record-transmission, circularization, factual, and constitutional errors. The SJC denied Specifications 1–5 but sustained Specification 6: because the pastor was a Session member, the Session’s authorization made the reports available to him regardless of whether other elders collected copies. His pastoral relationship had ended, however, so the access claim was moot and conferred no present right to the reports. Reasons for Specifications 1–2 were adopted from Case 90-9. | [full text](../cases/ga19_1991__1990-10.md) |
| 1983-11 | Morrison v. Philadelphia Presbytery | withdrawn |  | _reference (decided earlier)_ · [ga19_1991 p.62](../markdown/ga19_1991.md#ga19-p62) |

## 20th General Assembly (1992)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1991-01](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-01.md) | Sandra Lovelace v. Northeast Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Judicially out of order | The SJC found two attempted complaints administratively and judicially out of order. The October 1990 filing did not satisfy BCO 43-2 because the earlier letters it invoked had merely asked questions or communicated concern, rather than first presenting a complaint to Presbytery, and concerned an oral report not made part of Presbytery's record. The January 1991 filing supplied no supporting reasons as BCO 43-3 required and challenged specifications of error that were not themselves acts or decisions of Presbytery. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-01.md) |
| [1991-02](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-02.md) | Nickoley and Stephens v. Southwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | Complainants challenged Southwest Presbytery's January 1991 actions: withdrawing charges against two ruling elders, dissolving Pastor Pollock's pastoral relationship, and rescinding its suspension of Covenant PC's session. The SJC did not sustain the complaint and affirmed. It held the BCO gives no presbytery authority to suspend a session or act as a Session pro tem without formal congregational consent; BCO 31-1 left the elder charges with the session, so they were never validly before Presbytery; the dissolution challenge was moot after Pollock left the PCA, though Presbytery acted correctly on the congregation's request. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-02.md) |
| [1991-03](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-03.md) | Daniel R. Morse v. Covenant Presbytery | Abandoned; Moot | On Covenant Presbytery’s mixed BCO 41 reference, the SJC held that ministers Morse and Crenshaw could not resign unilaterally; joining another evangelical church invoked BCO 38-3, requiring their names erased, with notice that they were in good standing and faced no pending charges. Grace Church’s withdrawal required a congregational vote, not notice to Presbytery, so its name also was to be erased. Grace had abandoned its complaints, rendering them moot; the SJC nevertheless opined that recording a protest is insulated from complaint by BCO 45-5. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-03.md) |
| [1991-04](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-04.md) | Robert D. Hopper v. James River Presbytery | Denied | The SJC denied Hopper's complaint against James River Presbytery's April 13, 1991 order barring him from teaching or preaching his views on prophecy and tongues. It rejected all five specifications: Scripture did not forbid the order; the 1974 Pastoral Letter did not address presbytery's power to enjoin such teaching; forbearance did not preclude protecting peace and purity; presbytery could restrict preaching short of a heresy finding without binding conscience; and allowing presbyteral discussion while restricting congregational teaching was not inconsistent. The action remained in force. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-04.md) |
| [1991-05](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-05.md) | Herman Gunter III et al. v. Central Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Two ruling-elder nominees challenged the Session’s refusal to approve them based on their theonomic views and perceived divisiveness. The full SJC denied the complaint, holding that the court of original jurisdiction may assess an unsettled theological position and whether it threatens the church’s peace, purity, or progress; divisiveness was therefore properly considered. The ruling did not decide the merits of theonomy itself, and it left undisturbed Presbytery’s annulment of the Session’s categorical statement that theonomy violated WCF 19.4. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-05.md) |
| [1991-07](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-07.md) | Complainants v. Potomac Presbytery | Not sustained; Dismissed | Complainants challenged Potomac Presbytery's 11/12/91 adjudication denying their complaint over the Timonium Presbyterian Church pastoral dispute, arguing they received too little time, deliberation, and explanation. The SJC held the complaint not sustained: a higher court may not overturn a competent lower court merely because complainants are dissatisfied with the amount of input or time spent, and no constitutional defect was demonstrated. Two earlier complaints were dismissed as not timely filed. The timely 12/11/91 complaint was heard 3/6/92. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1991-07.md) |
| [1992-03](../cases/ga20_1992__1992-03.md) | Richard E. Olson, et al. v. Heritage Presbytery | Administratively out of order | Heritage Presbytery adopted a committee report answering a session's reference on whether a session may require officers to abstain from beverage alcohol. Olson and others complained. The SJC held the complaint administratively out of order because it was not timely filed with the General Assembly, and independently ruled that a court's response to a non-judicial reference seeking advice under BCO 41-3 is not complainable. Complainants may submit their questions to the Committee on Constitutional Business. Confirmed 23-0. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1992-03.md) |
| [1992-04](../cases/ga20_1992__1992-04.md) | William A. Conrad, et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order; Remanded | Complainants alleged Central Carolina Presbytery failed to act when it remanded their complaints against the Prosperity Presbyterian Church Session to the commission acting as Interim Session. The SJC held Presbytery did not fail to act: that Interim Session was the court of original jurisdiction and should adjudicate the complaints timely. The SJC judged the complaint administratively out of order and remanded it to the Interim Session, reminding complainants of their right to appeal to a higher court. | [full text](../cases/ga20_1992__1992-04.md) |

| 1992-06 | Conrad et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery. (Case #1) | administrative | Central Carolina Presbytery remanded complaints to an Interim Session Commission rather than adjudicating them directly. The SJC confirmed this action, finding that the Commission appointed as Interim Session was the proper court of original jurisdiction and remanding the complaints for adjudication by that Commission. | _decided at 21st GA_ · [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-06.md) |

## 21st General Assembly (1993)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1991-06](../cases/ga21_1993__1991-06.md) | Lovelace v. Northeast Presbytery (Case #2)  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | A member challenged Presbytery’s refusal, after investigation, to try four elders on favoritism charges and its ruling that her related complaint was out of order because dissolution of her church ended her PCA membership. The SJC denied the first item because BCO 31-2 permits declining process when investigation raises no strong presumption of guilt. It sustained the second: jurisdiction properly invoked while a communicant member is not lost through a later status change. No remand was expressly ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1991-06.md) |
| [1992-01](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-01.md) | Smith v. Northeast Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | Smith challenged Northeast Presbytery’s ruling that his complaint was untimely and unconstitutional after it dissolved Grace Church and indefinitely postponed a proposed Braintree mission. The SJC sustained the complaint and remanded it for Presbytery adjudication. The May action first revealed Presbytery’s view that former members were outside the PCA, so the June complaint was timely. BCO 11-4 neither barred nor itself required care; responsibility arose here from the April referral to Presbytery’s missions committee. Care need not take the form of a mission, and mere dissolution alone would not create that responsibility. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-01.md) |
| [1992-02](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-02.md) | Roberts v. New River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | A congregation member complained that New River Presbytery erred in November 1991 by declining to accept TE Don Post Jr.'s resignation and dissolve his pastoral relation, instead appointing a commission. The SJC sustained the complaint. Under BCO 23-1, once the congregation concurred and no sufficient reasons to retain the pastor appeared, the resignation 'shall be accepted' and the relation dissolved; the Presbytery's general BCO 13-9 power did not override that specific provision. The Presbytery had already dissolved the relation on December 7, 1991; the adopted judgment ordered no remand. Three commissioners dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-02.md) |
| [1992-05](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-05.md) | Parks et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Complainants challenged a Session plan under which most elders voluntarily resigned, the Session granted severance and benefits to its former pastor, proposed about a year with an interim pastor before a permanent call, and contemplated revising local bylaws. The SJC denied all four items. It held that the BCO permitted jointly considered voluntary resignations, that severance concerned resignation and the budget rather than a pastoral call, and that the plan and bylaw proposal were not themselves unconstitutional. Any later call still had to follow the BCO without undue delay, and local bylaws had to remain consistent with it. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-05.md) |
| [1992-06](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-06.md) | Conrad et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery. (Case #1)  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained; Denied | After a congregation voted to ask that all ruling elders' official relationships be dissolved without censure, the Session concurred and requested an interim Session. The SJC denied the elders' complaint, holding that BCO 24-6b extends by good and necessary deduction from one elder to multiple identified elders. Dissolution ended their service on that Session but did not divest them of office. Because both the congregation and Session requested it, BCO 13-9 and 15-2 authorized Presbytery to appoint an interim Session. A separate constitutional inquiry was denied without explanation. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-06.md) |
| [1992-07](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-07.md) | Conrad et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery (Case #2)  ·  *dissent* | Denied | A complaint arising from Central Carolina Presbytery’s refusal to prosecute TE Stephen O. Stout and to recognize his accusers as voluntary prosecutors was denied. The SJC framed two issues and answered both yes: the Presbytery could constitutionally adopt its Investigative Committee report and decline judicial process, because the Committee found no “strong presumption of guilt” under BCO 31-2; and it could refuse voluntary-prosecutor status, because BCO 31-1/31-3 left discipline to the Presbytery and made any voluntary prosecutor subject to its approval. The complaint was denied on both issues. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-07.md) |
| [1992-08](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-08.md) | Reference from Central Carolina Presbytery | Denied; Referred | Central Carolina Presbytery asked the SJC to adjudicate two complaints and a related reference concerning charges against church members. Although initially found administratively in order, the reference was declined because adjudicating only the formally referred matters could render at least two additional, related complaints moot without due consideration. All matters in Case 92-8 were returned to Presbytery; no underlying merits were decided. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-08.md) |
| [1992-09b](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-09b.md) | Overman v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated); Dismissed | Overman carried to the General Assembly his complaint that Eastern Carolina Presbytery improperly licensed Arthur Peterson, after the presbytery denied it. The SJC found the complaint not in order for lack of standing: Overman was not subject to the presbytery's jurisdiction (BCO 43-1; MSJC 6.2). It made no merits ruling; the same basic issue was noted as pending in a complaint by the Antioch Presbyterian Church session. The source header labels the disposition 'dismissed.' | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-09b.md) |
| [1992-10](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-10.md) | Mr. and Mrs. Matthew Bernard v. Ascension Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | The Bernards appealed directly to the General Assembly from their excommunication by a Presbytery commission acting as the congregation’s Session. The SJC ruled the appeal administratively out of order because an appeal must first go to the next higher court—Ascension Presbytery—not to the court of original jurisdiction. It did not decide whether the excommunication was proper; that issue remained for Presbytery to hear. | [full text](../cases/ga21_1993__1992-10.md) |

## 22nd General Assembly (1994)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1992-09a](../cases/ga22_1994__1992-09a.md) | Antioch Session v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | Antioch Session challenged Peterson’s licensure based on his prior divorce, creation and local-flood views, reversal of a committee recommendation, and close vote; it also challenged his regular preaching before licensure. The SJC rejected the licensure specifications because Presbytery had thoroughly examined repentance and doctrinal adherence, and no definitive PCA standard required exclusion on these facts. It sustained the pre-licensure supply issue under BCO 19-1, 22-5, and 22-6 and enjoined greater future circumspection. The licensure remained undisturbed; the ruling was expressly case-specific. | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1992-09a.md) |
| [1993-01](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-01.md) | Smith v. Northeast Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Smith complained that Northeast Presbytery failed to provide pastoral care after dissolving Grace Presbyterian Church. The SJC rejected Presbytery's objections that the complaint lacked supporting reasons or was barred by Smith's protest, held it in order, but denied it on the merits. Distinguishing the pastoral 'power of order' from judicial 'power of jurisdiction,' the SJC ruled that the Constitution neither requires nor permits a presbytery to retain jurisdiction over individual members of a dissolved particular church, and that pastoral care of former members is generally discretionary; on these facts, Presbytery's efforts satisfied any duty. | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-01.md) |
| [1993-02](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-02.md) | Johnson v. Evangel Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | An appeal challenged Evangel Presbytery's judgment that a teaching elder wrongfully pursued divorce without biblical grounds, alleging procedural irregularities, refused evidence, haste, prejudice, and mistaken censure. The SJC rejected each claim: failure to swear two minor witnesses was acknowledged and nonprejudicial, notices and defense opportunity were adequate, and the guilty finding rested on Scripture, WCF 24:6, BCO 26-1, and 30-3; the 1992 study did not authorize divorce for alleged spiritual desertion. The appeal was not sustained, leaving the indefinite suspension in force; one commissioner dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-02.md) |
| [1993-03](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-03.md) | Chen v. Ascension Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Reversed; Other |  | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-03.md) |
| [1993-04](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-04.md) | George L. Cox v. Mid-America Presbytery | Denied |  | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-04.md) |
| [1993-05](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-05.md) | Clark v. Southwest Presbytery (Request for Clarification)  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | John P. Clark, Jr. requested clarification of the SJC decision in Case 93-5 regarding who controls the assets of South Valley Presbyterian Church, Inc. after the congregation voted to withdraw from the PCA. The SJC held it lacked jurisdiction over the civil corporate entity and its property, declining to rule on Clark's request. | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-05.md) |
| [1993-06](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-06.md) | Robert J. Borger v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied |  | [full text](../cases/ga22_1994__1993-06.md) |

## 23rd General Assembly (1995)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1993-10](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-10.md) | Clark v. Southwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Remanded | TE John P. Clark, Jr. appealed his conviction for contumacy and suspension/deposition by Southwest Presbytery's Judicial Commission. The SJC found the appeal in order (17-4) and remanded the case for retrial (21-0) on grounds that the Judicial Commission committed significant procedural errors, including abandoning original charges and convicting Clark of contumacy on new charges without giving him opportunity to respond. | [full text](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-10.md) |
| [1993-10b](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-10b.md) | Clark v. Southwest Presbytery | Moot; Other | Case 93-10B arose from Southwest Presbytery's action at its June 14, 1994 meeting declaring TE Clark's suspension 'still binding' notwithstanding his appeal. The SJC held the issue moot in light of its judgment in Case 93-10 remanding the entire matter to the Presbytery for retrial. | [full text](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-10b.md) |
| [1993-11](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-11.md) | McDade v. Susquehanna Valley Presbytery | Denied | Paul McDade complained that Susquehanna Valley Presbytery erred in investigating rather than immediately trying charges against session members and pastors for allegedly upholding a false doctrine of repentance and forgiveness. The SJC denied all five specifications, finding the Presbytery followed proper procedure and that no doctrinal error or slander was established. | [full text](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-11.md) |
| [1993-12](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-12_1993-14.md) | Newton v. Heartland Presbytery | Affirmed; Reversed; Other | TE Steve Newton appealed his conviction for contumacy and indefinite suspension from office by Heartland Presbytery's judicial commission for persistent failure to submit statistical reports and refusal to cooperate with Presbytery committees. The SJC affirmed Heartland Presbytery's judgment in all respects, with a concurring opinion finding three of four specifications invalid but deferring to the lower court's factfinding on the remaining specification. | [full text](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-12_1993-14.md) |
| [1993-14](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-12_1993-14.md) | Grace RPC Session v. Heartland Presbytery | Affirmed; Reversed; Other | The Session of Grace Reformed Presbyterian Church appealed its conviction for contumacy and termination of its relationship with Heartland Presbytery. The SJC reversed the conviction, holding that a presbytery lacks original jurisdiction to indict and try a session (lower court) for an offense such as contumacy, as BCO provisions reserve original jurisdiction over ruling elders to the Session level. | [full text](../cases/ga23_1995__1993-12_1993-14.md) |

| [1995-10](../cases/ga25_1997__1995-10.md) | Stuart Merriam v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery (Appeal) | Remanded | After Tennessee Valley Presbytery deposed a minister without conducting a church trial, he appealed, alleging denial of PCA due process. The SJC remanded for a new trial. It held that Presbytery could bring charges but could not substitute the Papua New Guinea criminal proceedings for ecclesiastical process, especially when it neither served an indictment and charges nor appointed counsel for an accused unable to attend. The remand permitted a constitutionally compliant trial; it did not resolve guilt or bar discipline. | [full text](../cases/ga25_1997__1995-10.md) |
| [1995-11](../cases/ga25_1997__1995-11.md) | James Landrum v. Mississippi Valley Presbytery | Sustained | Complainants challenged Mississippi Valley Presbytery’s approval of James Blaha’s ordination examination, arguing that his views on tongues and prophecy were constitutional exceptions and precluded approval. The SJC agreed that Presbytery should have acknowledged and noted the exceptions, but upheld the examination. It found no proof that Blaha’s denial that the gifts conveyed revelation crossed prior Assembly limits and deferred to Presbytery on an unsettled issue. It expressly declined reexamination or remand; a substantial partial dissent would have set the examination aside. | [full text](../cases/ga25_1997__1995-11.md) |
