## 26th General Assembly (1998)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1997-01](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-01.md) | Felton v. Grace Presbytery | Withdrawn | A complaint docketed as Case 97-1, Felton v. Grace Presbytery, was withdrawn by the complainant in the 26th Assembly records. No merits opinion was published, and the available notice does not identify the challenged presbytery action, the grounds, or any reason for withdrawal. The practical effect was that the matter ended without any merits ruling or relief. It illustrates that a bare withdrawal entry supports only a status synopsis, not an inference about the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-01.md) |
| [1997-02](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-02.md) | Nickoley v. Rocky Mountain | Remanded | Rocky Mountain Presbytery cited Pastor Bruce Nickoley for contumacy during his trial, after he allegedly refused to tell his congregation to stop picketing, and immediately deposed him without a hearing. On appeal, the SJC sustained the appeal, holding the Presbytery misapplied BCO provisions on confession, citation, refusal to testify, and deposition; Nickoley neither confessed nor refused to appear. Although presbyteries have general authority to maintain order, serious charges require due process. The SJC remanded, recommending either a new process beginning with BCO §32-3 or revocation of the deposition and reinstatement. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-02.md) |
| [1997-03](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-03.md) | Nickoley v. Rocky Mountain | Administratively out of order; Remanded | Bruce Nickoley complained against Rocky Mountain Presbytery over its action dissolving the relationship with Evergreen Church. The SJC held the complaint administratively out of order under SJC Manual 10.1. The same matter was already the subject of an appeal granted to Evergreen Church and docketed for Presbytery's January 22–24, 1997 meeting, and BCO 43-1 provides that no complaint is allowable in a judicial case in which an appeal is taken. No merits ruling was reached. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-03.md) |
| [1997-04](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-04.md) | Nancy A. Conrad et al. v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Not sustained | After a Session corrected an earlier unauthorized removal from its rolls, then formally voted to remove the same members, the members challenged Presbytery's denial of their complaint, arguing that the new removal defied the prior decision. The SJC upheld Presbytery's action. It concluded that the record showed no unconstitutional procedure or BCO violation and that it could consider only facts properly in the record, not material from earlier cases offered in oral argument. The ruling left the later membership removal undisturbed. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-04.md) |
| [1997-05](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-05.md) | Mt. Carmel Session v. New Jersey Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained; Denied | Mt. Carmel Session challenged New Jersey Presbytery's creation-days resolution, arguing that the Westminster Standards require six consecutive twenty-four-hour days. The SJC majority concluded that Presbytery could resolve the doctrinal question under BCO 11-4 and that prior Assembly actions permitted latitude on the length of the creation days. The General Assembly rejected the minority report, adopted the majority report, and denied the complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-05.md) |
| [1997-06](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-06.md) | Fitzsimmons v. Evangel Presbytery | Remanded | After a church member and deacon was excommunicated by his session, he appealed to Evangel Presbytery. Presbytery found the appeal in order and appointed a commission, but then rescinded that action and never adjudicated the appeal. The SJC held Presbytery erred by failing to notify the appellant of the appeal hearing and by failing to adjudicate an appeal properly before it. It remanded under BCO 42-9 for proper adjudication and made no ruling on the remaining session-related merits issues. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-06.md) |
| [1997-07](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-07.md) | Steve Farris v. Central Florida Presbytery | Judicially out of order | The Standing Judicial Commission held Steve Farris's complaint against Central Florida Presbytery judicially out of order because Farris was not a member of a PCA church and therefore lacked standing before the Commission. The Commission reached no merits issue and granted no relief; the only stated ground was the complainant's lack of standing. The case illustrates a threshold standing bar for SJC complainants, but the sparse record does not identify the underlying presbytery action complained of. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-07.md) |
| [1997-08](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-08.md) | Bridie v. Central Florida | Judicially out of order | Whether a non-PCA church member may bring a complaint against a presbytery? The full Standing Judicial Commission held no, finding Theresa Bridie's complaint against Central Florida Presbytery judicially out of order because she was not a member of a PCA church and therefore lacked standing. No merits issue was reached, and the sparse record does not identify the challenged presbytery action. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-08.md) |
| [1997-09](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-09.md) | Robert M. Shive v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Reversed; Remanded | A church member appealed convictions and excommunication arising from vulgar statements to two patients, challenging the lack of pretrial reconciliation, recusals, the prosecutor’s participation in deliberations, and Presbytery procedure. The SJC left guilt intact and denied a new trial, though it held that a prosecutor may not deliberate as a judge. It reversed excommunication because the record did not show impenitence or contumacy, which could not be inferred from exercising the right to remain silent. It remanded only to impose indefinite suspension from the Sacraments and conditions pending satisfactory evidence of repentance. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-09.md) |
| [1997-10](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-10.md) | Raymond Larsen v. Pacific Presbytery (Complaint) | Withdrawn | Raymond Larsen's complaint against Pacific Presbytery (Case 97-10) was withdrawn by the complainant before any merits ruling, and the Assembly recorded that withdrawal at its next stated meeting. His companion Case 97-12, also against Pacific Presbytery, was withdrawn at the same time. No separate merits opinion was published, so the record supplies only the disposition and no reasons, facts, or relief. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-10.md) |
| [1997-11](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-11.md) | Raymond Larsen v. Pacific Presbytery (Appeal) | Administratively out of order | Raymond Larsen sought to appeal a Session judgment deposing him for contumacy after he failed to appear for trial. The SJC unanimously found the appeal administratively out of order because BCO 42-2 permits an appeal only by a person who submitted to a regular trial. The notice does not review the merits of the deposition. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-11.md) |
| [1997-12](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-12.md) | Raymond Larsen v. Pacific Presbytery (Complaint) | Withdrawn | The SJC record shows that Raymond Larsen’s complaints against Pacific Presbytery—case 97-12, with 97-10—were withdrawn by the complainants. No separate merits opinion was published, and the source supplies no underlying issue, adopted reasoning, issue-level ruling, or relief. The operative disposition is therefore withdrawal, not a merits determination. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-12.md) |
| [1997-13](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-13.md) | Session of Harvestwood PCA v. New River Presbytery (Complaint) | Sustained | New River Presbytery treated Grace Covenant PCA's BCO 41 inquiry about a Sunday school teacher's acceptability as a request for advice, but then ruled he was eligible to teach. On Harvestwood's complaint, the SJC held that Presbytery lacked original jurisdiction to make that final disposition and that the record was unclear about the teacher's views. The SJC sustained the complaint and recommended that Presbytery rescind its March 15, 1997 action and clarify his views through proper BCO procedures; it declined to reach the creation and theistic-evolution merits. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-13.md) |
| [1997-14](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-14_1997-15.md) | Onda George v. Central Florida Presbytery (Complaint) | Abandoned | The SJC deemed Onda George’s complaint against Central Florida Presbytery abandoned because the complainant died without heirs. The sparse record does not identify the challenged action or contain a merits determination. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-14_1997-15.md) |
| [1997-15](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-14_1997-15.md) | Onda George v. Central Florida Presbytery (Complaint) | Abandoned | The SJC deemed Onda George’s complaint against Central Florida Presbytery abandoned because George died without heirs. The short record does not identify the challenged action or provide a merits ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-14_1997-15.md) |
| [1997-16](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-16.md) | Session, BPC, Concord, NC v. Central Carolina Presbytery (Complaint)  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | The Session of Bible Presbyterian Church, Concord, complained that Central Carolina Presbytery wrongly accepted its moderator's ruling making RE Chris Dollar's interim Candidates Committee appointment "null and void" because Christ Covenant Session never approved it. The SJC answered both issues no and did not sustain the complaint, leaving Presbytery's action undisturbed. It reasoned that approval was conditional under CCP rules, the moderator expressed Presbytery's intention, Presbytery did not surrender jurisdiction, and BCO 39-3 required deference; it found no appearance of prejudice. Vote 11-7, with dissents urging remand and challenging sessional control over committee eligibility. | [full text](../cases/ga26_1998__1997-16.md) |
| [1997-01](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-01.md) | Felton v. Grace Presbytery | Withdrawn | A complaint docketed as Case 97-1, Felton v. Grace Presbytery, was withdrawn by the complainant in the 26th Assembly records. No merits opinion was published, and the available notice does not identify the challenged presbytery action, the grounds, or any reason for withdrawal. The practical effect was that the matter ended without any merits ruling or relief. It illustrates that a bare withdrawal entry supports only a status synopsis, not an inference about the merits. | [disposition](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-01.md) |
| [1997-10](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-10.md) | Raymond Larsen v. Pacific Presbytery (Complaint) | Withdrawn | Raymond Larsen's complaint against Pacific Presbytery (Case 97-10) was withdrawn by the complainant before any merits ruling, and the Assembly recorded that withdrawal at its next stated meeting. His companion Case 97-12, also against Pacific Presbytery, was withdrawn at the same time. No separate merits opinion was published, so the record supplies only the disposition and no reasons, facts, or relief. | [disposition](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-10.md) |
| [1997-12](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-12.md) | Raymond Larsen v. Pacific Presbytery (Complaint) | Withdrawn | The SJC record shows that Raymond Larsen’s complaints against Pacific Presbytery—case 97-12, with 97-10—were withdrawn by the complainants. No separate merits opinion was published, and the source supplies no underlying issue, adopted reasoning, issue-level ruling, or relief. The operative disposition is therefore withdrawal, not a merits determination. | [disposition](../cases/ga26_1998__stub_1997-12.md) |

## 27th General Assembly (1999)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1996-06](../cases/ga27_1999__1996-06.md) | Williams v. South Texas Presbytery | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | The Standing Judicial Commission approved the panel's action dismissing Robert Williams's complaint against South Texas Presbytery as judicially out of order. The adopted reason was that the parties could not agree on the Record of the Case. The source does not describe the challenged Presbytery action, the complaint's specifications, or any merits ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1996-06.md) |
| [1997-17](../cases/ga27_1999__1997-17.md) | Lebo v. Susquehanna Valley Presbytery | Denied | A member challenged Presbytery’s approval of a congregational motion supplementing an assistant-pastor fund, alleging that Session had ceded its budget authority. The SJC denied both issues. The motion addressed a special circumstance, not approval of the budget, and left the salary figure to the elders and deacons; Session had already approved the budget and retained control of church funds. The ruling was confined to the congregation’s particular circumstances and did not approve congregational budget adoption. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1997-17.md) |
| [1998-01](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-01.md) | Snapp v. James River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | The complaint challenged James River Presbytery's approval of an ordination examination in which the candidate called the Genesis creation days God's anthropomorphic work days, not necessarily twenty-four-hour days. The complainant argued that view conflicted with WCF I.9. The SJC denied the complaint and answered 'No' to whether the Presbytery erred; the examination stood. It held the Presbytery had authority and discretion, no General Assembly had declared anthropomorphic days contrary to the Standards, and an advisory-committee study left the SJC unwilling to decide creation-day length. A six-member dissent sought an exception and study-committee referral. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-01.md) |
| [1998-02](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-02.md) | St. Paul Session, et al. v. Central Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | The St. Paul Session and other Central Florida presbyters complained that Presbytery wrongly sustained a commission report denying the Session complaint and declining to find a strong presumption of guilt against Pastor John Hutchinson. The SJC denied the complaint on both issues. It held CFP permissibly directed a BCO 31-2 investigation and chose its own procedures; procedural irregularities and possible bias did not show BCO 39-3 clear error, though the panel criticized CFP’s handling. A lone dissent would remand, alleging the commission ignored the substance of 41 of 45 specifications. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-02.md) |
| [1998-03](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-03.md) | Robert Williams v. South Texas Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | The SJC approved a panel action dismissing Robert Williams's appeal against South Texas Presbytery as administratively out of order. The sparse record does not identify the challenged Presbytery action or explain the administrative defect, so it supports no conclusion about the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-03.md) |
| [1998-04](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-04.md) | Jeffrey Yelton v. Westminster Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC declared Yelton’s complaint against Westminster Presbytery no longer in order after documentation showed that he had joined the Presbytery of the Alleghenies of the Reformed Presbyterian Church of North America. The brief ruling does not identify the conduct challenged in the complaint or decide its merits. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-04.md) |
| [1998-05](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-05.md) | Long, et al. v. James River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | At issue was whether James River Presbytery erred by instructing TE Andrew Conrad not to teach or preach his views that the Genesis days were not necessarily six twenty-four-hour days, after JRP treated the views as an exception. The SJC denied the complaint, leaving JRP's instruction in effect. It deferred under BCO 39-3, finding no significant procedural or constitutional error and no clear error of discretion; the parties had stipulated that instruction as the sole issue. The eight dissenters would have found JRP exceeded its discretion. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-05.md) |
| [1998-06](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-06.md) | Daniel C. Kim v. Korean Southwest Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC did not reach the unidentified action challenged by Daniel C. Kim against Korean Southwest Presbytery. It approved a recommendation that the complaint was no longer in order because Kim and others reportedly withdrew from the PCA to form an independent congregation. Under BCO 43-1, a complainant must be a communing member subject to the respondent court’s jurisdiction; no merits ruling was made. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__1998-06.md) |
| [1998-07](../cases/ga27_1999__stub_1998-07.md) | Yi v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Administratively out of order | A complainant failed to file a complaint after Korean Capital Presbytery dealt with his case at a called meeting. The SJC therefore approved the Panel’s recommendation declaring the matter administratively out of order. The notice provides no ruling on the underlying dispute and states no further relief or proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga27_1999__stub_1998-07.md) |
| [1998-07](../cases/ga27_1999__stub_1998-07.md) | Yi v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Administratively out of order | A complainant failed to file a complaint after Korean Capital Presbytery dealt with his case at a called meeting. The SJC therefore approved the Panel’s recommendation declaring the matter administratively out of order. The notice provides no ruling on the underlying dispute and states no further relief or proceedings. | [disposition](../cases/ga27_1999__stub_1998-07.md) |

## 28th General Assembly (2000)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1998-08](../cases/ga28_2000__1998-08.md) | Smith v. Southwest Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | Smith appealed discipline for divisiveness, gossip and slander, broken membership vows, and contumacy arising largely from complaints and letters during church conflict. The SJC sustained her appeal, reversed guilt on all four charges, and annulled suspension from the sacraments. The documentary record proved none of the allegations; filing complaints was not itself divisiveness, and the trial improperly lacked a witness, shifted proof to Smith, showed prejudice, and unfairly rejected her written defense. The SJC declined remand because the evidence was insufficient. | [full text](../cases/ga28_2000__1998-08.md) |
| [1998-09](../cases/ga28_2000__1998-09.md) | Baer v. Illiana Presbytery | Denied | An appeal from a Presbytery judgment and indefinite suspension of a pastor turned on whether he violated the Ninth Commandment and ordination/installation vows by telling the congregation the Session had 'demanded' his resignation for six weeks. The SJC denied the appeal, holding the Session as a whole had not formally demanded resignation; individual elders' earlier opinions did not excuse the overstatement, which harmed the Session's good name. It also found no due-process irregularity or prejudice warranting reversal, deferring to the lower court under BCO 39-3, leaving indefinite suspension in place. | [full text](../cases/ga28_2000__1998-09.md) |
| [1998-10](../cases/ga28_2000__1998-10.md) | Curtis v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Remanded | Eastern Carolina Presbytery referred to the SJC both TE Byron Curtis's complaint against Presbytery's order barring him from teaching his exceptions and TE Jeff Black's already-sustained complaint on the same subject. Without reaching the merits, the SJC remanded to the Presbytery for adjudication at the next stated meeting, directing that Curtis's complaint be heard. It reasoned that judicial complaints in process cannot be referred for mere advice and that, despite uncertainty about what the Presbytery sought, the lower court should hear the case first. Five SJC members dissented; the merits remained open. | [full text](../cases/ga28_2000__1998-10.md) |
| [1999-01](../cases/ga28_2000__1999-01.md) | Western Carolina v. Tennessee Valley  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | Three presbyteries asked the General Assembly under BCO 34-1 to assume original jurisdiction over a minister alleged to have allowed women to preach from a church pulpit. The SJC held the requests administratively out of order and did not reach the allegation’s merits. Worship was the Session’s responsibility, so the minister could not be held liable for actions assigned to it; moreover, Tennessee Valley Presbytery had acted on the allegations, defeating BCO 34-1’s refusal-to-act prerequisite. No further proceedings were ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga28_2000__1999-01.md) |
| [1999-02](../cases/ga28_2000__1999-02.md) | Tan v. South Texas Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | Tan's complaint challenged South Texas Presbytery's action at its July 30–31, 1999 meeting. The SJC declared the complaint administratively out of order because it was untimely under BCO 43-3: although dated and faxed August 28, it was not mailed until September 4 and was received September 7—beyond 30 days. SJC Manual 19.10 requires receipt in the Stated Clerk's office and disallows fax filing. The merits were not reached. Twenty-two members concurred; one was recused and one absent. | [full text](../cases/ga28_2000__1999-02.md) |
| [1999-04](../cases/ga28_2000__1999-04.md) | Ed Fitzsimmons v. Evangel Presbytery | Administratively out of order | The SJC refused to hear Ed Fitzsimmons's appeal from Evangel Presbytery because his appeal was not filed with the General Assembly's Stated Clerk within the 30-day deadline of BCO 42-2. The appeal was declared administratively out of order, and no merits question was decided. The sparse record shows a straightforward application of the filing deadline; it does not disclose the underlying presbytery action or any merits view. | [full text](../cases/ga28_2000__1999-04.md) |

## 29th General Assembly (2001)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [1999-03](../cases/ga29_2001__stub_1999-03.md) | Gatis v. Northeast Presbytery | Withdrawn | A minister appealed an unidentified Presbytery judgment, then requested withdrawal. The full SJC approved the request, ending Case 99-3 without a merits decision. The source also records that Adams’s related complaint was considered out of order under BCO 43-1, but that complaint was distinct from the withdrawn appeal. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__stub_1999-03.md) |
| [1999-05](../cases/ga29_2001__1999-05.md) | Rountree v. Covenant | Partially sustained; Denied | After a minister remained on his presbytery's rolls without call and outside its bounds for more than seven years, the presbytery completed involuntary divestiture without censure. He appealed. The SJC denied the appeal and sustained the divestiture, reasoning that BCO 13-2 and 34-10 allowed the presbytery to follow that procedure once the constitutional three-year limit had long passed and no call to a particular ministry was available. The action therefore left the divestiture without censure in force. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__1999-05.md) |
| [1999-06](../cases/ga29_2001__1999-06.md) | Robert M. Shive v. Central Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | A member challenged Presbytery’s affirmance of his excommunication, contending that SJC Case 97-9 had reversed his four guilty verdicts and precluded increased censure. The SJC did not sustain either issue. Case 97-9 had reversed only the original censure, leaving guilt intact. Because the offender bore the burden of showing repentance, and Session’s contrary factual determination received great deference absent clear error, the later excommunication remained in effect. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__1999-06.md) |
| [1999-07](../cases/ga29_2001__1999-07.md) | Jeffrey M. Black v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | Black challenged Presbytery’s handling of his complaint and its ruling that a licentiate’s non-24-hour creation-day view was not an exception to the Westminster Standards. The SJC denied remand because Presbytery had effectively adjudicated the complaint; the separate objection to proceeding without Black was not first filed below and was administratively out of order. It upheld Presbytery’s ruling that not every non-Calendar-Day view constitutes an exception. Because the record did not identify the licentiate’s precise view, the decision did not determine its acceptability or whether it could be taught. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__1999-07.md) |
| [2000-01](../cases/ga29_2001__2000-01.md) | Erwin Morrison, et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery |  |  | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__2000-01.md) |
| [2000-02](../cases/ga29_2001__2000-02.md) | Adams v. Northeast Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | A prosecutor's complaint relating to Northeast Presbytery's judicial case was held administratively out of order by the SJC. The SJC adopted two grounds: BCO 43-1 bars a complaint about a matter subject to an appeal in another case, and the complaint sought retrial of an accused on charges of which he had been acquitted, relief the SJC could not grant. Although the related appeal was later withdrawn and the withdrawal approved, the SJC still applied BCO 43-1. A concurrence disputed that first ground and urged BCO clarification of appellate review of acquittals. No merits ruling was made. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__2000-02.md) |
| [2000-03](../cases/ga29_2001__stub_2000-03.md) | South Coast Memorial | Withdrawn | The 29th General Assembly record reports that the South Coast Memorial was withdrawn. North Texas | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC found a complaint against North Texas Presbytery out of order because the complainant lacked standing: he was not a member of a PCA church. It did not reach the merits or identify the underlying presbytery action. The vote was 19 concur, 0 dissent, 0 abstain, and 5 absent. The sparse minute entry illustrates that complaint standing before the SJC requires PCA membership; no separate reasoning or incorporated decision appears. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__2000-04.md) |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2000-09](../cases/ga30_2002__2000-09.md) | Sung Keon Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Annulled | After Korean Capital Presbytery appointed a commission to suspend the functions of Korean Presbyterian Church Washington’s elected Session and replace them without prior congregational consent, TE Sung Keon Kim complained. The SJC sustained the complaint, holding that Presbytery violated Preliminary Principle Six and BCO 16-2, misread BCO 13-9, and impermissibly sought to act for the lower court. Relying on Chappell, it annulled the commission’s subsequent actions as the Session. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2000-09.md) |
| [2001-01](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-01.md) | Charles Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | The SJC addressed whether a presbytery and its administrative/judicial commission could suspend a local session and assume original jurisdiction over charges against a ruling elder. It answered no: original jurisdiction belonged to the session, and a presbytery cannot "act for" a lower court by replacing congregationally elected elders. Preliminary Principle Six, BCO 16-2, and BCO 31-1/33-1 controlled; the commission's interpretation of BCO 13-9 conflicted with those principles. The Presbytery's and Commission's actions, including the censure approved by Presbytery, were reversed. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-01.md) |
| [2001-02](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-02.md) | Sang Roo Ryoo v. Korean Capital | Sustained; Remanded | Korean Capital Presbytery's commission tried and suspended a Korean church assistant pastor for heresy and schism after he failed to answer two summonses, and Presbytery upheld the suspension. On appeal, the SJC sustained the procedural challenge because no indictment with witnesses had been served and the record lacked any minutes or hearing for the default judgment, contrary to BCO 32-3. The SJC remanded for a new trial but expressly declined to rule on the citations, default procedure, or merits. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-02.md) |
| [2001-03](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-03.md) | Sung Keon Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | A minister suspended for five years after a default judgment of heresy and schism appealed to the SJC. The SJC sustained the appeal on procedural grounds, holding the judicial commission did not follow BCO 32 and 34: no indictment was served, and the record lacked minutes or evidence of a hearing. It remanded to Korean Capital Presbytery for a new trial. The SJC expressly did not reach the merits, the adequacy of the citations, or whether default judgment is proper. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-03.md) |
| [2001-04](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-04.md) | Moon K. Ham v. Korean Capital | Sustained; Reversed | A ruling elder appealed Korean Capital Presbytery’s three-year suspension of him from the sacraments. The SJC sustained the appeal and reversed the Presbytery’s and its Judicial Commission’s actions because original jurisdiction over a communicant member belonged to his local Session. The ruling rests on the identity of the proper court, not the merits of the charges, and orders no remand or further relief. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-04.md) |
| [2001-05](../cases/ga29_2001__stub_2001-05.md) | RE Moon K. Ham et al. v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Withdrawn | The SJC’s 2002 report records the complaint of Moon K. Ham and others against Korean Capital Presbytery as withdrawn. The withdrawal notice gives no reason for the withdrawal and reports no decision on the underlying dispute. | [full text](../cases/ga29_2001__stub_2001-05.md) |
| [2001-07](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-07.md) | Andy Lee v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Annulled | A complaint challenged Presbytery’s suspension of a church Session and installation of a commission to perform Session functions without prior congregational consent. The SJC sustained the complaint and annulled all subsequent commission actions as the Session insofar as the commission was improperly constituted. Presbytery could not suspend elected officers and replace them without prior consent; later recognition could not cure the defect. The SJC also rejected Presbytery’s objection that a related appeal barred the complaint because the cases presented fundamentally different issues. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-07.md) |
| [2001-08](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-08.md) | TE Andy Lee v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* |  |  | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-08.md) |
| [2001-09](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-09.md) | Charles Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | On appeal from an excommunication, the SJC held that Korean Capital Presbytery and its special judicial commission were not the proper court of original jurisdiction to adjudicate charges against a communicant member. Because original jurisdiction belonged to the local session, the Presbytery/commission proceedings against the member were unconstitutional and reversed. The ruling turned only on jurisdiction; it did not decide the truth of the schism and contumacy charges. The excommunication therefore could not stand on that record. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-09.md) |
| [2001-10](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-10.md) | Charles C. Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery (Appeal) | Sustained; Reversed | A communicant member appealed Presbytery’s findings of guilt and censure of excommunication. The SJC reversed Presbytery’s and its Special Judicial Commission’s actions because original jurisdiction over judicial proceedings against a local church’s communicant member belonged to that church’s Session. The decision was jurisdictional: it neither resolved the underlying charges nor directed a remand or acquittal. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-10.md) |
| [2001-11](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-11.md) | Byung Han Yoo v. Korean Northwest Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated); Dismissed | A ruling elder's complaint against Korean Northwest Presbytery was found out of order because it was untimely under BCO 43-2. The action complained of was taken October 10, 2000, but the complaint was filed March 12, 2001, well outside the 30-day filing period, so the SJC reached no merits question. The case illustrates that a complaint failing BCO 43-2 timeliness is dismissed without adjudication of the underlying presbytery action. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-11.md) |
| [2001-12](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-12.md) | Peter Lee v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | The SJC sustained Deacon Peter Lee’s appeal from Korean Capital Presbytery’s censure and reversed the Presbytery and its special judicial commission for adjudicating charges that belonged to the local Session. Lee, a communicant member, had been suspended from the sacraments for five years after the commission found contumacy and a false statement; Presbytery approved. The SJC held that original jurisdiction over a member’s judicial action or censure rests with the Session, not Presbytery or its commission. The reversal was jurisdictional and did not decide the underlying charges. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-12.md) |
| [2001-13](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-13.md) | Samuel Hong v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | Deacon Samuel Hong appealed after Korean Capital Presbytery's special judicial commission convicted him of contumacy and making a false statement and imposed a two-year suspension from the sacraments. The SJC sustained the appeal, holding that original jurisdiction over charges against a communicant member belongs to the local Session, not Presbytery or its commission, and reversed the Presbytery/commission actions. The SJC did not decide the underlying charges; the reversal was jurisdictional, not an acquittal. One recorded dissent gave no reasons. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-13.md) |
| [2001-14](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-14.md) | Moon K. Ham v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | Ruling Elder Moon K. Ham appealed to the SJC after Korean Capital Presbytery approved a Special Judicial Commission's guilty findings for contumacy, church division/destruction, and a false statement, imposing indefinite suspension from the sacraments (called excommunication in the appeal). The SJC sustained the appeal and reversed Presbytery's and the Commission's actions. Because Ham was a church member, the Session, not Presbytery or its commission, was the proper court of original jurisdiction. The ruling did not remand, acquit, or address the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-14.md) |
| [2001-15](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-15.md) | In Mo Chung v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | The SJC decided whether a presbytery and its special judicial commission could assume original jurisdiction over disciplinary charges against a church member. It sustained the appeal and reversed: the court of original jurisdiction for communicant members is the local church session. Because the appellant was a member of Korean Presbyterian Church of Washington, the presbytery and its commission acted contrary to constitutional standards when they adjudicated the charges and imposed excommunication. The ruling rested on jurisdiction, not the merits; no remand was ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-15.md) |
| [2001-16](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-16.md) | Joo Bok Suh v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | In this SJC appeal, RE Joo Bok Suh challenged Korean Capital Presbytery's excommunication after its special judicial commission, rather than his local session, received and adjudicated charges that he had committed contumacy and made a false statement in a civil-court filing. The SJC sustained the appeal and reversed the Presbytery/commission action because the court of original jurisdiction over a communicant member is the local session. The reversal was jurisdictional; the SJC did not rule on the truth of the charges, so it was not an acquittal. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-16.md) |
| [2001-17](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-17.md) | Choon Soon Lee v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | Korean Capital Presbytery approved a three-year suspension from the sacraments after its special judicial commission found RE Choon Soon Lee guilty of contumacy and making a false statement on a civil-court document. On appeal, the SJC sustained and reversed. Because Lee was a communicant member, the Session—not Presbytery or its commission—was the court of original jurisdiction; the commission's adjudication therefore violated constitutional standards. The SJC did not decide the underlying charges, and one commissioner dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-17.md) |
| [2001-18](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-18.md) | Sang Soo Ryoo v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | The SJC sustained an assistant pastor’s appeal from a three-year suspension from the sacraments after a Korean Capital Presbytery commission found him guilty of participating in the call of an allegedly unconstitutional presbytery meeting. The decisive defect was procedural: no indictment was served, no prosecutor was clearly appointed, and no trial minutes appeared in the record; service of an indictment with a witness list was critical. The case was remanded for a new trial, and the SJC expressly did not reach the merits or citation adequacy. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-18.md) |
| [2001-19](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-19.md) | Sung Keon Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | On appeal from a minister’s deposition in a conflicted Korean Capital Presbytery church, the SJC asked whether the commission followed BCO procedures in instituting and conducting judicial process. It sustained the appeal, holding that no indictment appears to have been served and the record lacked minutes showing a trial, contrary to BCO 32-3’s writing, indictment, and service requirements. The SJC remanded to Presbytery for a new trial under BCO 32 and 34. It expressly did not rule on the adequacy of the citations or the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-19.md) |
| [2001-20](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-20.md) | Dae Hee Lee et al. v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Withdrawn | This complaint against Korean Capital Presbytery was withdrawn. The published record does not identify the challenged action, explain the withdrawal, decide the merits, or award relief. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-20.md) |

Korean Capital Presbytery | Withdrawn | A complaint against Korean Capital Presbytery was withdrawn at the 30th Assembly, which classified it with withdrawn rather than abandoned cases. The sparse record states that no separate merits opinion was published and gives no challenged action, grounds, or reason for withdrawal. Practical effect: no merits ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-21.md) |
| [2001-22](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-22.md) | Moon Ham v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Withdrawn | This sparse record concerns Complaint 01-22 by Moon K. Ham against Korean Capital Presbytery. The 30th General Assembly record lists the complaint as withdrawn, with no separate merits opinion and no stated reasons; the source does not describe the challenged lower-court action, the allegations, or any relief. It should be cited only as a withdrawal disposition, not as a merits precedent or an abandonment. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-22.md) |
| [2001-23](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-23.md) | Dae Hee Lee et al. v. Korean Capital Presbytery | Withdrawn | This sparse record shows complaint 2001-23, brought by Deacon Dae Hee Lee et al. against Korean Capital Presbytery, was withdrawn. The 30th Assembly (2002) listed it among withdrawn cases, and the source states that no separate merits opinion was published. The withdrawal ended the matter without any adopted merits ruling, stated reasons, or relief. The concrete dispute and grounds are not described, so the synopsis is limited to that procedural disposition. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-23.md) |
| [2001-24](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-24.md) | Keith A. Williams v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Withdrawn | A complaint by Keith A. Williams against Eastern Carolina Presbytery was withdrawn. The 30th Assembly's 2002 record lists it among withdrawn cases, and the stub identifies the Standing Judicial Commission as the court. No separate merits opinion was published; the source does not describe the challenged presbytery action, any issue-level ruling, or any adopted reason. The only usable information is the procedural disposition. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-24.md) |
| [2001-25](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-25.md) | Anthony Dallison v. North Florida Presbytery | Not sustained; Affirmed | After TE Anthony Dallison confessed spousal abuse and received an indefinite suspension, North Florida Presbytery later found him impenitent and deposed him and suspended him from the sacraments. Dallison complained that increasing the censure required fresh charges and trial. The SJC disagreed: his BCO 38-1 confession waived trial rights as to the original charge; the offender then had to satisfy the court of repentance, and the presbytery could investigate and impose higher censure under BCO 37-3, 37-4, 37-8, and 34-4b. The complaint was not sustained. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-25.md) |
| [2001-26](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-26.md) | David Scott Price v. Northern Illinois Presbytery | Abandoned | David Scott Price’s complaint against Northern Illinois Presbytery was abandoned. | [2001-27](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-27.md) | David Scott Price v. Northern Illinois Presbytery | Abandoned | The complaint concerning David Scott Price and Northern Illinois Presbytery was abandoned. The sparse Assembly record does not identify the challenged action, explain the abandonment, or decide the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__stub_2001-27.md) |
| [2001-28](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-28.md) | Larry Ball v. Westminster Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained; Affirmed | A complaint challenged Westminster Presbytery's denial of a challenge to its May 15, 2001 plan to withdraw from the PCA. The SJC answered no: the Presbytery did not err, and denied the complaint. BCO 25's local-church withdrawal right and the BCO Preface's grassroots principles extend to presbyteries unless expressly excluded, so a presbytery may determine its own membership and affiliation. The plan did not 'act for' local churches; they retained rights to remain in the PCA or leave, and retained property and internal operations. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-28.md) |

The ruling did not address the complaint’s merits. | [full text](../cases/ga30_2002__2001-36_2001-37.md) |

## 31st General Assembly (2003)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2001-06](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-06.md) | Sang Bai Kim and RE Chan Soo Kim v. Korean Eastern Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | The SJC sustained a complaint by two ruling elders against Korean Eastern Presbytery over a disputed congregational meeting (three commissioners dissented). The session was deadlocked and had not called a requested meeting; KEP's administrative commission called and supervised the February 18, 2001 meeting, and KEP approved that action. The SJC reversed both lower-court actions, holding that BCO 25-2 places responsibility on the session and that the presbytery's proper course was to adjudicate a complaint under BCO 43, not act for the session. It declined to rule on the meeting's legal effect. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-06.md) |
| [2001-30](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-30.md) | Third Presbyterian Church v. Evangel Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order | Third Presbyterian Church asked the SJC to review Evangel Presbytery's denial of its complaint against a Presbytery commission's binding arbitration award of $57,000 to Covenant Classical School. The SJC ruled the complaint not judicially in order, not denied on the merits: PCA courts may not serve as civil arbitrators, and neither Presbytery nor the SJC may review legally binding arbitration judgments. Church courts may act only as ecclesiastical arbitrators, whose decisions lack civil effect and are reviewable under BCO 43. The complaint's five grounds were not reached. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-30.md) |
| [2001-31](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-31.md) | Deacon Neil Jeansonne v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Denied; Remanded | After Deacon Neil Jeansonne was convicted by his Session of causing dissension and strife, Eastern Carolina Presbytery reversed, then reinstated the conviction; the SJC asked whether his General Assembly appeal was properly before it. It was not. Because Case 2001-33 held ECP erred in hearing RE Williams's complaint, ECP's April 21, 2001 reversal and removal of censures stand. The case is remanded to the Session, which may retry using the prior indictment or not pursue the matter. BCO 43-1 barred the complaint once appeal was taken. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-31.md) |
| [2001-32](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-32.md) | Christ Covenant Church Session v. Central Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order | Christ Covenant Church Session complained under BCO 43 that Central Carolina Presbytery wrongly annulled the session trial commission's judgment and found due-process error concerning H. The SJC did not reach the merits. It held the case not judicially in order because H's complaint to the Session was untimely: it exceeded the thirty-day requirement of BCO 43-2 and came more than eighty days after the oral verdict. The ruling thus turned on a procedural gate, making no merits ruling on Presbytery's annulment or its due-process rationale. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-32.md) |
| [2001-33](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-33.md) | W.W. Marshall Jr. v. Eastern Carolina Presbytery | Sustained | A minister challenged Presbytery’s decision to sustain a complaint that rescinded its earlier appellate reversal of a deacon’s conviction and censures. The SJC sustained the minister’s complaint because BCO 43-1 bars a complaint in a judicial case once an appeal has been taken, whether the appeal is past, current, or pending. It reversed Presbytery’s July judgment, restored the April judgment, and remanded to the Session, which could retry the charges using the prior indictment or decline to pursue them. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2001-33.md) |
| [2002-01](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-01.md) | Sang Bai Kim and RE Chan Soo Kim v. Korean Eastern Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Affirmed; Reversed | In a dispute arising from a Korean Eastern Presbytery congregational election, two ruling elders were tried, refused to plead, and were suspended from sacraments and office; KEP denied their appeal, then increased the censures to deposition and excommunication for contumacy. The SJC affirmed KEP's denial and the guilt findings, but reversed the increased censures. An appellate court may not increase a censure under BCO 42-9; the trial court retains discretion to remove or increase an indefinite suspension, though the appellate court may recommend a lesser censure. The original suspension remained. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-01.md) |
| [2002-04](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-04.md) | Judicial Reference from Evangel Presbytery for Adjudication | Denied | Evangel Presbytery asked the SJC to accept and adjudicate a judicial reference. The SJC did not accede to the request. The published report gives no reason for declining the reference and no judgment on the underlying matter. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-04.md) |
| [2002-06](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-06.md) | Wright v. Northern California Presbytery | Judicially out of order; Withdrawn | The SJC docket reports that Randall Wright’s appeal against Northern California Presbytery “was found out of order and withdrawn.” No merits opinion followed. The sparse record does not disclose the challenged lower-court action, the grounds, or any relief; it establishes only procedural termination of the appeal. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-06.md) |
| [2002-07](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-07.md) | Nichols and Couch v. James River Presbytery | Judicially out of order | The SJC found Nichols and Couch’s complaint against James River Presbytery out of order. The docket report does not identify the challenged Presbytery action, the grounds for the ruling, or an administrative or judicial subtype. No merits opinion followed under this docket number. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-07.md) |
| [2002-10](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-10.md) | Carolyn Goerig v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | A church member challenged Pacific Northwest Presbytery's denial of her complaint that Faith Presbyterian Church (Tacoma) Session had banned her from worship and sought to erase her membership retroactively under BCO 38-4. The SJC sustained the complaint. Because she never joined Covenant PCA, BCO 46-3 left her membership under Faith's Session; the Session's proposed retroactive erasure did not comply with BCO 38-4's pastoral-oversight steps. The Presbytery therefore erred and should have remanded the matter to the Session. No separate merits relief beyond that remand directive appears. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-10.md) |
| [2002-11](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-11.md) | Brian Abshire v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Denied; Remanded | Abshire challenged Presbytery’s commission for its appointment, scope, exhortation, summary disposition of two elders’ complaint, and other actions. The SJC sustained only the challenge to deciding the elders’ complaint without BCO 43 procedures and remanded that complaint. It denied the other issues: the commission could conduct the required investigation, the scope objection was untimely, and its exhortation and report were pastoral rather than formal discipline. A separate sixth issue remained before Presbytery and was not decided. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__2002-11.md) |
| [2002-12](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-12.md) | Warren E. Gardner v. North Georgia Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | TE Warren E. Gardner's complaint against North Georgia Presbytery was found out of order. The supplied record contains no separate merits opinion, issue-level ruling, or adopted reasoning; it only cross-references 31-35, II, p. 93. The practical effect is that the complaint did not proceed to a merits adjudication. The case is useful mainly as a sparse-record example: the disposition is recorded, but the basis for the out-of-order finding is not disclosed. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-12.md) |
| [2002-13](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-13.md) | Lachman v. Philadelphia | Out of order (type not stated) | Philadelphia Presbytery was the respondent in a complaint by RE David C. Lachman et al., but the record does not identify the challenged action. The General Assembly found the complaint out of order. No separate merits opinion was published, and the stub supplies no issue-level rulings, reasons, or relief beyond the out-of-order disposition and a cross-reference to 31-35, II, p. 93. It therefore supports only a status note, not a merits synopsis. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-13.md) |
| [2002-15](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-15.md) | Walter Bjorck v. Northern New England Presbytery | Administratively out of order | This sparse record disposes of TE Walter Bjorck's complaint against Northern New England Presbytery as out of order; no merits opinion or reason for that ruling is published. The same minute entry also records that a request that the SJC accept and act upon the judicial reference in Case 2002-04 was not acceded to. It therefore documents only a procedural status, not the complaint's substance or the basis for the ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga31_2003__stub_2002-15.md) |

## 32nd General Assembly (2004)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2002-05](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-05.md) | Plowman v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | A church session suspended a member from the Lord's Table and found her guilty of contumacy. The chairman of Presbytery's Judicial Business Committee advised her that her appeal was out of order; Presbytery later ruled her complaint untimely. The SJC sustained her complaint: she had submitted to regular trial, making her appeal in order under BCO 42-2, and her complaint was timely after Presbytery's advice caused the appeal to be renamed. It remanded for Presbytery to adjudicate the appeal. Filing the appeal suspended the Lord's Table censure pending final adjudication; the SJC questioned the contumacy ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-05.md) |
| [2002-09](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-09.md) | Merriam v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery | Denied | The SJC denied TE Stuart H. Merriam's appeal from Tennessee Valley Presbytery's April 20, 2002 conviction on three ordination-vow specifications and its indefinite suspension. It rejected procedural challenges—that the earlier called meeting was improperly convened and that TVP was prejudiced by prior cases—and found no clear error, applying BCO 39-3(2)'s deference to lower-court factual findings. The SJC sustained TVP's judgment; prior SJC Cases 95-10 and 96-7 had only indirect bearing and did not require retrial. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-09.md) |
| [2002-14](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-14.md) | Lachman v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Denied | A ruling elder convicted by his Session of fifth-, sixth-, and ninth-commandment violations challenged the conviction because the prosecution presented no live witness and relied on his own letters. The SJC denied the appeal and upheld Presbytery's affirmance, holding that the documents, Lachman's acknowledgments of writing them, and defense witnesses supplied sufficient proof; the Session also followed Matthew 18 with patience. A concurrence urged change-of-venue procedures for small-Session bias cases, but noted Lachman never requested a reference or challenged the court before trial. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-14.md) |
| [2002-16](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-16.md) | Session of Delhi Presbyterian Church v. Louisiana Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | The SJC sustained a session's timely complaint that Louisiana Presbytery barred one of its members from the Lord's Table after assuming original jurisdiction. Under BCO 33-1, a presbytery may assume jurisdiction only when the session 'refuses to act'; because the session had investigated and found no strong presumption of guilt, it acted, so LAP never acquired jurisdiction. The censure was therefore a nullity, and the member remained under his session. The SJC also held the jurisdictional assumption reviewable despite no timely complaint against it. Three commissioners dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-16.md) |
| [2002-17](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-17.md) | Sung K. Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order | TE Sung K. Kim appealed his deposition by Korean Capital Presbytery, but the SJC declared the appeal judicially out of order. Because Kim had not submitted to a regular trial, he was not entitled to appeal under BCO 42-2; the Presbytery had instead found him guilty of contumacy and deposed him from office under BCO 34-4. The ruling disposes of the appeal without reaching its merits and leaves the deposition in force. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-17.md) |
| [2002-18](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-18.md) | Mark Herzor and Erwin Morrison v. Philadelphia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order | Case 2002-18 is a complaint (the heading also says "Reference") identified in the adopted motion as TE Erwin Morrison v. Philadelphia Presbytery. The SJC found it judicially out of order because it was filed against actions of a judicial commission, not completed Presbytery actions (BCO 15-3, 43-1). The full SJC approved that action on October 16, 2003. No merits ruling or later dismissal is stated; the Manual only outlines a possible dismissal route. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2002-18.md) |
| [2003-01](../cases/ga32_2004__2003-01.md) | Mark Chavalas v. Northern Illinois Presbytery | Sustained | Chavalas appealed a Northern Illinois Presbytery judgment, but testimony cited by both parties was missing from the record. Because recording-machine failures made the required transcripts unavailable and Presbytery could not cure the defect, the SJC found the appeal judicially out of order, dismissed it under SJC Manual 11.6, and suspended Presbytery’s judgment under BCO 42-7. Chavalas’s later resignation did not abandon the appeal because he had appealed beforehand after a regular trial; no rebuke was issued. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2003-01.md) |
| [2003-06](../cases/ga32_2004__2003-06.md) | Wright v. Eastern Carolina  ·  *dissent* | No final disposition in available record | The SJC found Case 2003-6 judicially out of order because the challenged Eastern Carolina Presbytery action was only advice in response to a BCO 41-1 reference from White Oak Session, not a binding or complainable doctrinal decision under BCO 41-3. The underlying dispute—whether elders could hold and teach a broader atonement view—was therefore not reached. The adopted vote was 17 concurring, 1 dissenting, with 1 abstention and 4 absent. The dissent argued Presbytery's 'not allowed' language read as compulsory final disposition. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2003-06.md) |
| [2003-07](../cases/ga32_2004__2003-07.md) | Aureliano Tan v. South Texas Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | TE Aureliano Tan filed a complaint against South Texas Presbytery with the SJC before the presbytery had heard it. The SJC found the complaint administratively out of order as prematurely filed, so it reached no merits. The adopted vote was 19 concurring, 0 dissenting, with 4 absent; the full SJC approved the ruling on March 4, 2004. The same pages separately contain proposed SJC Manual changes and a dissent, not a ruling in this complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga32_2004__2003-07.md) |
|  | SJC judicial decision (GA 32, p.49) |  |  | _no separate decision located_ · [ga32_2004 p.49](../markdown/ga32_2004.md#ga32-p49) |

| 2004-03 | Tim J. Harris v. Heritage Presbytery | not_sustained |  | _decided at 33rd GA_ · [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-03.md) |
| 2004-02 | Nancy J. Plowman v. Philadelphia Presbytery | sustained |  | _decided at 33rd GA_ · [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-02.md) |

## 33rd General Assembly (2005)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2001-34](../cases/ga33_2005__2001-34_2002-02_2002-03.md) | Nichols and Couch v. James River Presbytery (Case 2001-34)  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | Church members challenged Presbytery’s repeated delay in granting a properly adopted congregational request to dissolve its pastoral relationship and its gift equivalent to three months of the pastor’s compensation. The SJC found seven complaints judicially out of order, sustained the dissolution-delay challenge, and rejected the gift challenge because Presbytery controlled its own civil property. No further relief was ordered. A concurrence would permit delay based on objective, justifiable findings, but concluded that Presbytery’s stated grounds were insufficient. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2001-34_2002-02_2002-03.md) |
| [2002-02](../cases/ga33_2005__2001-34_2002-02_2002-03.md) | Nichols and Couch v. James River Presbytery (Case 2002-02)  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | Three complaints arising from Presbytery’s treatment of Session actions toward its pastor were judicially out of order. Complaints 10-01 and the unnumbered complaint were filed directly with the SJC without first being presented to James River Presbytery, contrary to BCO 43-2. Complaint 13-01 challenged an action never taken because the motion was ruled out of order. The SJC did not reach their merits. The combined decision’s pastoral-dissolution and benevolent-gift rulings concerned the other consolidated cases, not Case 2002-02. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2001-34_2002-02_2002-03.md) |
| [2002-03](../cases/ga33_2005__2001-34_2002-02_2002-03.md) | Nichols and Couch v. James River Presbytery (Case 2002-03)  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | Ruling elders challenged James River Presbytery’s delay in honoring West End’s valid congregational vote to dissolve its pastoral relationship and a three-month salary gift to the pastor. The SJC held that Presbytery lacked a constitutional basis to delay the dissolution, but rejected the challenge to its benevolent use of its own civil funds. Complaints 16-01, 18-01, and 19-01 were judicially out of order; the others were in order. No remand or repayment was ordered. A concurrence cautioned that prompt dissolution is not automatic when objective, justifiable findings support refusal. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2001-34_2002-02_2002-03.md) |
| [2003-02](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-02_2003-05.md) | Thornton v. Westminster  ·  *dissent* | Remanded | Thornton challenged Westminster Presbytery’s investigation of TE Frank Smith’s secret recording and its refusal to institute process. The SJC remanded for a new BCO 31-2 investigation because Presbytery had not timely obtained the other participant’s testimony or examined relevant legal and confessional questions. It rejected claims concerning alleged lying and threats, partiality, and an unbiblical recording policy. The invitation of an outside voluntary prosecutor was moot because he declined; only a separate concurrence found the invitation improper. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-02_2003-05.md) |
| [2003-05](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-02_2003-05.md) | Thornton v. Westminster  ·  *dissent* | Remanded | Thornton challenged Presbytery’s investigation and refusal to institute process against Smith over secretly recording Roberts, alleged lying and threats, partiality, and its taping policy. The SJC sustained only the challenge to the secret-taping investigation: the committee failed to interview Roberts before reporting and examine the legal and confessional implications of transcription and destruction. It remanded for a new BCO 31-2 investigation. The other claims lacked sufficient proof; the voluntary-prosecutor issue was moot because Roberts declined. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-02_2003-05.md) |
| [2003-03](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-03.md) | Paul W. Lee v. Korean Southwest | Sustained | A minister appealed three convictions and suspension that would automatically become indefinite absent repentance. The SJC sustained the appeal: unauthorized committee citations and admonition without process could not support guilt, and multiple trial violations were fatally prejudicial. It set aside the judgment, dismissed the citation and admonition charges, and revoked both censures. The inappropriate-language charge was not adjudicated on its merits; Presbytery could retry it on remand or dismiss it. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-03.md) |
| [2003-04](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-04.md) | Dr. and Mrs. Frank Chin v. Covenant Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | The Chins complained to the SJC after Covenant Presbytery denied their complaint against Covenant Presbyterian Church Session's refusal to examine their young children for admission to the Lord's Supper. The SJC did not sustain the complaint. Its adopted reasoning held that BCO 57-2 leaves the time for examining young persons to the Session's prudence and imposes no mandate to examine every child put forward; if the Session decides the time has come, careful examination is required. The dissent read BCO 57-1 as requiring examination of covenant children seeking admission. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2003-04.md) |
| [2004-01](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-01_2004-10.md) | Westminster Presbyterian Church v. Westminster Presbytery | Withdrawn | Westminster Presbyterian Church’s complaint against Westminster Presbytery was withdrawn. The SJC docket report gives no reason for the withdrawal, identifies no merits determination, and records no further relief or proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-01_2004-10.md) |
| [2004-10](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-01_2004-10.md) | Merriam v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery | Withdrawn | The appeal of TE Stuart Merriam against Tennessee Valley Presbytery was withdrawn. The report does not identify the challenged judgment, explain the withdrawal, or provide a merits opinion. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-01_2004-10.md) |
| [2004-02](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-02.md) | Nancy J. Plowman v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Sustained | A member suspended from the Lord's Table appealed after Presbytery let the Session resume her trial and failed to lift all suspensions. The SJC sustained the appeal, finding the Session's premature censure, truncated trial, and prosecutor-judge conflict manifested prejudice and made a fair trial impossible. It set aside all judgments and censures, treated the November 13, 2003 communion letter as non-censuring advice, and did not remand. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-02.md) |
| [2004-03](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-03.md) | Tim J. Harris v. Heritage Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | A church member challenged Heritage Presbytery's ruling that the BCO neither expressly nor implicitly requires a congregation to approve changes in a pastor's call, and that it does not require the session to publicize budget details. The SJC held the BCO silent on both points: once the original call and terms are approved, later changes are budgetary and belong to the session under BCO 12-5b; publicizing the budget is prudent but not mandated. The complaint was not sustained; seven commissioners dissented, arguing congregational approval follows by good and necessary consequence. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-03.md) |
| [2004-04](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-04.md) | Jerguson v. Western Carolina | Judicially out of order | The SJC held Jerguson’s appeal judicially out of order under BCO 42-2 because Western Carolina Presbytery was rehearing the case. The matter therefore was not ready for higher-court review. The decision did not reach the merits or dismiss the underlying Presbytery proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-04.md) |
| [2004-05](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-05.md) | Blevins v. Westminster | Sustained | SJC sustained TE Philip Blevins's complaint against Westminster Presbytery, holding the Blevins/Seufert complaint was timely despite the commission's February 3, 2004 untimeliness ruling. The wife's April 2003 letter was essentially a BCO 35-13 request to review new evidence, and the Session's May 2003 denial was a new complaintable decision. The SJC did not decide the wife's divorce grounds or the Session's merits; it remanded for Presbytery to rehear the Session and Warhurst complaints with the Blevins/Seufert merits considered. A concurrence added that no full new trial is required. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-05.md) |
| [2004-06](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-06.md) | Aureliano Tan Jr. v. Houston Metro Presbytery | Judicially out of order | Tan attempted to appeal without first submitting to a regular trial. The SJC found the appeal judicially out of order under BCO 42-2 and therefore did not reach its merits. Although the source header labels the disposition “dismissed,” the adopted judgment expressly classifies it as judicially out of order. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-06.md) |
| [2004-07](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-07.md) | Session of First Presbyterian Augusta v. Savannah River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | A presbytery annulled a session's actions after the session added to a petition-called congregational meeting a recommendation that the congregation invoke BCO 24-6 against eleven elders. The SJC sustained the session's complaint: BCO 25-2 permits a session to add agenda items, and BCO 24-6 does not require a member-initiated motion; the congregation's own 503–229 vote began the process. It reinstated the session's actions, declared remaining relief moot, and noted one recorded dissent; no dissent text appears. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-07.md) |
| [2004-09](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-09.md) | Scott Robar v. Central Carolina Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | The SJC found RE Scott Robar's appeal from Central Carolina Presbytery judicially out of order and dismissed it because he filed his notice of appeal only with Presbytery's stated clerk, not also with the General Assembly's stated clerk as BCO 42-4 requires. No merits review of his conviction occurred. Four commissioners dissented, arguing that the presbytery clerk's admittedly incorrect advice, Robar's reliance, and Presbytery's request to treat the appeal as timely should excuse the filing defect under the Plowman precedent and equity or estoppel principles. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__2004-09.md) |
| [2004-11](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-11.md) | Scott v. Northern California Presbytery | Moot | A teaching elder's appeal against Northern California Presbytery was found moot. The published entry on page 71 identifies neither the challenged ruling nor the reason for mootness, and no separate merits opinion was published. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-11.md) |
| [2004-12](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-12.md) | Hunt v. Western Carolina | Out of order (type not stated) | Mr. M. E. Hunt sought review of an unidentified action by Western Carolina Presbytery. The Assembly record reports that his complaint was found out of order, without identifying an administrative or judicial subtype. It provides no reason, merits determination, relief, or further proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-12.md) |
| [2004-11](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-11.md) | Scott v. Northern California Presbytery | Moot | A teaching elder's appeal against Northern California Presbytery was found moot. The published entry on page 71 identifies neither the challenged ruling nor the reason for mootness, and no separate merits opinion was published. | [disposition](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-11.md) |

It provides no reason, merits determination, relief, or further proceedings. | [disposition](../cases/ga33_2005__stub_2004-12.md) |

## 34th General Assembly (2006)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2004-08](../cases/ga34_2006__2004-08.md) | Thornton v. Westminster Presbytery | Sustained | Thornton challenged Westminster Presbytery’s refusal to place Memorial PCA Session’s call in his hands and its simultaneous initiation of administrative divestiture without censure. The SJC sustained both issues, reversed the divestiture decision, and remanded with directions to approve and deliver the call and set an installation date. A valid call supplied the congregational approbation whose absence BCO 34-10’s administrative remedy requires; Presbytery also could not rely on undisclosed concerns while avoiding required judicial process. The ruling found clear error, not an absence of all presbyterial review authority. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__2004-08.md) |
| [2004-13](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2004-13.md) | Zaepfel v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Abandoned | Complaint 2004-13, TE Wayne Zaepfel v. Central Carolina Presbytery, was abandoned at the 34th General Assembly (2006). The available record gives no merits analysis, adopted reasons, or relief beyond the statement that this case was abandoned; it serves only as a disposition notice, not as authority on the underlying complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2004-13.md) |
| [2005-01](../cases/ga34_2006__2005-01.md) | Chastain v. Heritage Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained; Affirmed | An appeal challenged a presbytery's conviction of a minister for divisive behavior and its indefinite suspension from office and the sacraments. The SJC affirmed: the charge was lawful because publicly publishing disagreement with the session after it acted violated his ordination vows to subjection and peace/unity, even though “divisive” is not a constitutional term; the censure was not unjust absent repentance; procedural errors did not require reversal or remand. A dissent argued the charge failed BCO 29-1’s Scripture-proof requirement. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__2005-01.md) |
| [2005-02](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-02.md) | Andrino v. Southern Florida Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC found RE Hugo Andrino’s complaint against Southern Florida Presbytery out of order. The published record neither identifies the challenged action nor explains the ruling, and it reports no merits determination or further proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-02.md) |
| [2005-03](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-03.md) | Session of Living Word Presbyterian Church v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The Session of Living Word Presbyterian Church brought a complaint against Pacific Northwest Presbytery, but the record does not identify the challenged action. The Standing Judicial Commission found the complaint out of order without specifying an administrative or judicial subtype or providing reasons. No merits determination or further relief is recorded. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-03.md) |
| [2005-04](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-04.md) | Session of Hudson Korean American Presbyterian Church v. Korean Eastern Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The Standing Judicial Commission found the Session’s complaint against Korean Eastern Presbytery out of order. The published record does not describe the challenged action, identify the procedural defect, classify the ruling as administrative or judicial, or provide a merits opinion or relief. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-04.md) |
| [2005-05](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-05.md) | Witcher Memorial re: 2004-05 Blevins v. Westminster Presbytery | In order | A memorial concerning 2004-05 Blevins v. Westminster Presbytery was found in order, but SJC officers decided not to hear it. The docket gives no reason and publishes no merits opinion; it therefore establishes procedural regularity and non-hearing, not a ruling on the memorial’s allegations or the underlying case. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-05.md) |
| [2005-06](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-06.md) | Andrino v. Southern Florida | Out of order (type not stated) | The Standing Judicial Commission, as recorded in the 34th Assembly, found RE Hugo Andrino's complaint against Southern Florida Presbytery out of order. The supplied record includes no separate merits opinion, no adopted reason, and no relief; it does not identify the challenged presbytery action or classify the out-of-order ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-06.md) |
| [2005-07](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-07.md) | Andrino v. Southern Florida Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC at the 34th General Assembly found out of order the complaint of RE Hugo Andrino against Southern Florida Presbytery. The published record gives no merits opinion, no challenged action, and no reason for the ruling; it functions only as a sparse status notice. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__stub_2005-07.md) |
| [2005-08](../cases/ga34_2006__2005-08.md) | Kim v. Korean Eastern Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Not sustained | A minister appealed convictions for contumacy and threatening elders with civil litigation, plus deposition and excommunication. The SJC sustained contumacy as failure to remain subject to the brethren under the ordination vow, not BCO 32-6-type contumacy. It sustained deposition but found excommunication excessive given the conflict and replaced it with indefinite suspension from the sacraments. It did not sustain the threat conviction because the attorney’s letter was absent from the record. A dissent disputed the contumacy ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__2005-08.md) |
| [2005-09](../cases/ga34_2006__2005-09.md) | Kim v. Korean Eastern Presbytery (Complaint)  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Moot | A minister challenged Presbytery’s denial of his complaints concerning a congregational petition and an Administrative Commission’s actions. The SJC sustained both issues: the petition should first have gone to the Session, and Presbytery and its Commission committed additional constitutional errors. Those errors did not excuse his refusal to obey Presbytery, however. Because he had been deposed in Case 2005-8, further action was moot; the SJC granted no remedy, vacatur, or remand. | [full text](../cases/ga34_2006__2005-09.md) |
