## 35th General Assembly (2007)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2006-06](../cases/ga35_2007__2006-06.md) | Ehrlich v. North Georgia | Denied | Members challenged Presbytery’s denial of their complaint against a Session instruction not to circulate an open letter repeating allegations against their pastor. The SJC denied the complaint, leaving the instruction in force. It held that liberty of conscience does not eliminate members’ obligation to obey lawful church injunctions. Here the members had sought the Session’s advice, Presbytery had found no strong presumption of chargeable offenses, and renewed circulation would disturb the church’s peace; under those circumstances, the Session acted within its authority. | [full text](../cases/ga35_2007__2006-06.md) |
| [2006-07](../cases/ga35_2007__2006-07.md) | Michael Chastain v. Heritage Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Out of order (type not stated) | Chastain appealed Heritage Presbytery’s 2006 excommunication and deposition. The SJC held the appeal judicially out of order because Chastain had renounced PCA jurisdiction on May 8, 2006. Consequently, Presbytery’s later proceedings were moot and SJC 2005-1 remained in effect. The SJC did not reach the merits. A concurrence questioned the discipline process after renunciation; a dissent argued the majority lacked constitutional authority to recognize unilateral withdrawal and thereby undercut discipline. | [full text](../cases/ga35_2007__2006-07.md) |
| [2007-05](../cases/ga35_2007__stub_2007-05.md) | Mitchell v. Evangel | Withdrawn | Dr. George W. Mitchell’s appeal against Evangel Presbytery was withdrawn. The 35th General Assembly record reports no merits decision, reason for the withdrawal, or ruling on the unidentified underlying dispute. | [full text](../cases/ga35_2007__stub_2007-05.md) |
| [2007-05](../cases/ga35_2007__stub_2007-05.md) | Mitchell v. Evangel | Withdrawn | Dr. George W. Mitchell’s appeal against Evangel Presbytery was withdrawn. The 35th General Assembly record reports no merits decision, reason for the withdrawal, or ruling on the unidentified underlying dispute. | [disposition](../cases/ga35_2007__stub_2007-05.md) |

## 36th General Assembly (2008)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2006-02](../cases/ga36_2008__2006-02.md) | Memorial of Central Carolina v. Louisiana | Sustained | In a BCO 40-5 memorial, Central Carolina Presbytery alleged that Louisiana Presbytery had exonerated TE J. Steven Wilkins despite his published views on election, the church, perseverance, assurance, and baptism allegedly contradicting the Westminster Standards. The SJC sustained the memorial’s gravity and found Presbytery failed to show due diligence, ordering a recorded examination, formal responses, and report. Presbytery later met the procedural directives, but the SJC relied on Case 2007-8’s substantive finding, cited Presbytery under BCO 40-5, and appointed a prosecutor for Case 2007-14. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2006-02.md) |
| [2007-01](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-01_2007-06_2007-07.md) | Eliot Lee v. Korean Eastern Presbytery | Partially sustained | Lee challenged Korean Eastern Presbytery’s actions affecting his interim pastoral relationship, alleging that its October 3, 2006 meeting lacked a quorum. The SJC held the complaint moot and declared every action taken at that meeting null and void—not merely those concerning his church. The meeting opened with only two ruling elders when three were required, and the minutes did not establish when a quorum was later achieved; its presence therefore could not be presumed. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-01_2007-06_2007-07.md) |
| [2007-06](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-01_2007-06_2007-07.md) | Eliot Lee v. Korean Eastern | Denied; Affirmed | Lee challenged irregular Presbytery meetings, classification of his interim pastorate as stated supply, expiration of his term, and appointment of a Judicial Commission. Meeting-related concerns were moot or resolved by nullification; the SJC otherwise denied the complaint and affirmed Presbytery. No pastoral call had been approved or installation conducted, and BCO 22-6 limited the stated-supply relationship to one year absent renewal. The ruling approved only the Commission’s appointment, not its later actions, which remained subject to pending complaints. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-01_2007-06_2007-07.md) |
| [2007-07](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-01_2007-06_2007-07.md) | S. Han v. Korean Eastern Presbytery | Denied; Affirmed | RE Han challenged Korean Eastern Presbytery’s treatment of TE Lee as stated supply rather than pastor, its clarification of its earlier action, and its appointment of a Pulpit Commission. The SJC denied the complaint and affirmed Presbytery: Lee lacked an approved pastoral call or installation, “interim pastor” meant stated supply under BCO 22, and his unrenewed one-year term had expired. BCO 15-2 permitted the commission’s appointment to process stated-supply requests. The ruling did not approve or disapprove the commission’s later actions. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-01_2007-06_2007-07.md) |
| [2007-02](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-02.md) | Malone v. Metro NY | Partially sustained; Vacated | Malone challenged Presbytery’s approval of a commission’s judgment and indefinite suspension imposed without process. The SJC sustained the complaint in part and vacated the judgment and censure: the commission had authority only to investigate, and Malone’s general acknowledgments neither confessed specific charges nor showed intent to waive process under BCO 38-1. Presbytery could begin anew under proper procedures. The SJC rejected Malone’s claim that suggested steps evidencing repentance improperly bound him, treating them as noncompulsory pastoral advice. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-02.md) |
| [2007-03](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-03.md) | Segallis v. Central Florida | Out of order (type not stated) | The 36th Assembly (2008), on the SJC docket, recorded the complaint of Greg Segallis et al. against Central Florida Presbytery as out of order (36-31, p. 75). No separate merits opinion or reason for the out-of-order disposition appears in the supplied record; the stub supports only that disposition. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-03.md) |
| [2007-04](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-04.md) | Stephen P. Engel v. Evangel Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | A member challenged Presbytery’s refusal to decide his guilt or innocence and its failure to return charges after his Session suspended him from the sacraments without trial or confession. The SJC sustained the complaint on the remand issue: rescission of the censure did not dispose of pending charges, so Presbytery had to rehear the complaint and require the Session either to institute proper process or dismiss them. It declined to determine guilt without a trial record and rejected the claim that the censure’s prescribed form willfully took the Lord’s name in vain. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-04.md) |
| [2007-08](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-08.md) | Jones v. Louisiana | Sustained | Louisiana Presbytery’s refusal to institute process after examining Steve Wilkins’s Federal Vision teachings was challenged for applying the wrong constitutional standard and overlooking a strong presumption that some views were out of conformity with the Westminster Standards. The SJC sustained the complaint and annulled Presbytery’s denial. A “difference” need not expressly contradict the Standards; Presbytery must independently investigate and classify it rather than accept the member’s assurance or shift the burden to objectors. The ruling did not adjudicate Wilkins’s guilt; the Central Carolina memorial remained before the SJC. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-08.md) |
| [2007-12](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-12.md) | Grady v. Southwest Florida  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | A minister complained that Presbytery placed a shepherding committee report containing findings of his alleged “sins and errors” in its minutes and directed that it be read to his congregation. The SJC sustained the complaint and ordered the report expunged. Although a committee may form and record an opinion without judicial process, Presbytery's endorsement and public dissemination effectively admonished the minister on unsubstantiated and unproven opinions without due process. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-12.md) |
| [2007-14](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-14.md) | PCA v. Louisiana Presbytery (Trial)  ·  *dissent* | Guilty; Dismissed | In this BCO 40-5 proceeding over Louisiana Presbytery’s investigation of TE Stephen Wilkins, the SJC dismissed Specification 1 because it could not find that the cited procedures were constitutionally required or that guilt was proved. The dismissal did not erase concerns about undocumented doctrinal findings; those failures were covered by Specification 2. On Presbytery’s guilty plea to that specification, the SJC imposed a public admonition for failing to find a strong presumption of doctrinal guilt and pursue trial. Wilkins’s and his church’s withdrawal made practical amends unavailable. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__2007-14.md) |
| [2007-15](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-15.md) | Sang C. Choi v. Korean Central | Out of order (type not stated) | TE Sang Chul Choi’s complaint against Korean Central Presbytery was found out of order. The sparse published record does not identify the challenged presbytery action, specify an administrative or judicial subtype, explain the ruling, or resolve the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-15.md) |
| [2007-03](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-03.md) | Segallis v. Central Florida | Out of order (type not stated) | The 36th Assembly (2008), on the SJC docket, recorded the complaint of Greg Segallis et al. against Central Florida Presbytery as out of order (36-31, p. 75). No separate merits opinion or reason for the out-of-order disposition appears in the supplied record; the stub supports only that disposition. | [disposition](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-03.md) |
| [2007-15](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-15.md) | Sang C. Choi v. Korean Central | Out of order (type not stated) | TE Sang Chul Choi’s complaint against Korean Central Presbytery was found out of order. The sparse published record does not identify the challenged presbytery action, specify an administrative or judicial subtype, explain the ruling, or resolve the merits. | [disposition](../cases/ga36_2008__stub_2007-15.md) |

## 37th General Assembly (2009)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2007-09](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-09_2007-10.md) | Lee v. Korean Eastern Presbytery (2007-09 & 2007-10) | Sustained; Annulled | Lee challenged Presbytery’s authorization of its Pulpit Commission to act for HKPC’s Session. The SJC sustained the complaint, annulled Presbytery’s decision and the Commission’s resulting actions, and directed return of expended church funds if the congregation requested. Neither the Session nor congregation had requested or consented to that authority. The ruling distinguished the Commission’s valid appointment to receive stated-supply requests from its impermissible empowerment to function as the Session or pulpit committee. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-09_2007-10.md) |
| [2007-10](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-09_2007-10.md) | Lee v. Korean Eastern Presbytery (2007-09 & 2007-10) | Sustained; Annulled | Lee challenged Korean Eastern Presbytery’s ratification and filing of a New Jersey lawsuit through a commission acting for the local church, seeking civil adjudication of who was the rightful pastor and who could act as the church’s session. The SJC sustained the complaint and annulled Presbytery’s decision in whole. PCA courts have moral and spiritual, not civil coercive, authority and cannot sue to enforce ecclesiastical decisions. The ruling does not prohibit litigation over purely civil matters and did not condemn the commission’s original appointment. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-09_2007-10.md) |
| [2007-11](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-11.md) | Eliot Lee v. Korean Eastern | Sustained; Reversed | After Korean Eastern Presbytery tried, convicted, deposed, and excommunicated TE Eliot Lee, the SJC sustained his appeal and reversed the disposition and excommunication. The decisive reason was procedural: the record did not show timely service of the indictment/citations or the required 14-day trial notice, violating BCO 32-3, 32-7, and 32-10. Under BCO 42-9, the SJC rendered the decision it thought should have been made, finding no strong presumption of guilt under BCO 31-2 justified formal process; it added pastoral counsel, expressly not censure. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-11.md) |
| [2007-13](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-13.md) | David Kniseley et al. v. Rocky Mountain Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | On complaint from TE David Kniseley and others, the SJC reviewed Rocky Mountain Presbytery's refusal to forbid a mission church from giving an unordained female staff member the title 'Minister of Church Life.' The SJC denied the complaint. It held the BCO uses 'minister' synonymously with pastor and teaching elder, but is silent on general use for non-ordained staff; a lower court may exercise discretion subject to BCO 40-2(3)'s WESP review. The decision addressed only the constitutional issue, not the title's wisdom or propriety; four commissioners dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-13.md) |
| [2007-16](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-16.md) | Grady v. Southwest Florida  ·  *dissent* | Affirmed; Reversed; Vacated; Remanded | A minister appealed convictions and indefinite suspension arising from conflict over Presbytery's direction that he resign. The SJC upheld all three convictions, finding two procedural errors nonprejudicial and rejecting the other preserved challenges. But Presbytery could not treat its authority to dissolve a pastoral relationship as authority to compel resignation on pain of discipline. The SJC therefore vacated the censure and dissolution of the call and remanded for reconsideration of censure; Grady's separate administrative suspension remained effective. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2007-16.md) |
| [2008-01](../cases/ga37_2009__2008-01_2008-10.md) | Session of Crossroads Community Church v. Philadelphia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied; Affirmed | Two consolidated complaints challenged Philadelphia Presbytery’s licensing and ordination of a candidate who believed Scripture could permit women to hold the deacon’s office but promised to follow the BCO and not ordain women. The SJC denied both complaints and affirmed Presbytery. It distinguished subscription to doctrinal standards from approval of BCO practices, applied a three-part inquiry, and deferred to Presbytery on the candidate’s credible vow. Because complainants identified no violated doctrinal standard, no error was shown. The ruling reaffirmed that the BCO bars ordaining women as deacons. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2008-01_2008-10.md) |
| [2008-10](../cases/ga37_2009__2008-01_2008-10.md) | Mark Grasso et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied; Affirmed | The complaint challenged Presbytery’s approval of a candidate who believed women could hold the diaconal office but promised to follow the BCO, which permits only men to be ordained as deacons. The SJC denied the complaint and affirmed approval of his ordination examination. It held that a BCO difference is evaluated through compliance, doctrinal, and ordination-vow inquiries; the record supported compliance and a credible vow, while complainants had not identified violated doctrinal standards. Absent contrary evidence, Presbytery’s judgment received deference. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2008-01_2008-10.md) |

| [2008-09](../cases/ga37_2009__2008-09.md) | Session of Red Mountain Presbyterian Church v. Evangel Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | Red Mountain Session challenged Presbytery’s ruling that it had inadequately addressed a member’s demand to declare his wife’s divorce unbiblical, and Presbytery’s resulting declaration and Matthew 18 instruction. The SJC sustained all four issues and reversed Presbytery. It held Session’s context-sensitive ruling disclosed no clear error, use of BCO 31-2 was reasonable, Presbytery violated BCO 15-3 by allowing its commission to revise a report after floor discussion, and Presbytery judged the wife without due process. The SJC expressly did not decide whether biblical divorce grounds existed. | [full text](../cases/ga37_2009__2008-09.md) |

## 38th General Assembly (2010)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2008-11](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-11.md) | Broadwater et al. v. Chesapeake Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Vacated | Presbytery empowered a commission both to assume original jurisdiction over matters involving church members and to receive a Session's reference concerning a membership transfer. The SJC vacated the original-jurisdiction mandate because Presbytery had neither found that the Session refused to act nor required the commission to make that finding before proceeding. The authority to receive the reference remained intact; requests from other Sessions alone did not satisfy BCO 33-1. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-11.md) |
| [2008-13](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-13.md) | Meyerhoff v. Chesapeake Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Moot | Meyerhoff challenged Chesapeake Presbytery’s called meeting to receive, discuss, and entertain questions about an original-jurisdiction commission’s initial report. Without deciding whether those actions were procedurally erroneous, the SJC held the complaint moot in view of its judgment in Case 2008-11. It directed Presbytery to note the mootness ruling in the minutes of the August 16, 2008 meeting. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-13.md) |
| [2008-14](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-14.md) | White v. Siouxlands Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | A minister complained that his presbytery wrongly refused to order a BCO 31-2 investigation into another minister’s Covenant of Works/Federal Vision views. The SJC sustained the complaint and remanded for that investigation, holding that BCO 31-2 requires investigation of credible, injurious reports about Christian character. The presbytery erred by applying the higher evidentiary standard for conviction after process; reports sufficient to trigger investigation need not establish guilt or a strong presumption of guilt, and hearsay or conflicting testimony can require investigation rather than justify refusal. A concurrence agreed in result; the dissent found no clear error in the presbytery’s exercise of discretion. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-14.md) |
| [2008-15](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) | Morton Smith v. Western Carolina | Partially sustained | The complaint challenged Presbytery’s commissions and its response to a canceled congregational meeting. The SJC upheld the Inman Commission because a Session may reference limited duties without congregational consent. It sustained the challenges to Presbytery’s consideration of the August 7 meeting and appointment of the Sealy Commission because the Session majority had validly canceled the meeting. Linton’s and Pellom’s calls therefore remained undissolved and Neville was uncalled, absent later congregational action. The SJC also upheld Presbytery’s criticism of the cancellation as unwise and its refusal to issue a BCO 40-5 citation. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) |
| [2008-16](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) | Hutchinson & Bulkeley v. Western Carolina | Denied; Affirmed | Ministers challenged Presbytery’s handling of RE Payne’s confession as a BCO 38-1 case without process, asking the SJC to declare it incomplete and require prosecution of additional alleged sins. The SJC did not sustain the complaint. It held that a complaint cannot bypass the BCO 33-1 and 34-1 procedures by seeking relief akin to original jurisdiction. Further allegations could be filed as formal charges with Payne’s Session, which could investigate, prosecute, or reference the matter to a higher court. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) |
| [2008-17](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) | Hutchinson & Bulkeley v. Western Carolina | Partially sustained | Hutchinson and Bulkeley challenged Presbytery’s decision to suspend RE Neill Payne indefinitely from office but not from the Sacraments. The SJC did not sustain the complaint: BCO 30-3 expressly permits suspension from office without sacramental suspension, and no provision requires both censures together. Because the appropriate censure involved discretion and judgment, the SJC deferred absent clear error. Authority to remove or increase Payne’s censure had returned to his Session. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) |
| [2008-18](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) | Hutchinson & Bulkeley v. Western Carolina | Partially sustained | Hutchinson and Bulkeley challenged Presbytery’s refusal to condemn RE Neill Payne’s views in a circulated “Africa e-mail.” The SJC did not sustain the complaint. It held that a presbytery may not condemn a church officer’s allegedly erroneous opinions without due process and that four sentences, an approving newspaper reference, and the Commission’s summary did not present Payne’s views fully enough for responsible adjudication. The SJC rejected race or intelligence as the primary determinant of a nation’s fate, while allowing the e-mail to be used as evidence in formal charges. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) |
| [2009-01](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) | Morton H. Smith v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Referred | Smith challenged Presbytery’s ruling that his complaint was filed too late. The SJC gave no independent yes-or-no answer on timeliness, instead answering by reference to Case 2008-15. That decision held the August 7 congregational meeting illegitimate and voided resulting Presbytery actions and related complaints. The record does not identify which particular ruling in 2008-15 controlled Smith’s complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) |
| [2009-03](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) | Leissing v. Western Carolina | Partially sustained | Leissing challenged Presbytery's November 7 action dissolving the calls of REs Linton and Pellom. By reference to Case 2008-15, the SJC sustained the complaint: the Session had canceled the August 7 congregational meeting, so it generated no valid dissolution request and Presbytery erred in appointing the Sealy Commission. The Commission proceedings, the complaint against its decision, and Presbytery's resulting dissolution action were voided; absent subsequent congregational action, the elders' calls remained undissolved. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2008-15_2008-16_2008-17_2008-18_2009-01_2009-03.md) |
| [2009-02](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-02.md) | Morton Smith v. Western Carolina | Partially sustained; Annulled; Remanded | Smith challenged Presbytery’s reversal of a finding that an elder’s race-and-IQ views did not create a strong presumption of guilt and its immediate judgment against those views. The SJC upheld the reversal as a discretionary determination, without addressing its merits, but annulled the declaration against the views because a strong-presumption finding required judicial process. On remand, Presbytery was to commence process against the elder or rescind that finding and drop the matter. The ruling did not restrict Presbytery’s ability to adopt general position statements outside an investigation. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-02.md) |
| [2009-05](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) | Payne v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Not sustained; Denied; Affirmed | Four complainants challenged Presbytery’s BCO 31-2 investigation and its finding of no strong presumption of guilt, citing delayed action and failure to interview accusers. The SJC denied the complaints: BCO 31-2 requires due diligence but grants great discretion and prescribes neither interviews nor a timetable, and the record showed no clear constitutional error overcoming deference to Presbytery. Lyons and Woodward also lacked standing because they were not under Presbytery’s jurisdiction. No renewed investigation or process was ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) |
| [2009-08](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) | Linton v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Denied; Affirmed | An elder challenged Presbytery’s failure to interview accusers, alleged investigative delay, and resulting finding of no strong presumption of a pastor’s guilt. The SJC denied the complaint. BCO 31-2 requires due diligence but prescribes no timeline or interview procedure, giving courts substantial discretion before formal process begins. The ordinary stated-meeting sequence used here disclosed no clear constitutional error. The SJC therefore deferred to Presbytery’s superior proximity to the facts and upheld its finding. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) |
| [2009-09](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) | Lyons v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Partially sustained; Denied | Lyons challenged Presbytery’s investigation of accusations against a teaching elder, alleging failure to interview accusers, undue delay, and an erroneous finding of no strong presumption of guilt. The SJC denied the complaint and additionally held that Lyons lacked standing because he was not under Presbytery’s jurisdiction. The consolidated opinion explained that BCO 31-2 requires due diligence but prescribes neither interviews nor a timeline; finding no constitutional or clear factual error, it deferred to Presbytery’s judgment. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) |
| [2009-10](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) | Woodward v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Denied | Robert Woodward challenged Presbytery’s acceptance of a finding that accusations against TE Craig Bulkeley did not raise a strong presumption of guilt, alleging investigators never heard his evidence. The SJC denied the complaint, specifically holding that Woodward lacked standing because he was not under Presbytery’s jurisdiction. It also held that BCO 31-2 prescribes no interview requirement or fixed timeline and found no clear constitutional error warranting departure from ordinary deference to Presbytery. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-05_2009-08_2009-09_2009-10.md) |
| [2009-06](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-06.md) | Bordwine v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed; Remanded | Pacific Northwest Presbytery adopted a report finding TE Peter Leithart's views not out of accord with the fundamentals of the system of doctrine. The SJC sustained a complaint, holding PNW erred because, without formal judicial process, it lacked authority to declare either that the views were out of accord or that they were not. The record still suggested a strong presumption of guilt. The SJC refused complainants' affirmative declaration and remanded with directions for pastoral counsel under BCO 31-7 and possible BCO 31-2 compliance. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-06.md) |
| [2009-07](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-07.md) | Urish v. Rocky Mountain Presbytery | Denied; Affirmed | The SJC denied James Urish and others' complaint against Rocky Mountain Presbytery for sustaining Dan Breed's ordination exam. Breed had said women could teach Scripture to mixed adults in settings like Sunday school under session oversight, but not preach, hold elder office, or teach outside session authority. Because the Westminster Standards and BCO did not expressly resolve that view, the SJC found no clear error under BCO 39 and affirmed Presbytery's denial. A concurrence criticized Breed's exegesis of 1 Tim. 2:12 but stressed the SJC was not endorsing it. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-07.md) |
| [2009-11](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-11.md) | Larry Edison et al. v. Southwest Florida Presbytery | Partially sustained | Ministers complained that Southwest Florida Presbytery improperly reexamined Bryan Gregory and reversed his examination, membership, and call after previously receiving him. The SJC upheld Presbytery’s authority to hear and sustain the earlier complaint but sustained the present complaint on the remedial actions. Sustaining a complaint did not itself undo prior acts; once Presbytery had treated Gregory as a member, his membership rights could be removed only through disciplinary due process. The SJC annulled the reexamination and March 24 actions and directed Presbytery to consider appropriate remedies. Gregory’s theology was not before it. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__2009-11.md) |
| [2009-13](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-13.md) | Johnson v. Southwest Florida | Administratively out of order | An unspecified complaint against Southwest Florida Presbytery was found administratively out of order. The published record does not identify the challenged action or explain the administrative defect, and no separate merits opinion, relief, or further proceedings are reported. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-13.md) |

The header's contrary description of the disposition as administratively out of order appears erroneous. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-15.md) |

| [2009-17](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-17.md) | Michael McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery | Administratively out of order | The SJC reported that Michael McNeil’s complaint against Chesapeake Presbytery was found administratively out of order. The published notice does not identify the challenged action or the reason for that ruling, and reports no merits determination, additional relief, or further proceedings for this docket. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-17.md) |
| [2009-27](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-27.md) | Cutler v. Platte Valley | Administratively out of order | A complaint against Platte Valley Presbytery was ruled administratively out of order by the Standing Judicial Commission at the 38th Assembly (2010), 38-31, p. 129. The published record is only a stub: it gives no merits opinion, no allegations, and no rationale for the administrative ruling, and it states no further proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-27.md) |
| [2009-29](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-29.md) | McNeil v. Chesapeake | Administratively out of order | McNeil v. Chesapeake, Case 2009-29, appears in the 38th General Assembly (2010) record as one of several 2009-2010 cases found administratively out of order; no separate SJC merits opinion was published. The available stub supplies no issue description, procedural-vehicle statement, or reasons for the administrative disposition, so it supports only a status synopsis, not the underlying dispute or ruling rationale. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-29.md) |
| [2009-32](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-32.md) | Warren v. Chesapeake | Administratively out of order | Russell Warren’s complaint against Chesapeake Presbytery was found administratively out of order by the Standing Judicial Commission at the 38th Assembly. The supplied source gives no merits opinion, no issue-level ruling, and no adopted reason for the administrative disposition; it leaves the underlying complaint and any relief unaddressed. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2009-32.md) |
| [2010-02](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2010-02.md) | Cutler v. Platte Valley | Administratively out of order | The 38th General Assembly recorded Cutler v. Platte Valley as administratively out of order. The published stub identifies neither the challenged action nor the circumstances supporting that ruling and contains no separate merits opinion. It therefore supports only the administrative disposition, not a merits determination or case-specific rule. | [full text](../cases/ga38_2010__stub_2010-02.md) |
| [2010-05](../cases/ga38_2010__2010-05_2010-06.md) | Dale Cutler v. Platte Valley Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | Dale Cutler filed a complaint against Platte Valley Presbytery. The SJC found it administratively out of order, but the sparse report does not identify the challenged action, explain the procedural defect, or provide further relief. ## 39th General Assembly (2011)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2009-12](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-12_2009-21.md) | Armes v. Southwest Florida Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | Presbytery refused to hear Armes’s complaint against his excommunication, initially reasoning that excommunication deprived him of standing and later ruling the complaint untimely. Presbytery conceded its error on the standing ground, and all parties stipulated to remand for a hearing. The SJC confirmed that stipulation, sustained the complaint, and remanded the case without deciding the validity of the excommunication itself. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-12_2009-21.md) |
| [2009-21](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-12_2009-21.md) | Armes v. Southwest Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Remanded | Armes challenged Presbytery's refusal to hear his second complaint about Session's trespass notice and threat of arrest, which Presbytery treated as duplicating his pending excommunication complaint. The SJC sustained the complaint because the trespass action occurred later and was distinct, although the matters could be heard together. It remanded for Presbytery or a commission to hear the complaint under BCO 43-8. The SJC did not decide whether Session could issue such a notice; review remained subject to timely filing and standing. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-12_2009-21.md) |
| [2009-22](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-22.md) | McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery | Denied | The SJC denied a censured husband's complaint against Chesapeake Presbytery's approval of a judicial commission report, which had upheld his session's refusal to receive his charges against his wife after she filed for civil divorce. Applying BCO 39-3.2–3.3, the SJC deferred to the lower courts' factual and discretionary findings absent clear error. It found no clear procedural error, upheld the conclusion that the wife did not sin by seeking civil protection, and invoked BCO 31-8's caution about accusations from one under censure. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-22.md) |
| [2009-23](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-23.md) | Koerkenmeier v. Illiana Presbytery | Sustained | Illiana Presbytery appointed a commission with broad power 'to deal with matters' at Center Grove Presbyterian Church after a pastor requested inquiry into alleged evils. The SJC sustained the complaint but held the error harmless: the broad charge could take original jurisdiction from the session contrary to BCO 11-3 & 4 and 33-1, while a limited investigative committee under BCO 13-9(f) and 31-2 would have been proper. Because the commission never exercised that power and the session endorsed its findings, no remedy, including invalidation of the adopted report, was ordered. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-23.md) |
| [2009-24](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-24.md) | Phelps v. Pacific Presbytery | Denied | Phelps complained that Pacific Presbytery erred by finding no constitutional irregularities in the New Life Burbank Session's ordination of Lee Irons as a ruling elder, despite PP's earlier refusal to receive Irons as a teaching-elder candidate. The SJC denied the complaint. Under BCO 39-3.3, the Court owed great deference to the lower court's discretionary fitness judgment; the single office of elder does not require identical eligibility processes, and the NLB Session had jurisdiction after Irons renounced OPC jurisdiction. PP's action stood; the SJC declined to decide the NAPARC issue. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-24.md) |
| [2009-25](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-25_2009-26.md) | Brown v. Northern California Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled | Brown challenged Presbytery’s abstract approval of six views concerning diaconal practice. The SJC sustained the consolidated complaints and annulled Presbytery’s action because a church court cannot issue a binding decree apart from properly exercising jurisdiction over specific persons. Constitutional conformity must instead be determined through authorized examination or judicial processes. The judgment deliberately left unresolved whether any of the six views themselves were faithful to the Constitution; the detailed view-by-view analysis appeared only in a concurring opinion. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-25_2009-26.md) |
| [2009-26](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-25_2009-26.md) | Session of Grace Presbyterian Church v. Northern California Presbytery | sustained | Joined with Case 2009-25; the Session of Grace Presbyterian Church complained that views 3, 4, 5, and 6 in Northern California Presbytery's resolution on diaconal practice violated the BCO. The SJC sustained the complaint on the same grounds as 2009-25, annulling the abstract resolution while declining to rule on the… | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-25_2009-26.md) |
| [2009-28](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-28.md) | Ruff v. Nashville Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | Nashville Presbytery's BCO 31-2 investigation of reports against TE George Grant was inadequately documented and internally inconsistent, and the evidence before Presbytery raised a strong presumption of guilt, the SJC held. It sustained the complaint, ruling that pastoral reconciliation does not displace the duty to institute judicial process, and remanded for further proceedings; it did not adjudicate Grant guilty. A concurrence agreed only in remanding to supplement the record under BCO 42-5 & 7. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2009-28.md) |
| [2010-03](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-03.md) | McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | An appellant under suspension from the sacraments challenged the presbytery's judicial commission process and censure. The SJC held the appeal judicially out of order—not decided on the merits—because he had not submitted to a regular trial, as BCO 42-2 requires. Treated as a complaint, it was also out of order: it had not first been presented to the presbytery, and the 30-day period had expired. The SJC further barred complaints about later court actions while he remained under censure, except as to the highest censure. The censure stood. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-03.md) |
| [2010-04](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-04.md) | Sartorious et al. v. Siouxlands Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | A complaint challenged Siouxlands Presbytery's investigation of allegations that TE Joshua Moon held heterodox Federal Vision views and its finding of no strong presumption of guilt. The SJC held that Presbytery had investigated diligently under BCO 31-2 and, deferring under BCO 39-3 to its credibility judgment, found no clear error. It declined to decide whether the overture should have been treated as charges because that issue was not raised below, while leaving future charges possible. A concurrence favored charges; the dissent would have found error and remanded. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-04.md) |
| [2010-07](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-07.md) | Sang C. Choi v. Korean Central Presbytery | Administratively out of order | Mr. Sang Chui Choi and others brought a case against Korean Central Presbytery. The Standing Judicial Commission, in the 39th General Assembly (2011), found 2010-07 administratively out of order, listing it among seven cases (2009-29, 2009-30, 2009-31, 2010-06, 2010-07, 2010-17, 2010-25) disposed of on that ground. No separate merits opinion was published, and the adopted record gives no reason for the ruling or any underlying facts. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-07.md) |
| [2010-08](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-08.md) | McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery | Judicially out of order | The SJC report lists Case 2010-08, Mr. Michael A. McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery, among cases found judicially out of order. No separate merits opinion was published, and the available report does not identify the challenged action, procedural vehicle, or reasons. The only usable result is the judicial out-of-order classification; no merits determination or relief is stated. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-08.md) |
| [2010-14](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-14.md) | Michael A. McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery | Judicially out of order | The SJC reported McNeil’s complaint against Chesapeake Presbytery as judicially out of order. The published status notice supplies no explanation for that ruling or substantive account of the dispute. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-14.md) |
| [2010-15](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-15.md) | Case 2010-15: was withdrawn. The Standing Judicial Commission has completed its work on 2009-12; 2009-21; 2009-22; 2009-23; 2009-24; 2009-25; 2009-26; 2009-28; 2010-03; 2010-04; 2010-16; 2010-18; 2010-19; 2010-20; | out_of_order |  | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-15.md) |
| [2010-16](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-16.md) | Lyons v. Western Carolina Presbytery | Denied | Kirk Lyons filed BCO 32-2 charges against TE Craig Bulkeley with Western Carolina Presbytery. WCP dismissed the charges, then ruled Lyons's complaint administratively out of order for lack of standing. The SJC answered that the dismissal was not erroneous—Presbytery had discretion under BCO 31-8, 34-2, and 32-20, and the record showed no clear error—but that WCP erred on standing because Lyons's filing brought him under its jurisdiction. Because that error was not prejudicial, the SJC denied the complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-16.md) |
| [2010-17](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-17.md) | David M. Sarafolean Request to Cite Siouxlands Presbytery | Administratively out of order | TE David M. Sarafolean requested that the General Assembly and SJC cite Siouxlands Presbytery under BCO 40-4 and 40-5. The SJC report records the request as administratively out of order without explaining the defect or deciding the underlying allegations. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__stub_2010-17.md) |
| [2010-18](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) | PCA v. Gulfstream | No final disposition in available record | The SJC considered whether Gulfstream Presbytery had adequately answered a BCO 40-5 citation seeking a more acceptable response to exceptions of substance in its records. It found Gulfstream’s response acceptable, but only pending certification to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records that the Presbytery had approved it. The decision does not describe the underlying exceptions or give further reasons for finding the response acceptable. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) |
| [2010-19](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) | PCA v. Korean Central | No final disposition in available record | Cited under BCO 40-5 to provide a more acceptable response to exceptions of substance in its records, Korean Central Presbytery submitted a response that the SJC accepted, pending certification to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records that Presbytery had approved it. The ruling accepted the response conditionally; it did not substantively approve the underlying records. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) |
| [2010-20](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) | PCA v. Korean Northwest | Other; Referred | Cited under BCO 40-5 for exceptions of substance and failure to submit minutes, Korean Northwest Presbytery provided an acceptable response and submitted its minutes. Acceptance of the substantive response remained contingent on certification that Presbytery had approved it. The minutes were forwarded to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records, so the ruling established compliance with the citation rather than final approval of the minutes themselves. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) |
| [2010-21](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) | PCA v. Korean Southeast | No final disposition in available record | Korean Southeastern Presbytery was cited under BCO 40-5 for failing to submit its Minutes. The SJC found that Presbytery had since submitted them and directed that they be forwarded to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records. The decision established compliance with the submission requirement; it did not report substantive exceptions or approve the Minutes on review. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) |
| [2010-22](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) | PCA v. Korean Southeast | No final disposition in available record | Cited under BCO 40-5 for failing to submit its minutes, Korean Southwest Presbytery responded and supplied them. The SJC found that the minutes had been submitted and directed that they be forwarded to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records. The decision resolved the submission deficiency but left review of the minutes to that committee. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) |
| [2010-23](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) | PCA v. Pacific | Other | Pacific Presbytery was cited under BCO 40-5 to provide a more acceptable response to exceptions of substance found during records review. The SJC accepted its response, conditioned on certification to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records that Presbytery had approved it. The decision gives no further substantive rationale and, unlike rulings concerning three other presbyteries considered collectively, does not direct that Pacific’s minutes be forwarded. | [full text](../cases/ga39_2011__2010-18_2010-19_2010-20_2010-21_2010-22_2010-23.md) |
