## 40th General Assembly (2012)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2010-01](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-01.md) | McNeil v. Chesapeake Presbytery | Dismissed | Michael McNeil’s complaint against Chesapeake Presbytery was dismissed without a merits ruling after McNeil failed to appear at the SJC hearing. The panel called his name outside the hearing room and the Stated Clerk’s office; he was absent and had filed no brief. The notice had warned under BCO 42-11 that an appellant’s failure to appear would be treated as abandonment. The SJC declared the case abandoned and dismissed it. This illustrates dismissal for nonappearance, not adjudication of the underlying complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-01.md) |
| [2010-24](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-24.md) | Wood v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | A complaint challenging Northwest Georgia Presbytery’s denial of Laura Wood’s complaint against a session was declared judicially out of order and dismissed because Presbytery later rescinded the challenged action; the SJC said the defects could not be cured and no grounds remained. It reached no merits. The dissent argued rescission did not automatically defeat a complaint and that her complaint also alleged Presbytery’s failure to act. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-24.md) |
| [2010-26](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-26.md) | Lee v. Korean Eastern Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | Korean Eastern Presbytery dismissed TE Eliot Lee's charges against two teaching elders without trial, and the SJC sustained Lee's complaint that this was unconstitutional. Although a presbytery may dismiss charges under BCO 29-1, 32-20, 34-2, or 31-8, it bears the burden of documenting that basis; KEP did not. The SJC refused Lee's request that it enter the guilt verdicts the presbytery should have reached—that power exists on appeal, not complaint. It remanded for KEP to begin process under BCO 32. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-26.md) |
| [2010-27](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-27.md) | Ruff v. Nashville Presbytery | Dismissed | The SJC dismissed Matt Ruff's complaint against Nashville Presbytery as judicially out of order under OMSJC 10.5.a. Ruff challenged Presbytery's failure to investigate a teaching elder, but BCO 43 requires a complaint against an act or decision; lack of action qualifies only when a court meeting has occurred and the court failed to act. Presbytery's nearest antecedent meeting was August 18, 2010, and Ruff filed October 27, 2010, beyond BCO 43-2's thirty-day limit. Because the defect could not be cured, the case was dismissed without reaching the merits; a separate objection disputed that ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-27.md) |
| [2010-28](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-28.md) | Gonzales v. Great Lakes Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | Gonzales challenged Great Lakes Presbytery’s approval of recommendations following a BCO 31-2 investigation that found no basis for charges. The SJC sustained the complaint only as to recommendation five—which threatened adverse consequences for his recommendation or transfer if he rejected pastoral advice—and annulled its approval. Presbytery could offer pastoral oversight and counsel, including the other recommendations, but without judicial process that counsel could not expressly or implicitly diminish his good standing. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2010-28.md) |
| [2011-01](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-01.md) | Choi v. Korean Central Presbytery | Administratively out of order | A complaint challenged the nonacceptance of charges filed with Korean Central Presbytery. The record was unclear whether Presbytery or only its clerk had refused them, and did not show that Presbytery had denied a complaint concerning that refusal. The SJC therefore held the complaint administratively out of order as premature under BCO 43-2, without reaching the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-01.md) |
| [2011-02](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-02.md) | Gonzales v. Great Lakes Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC denied a minister's complaint challenging Presbytery's out-of-order dismissal of his request for a BCO 31-2 investigation of another minister. Although the SJC agreed Presbytery wrongly said it had already decided all issues—the earlier complaint (SJC 2010-28) was distinct—it upheld the refusal to investigate. The accused minister had delivered an authorized committee report as chairman; absent evidence he exceeded that authorization, his statements were the committee's, not personal, and BCO 31-2 did not require investigation. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-02.md) |
| [2011-03](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-03_2011-04.md) | Sagan et al. v. Covenant Presbytery | Denied | Complainants challenged Covenant Presbytery’s receipt and adoption of recommendations from its MNA Committee, alleging that the committee exceeded its assignment to investigate campus-minister salaries. The SJC denied both consolidated complaints. It held that examining Presbytery’s relationship with MJCCW naturally extended the salary inquiry, fell within the committee’s oversight responsibility, and proposed discussion rather than structural change. Presbytery had also ruled the matters properly before it and rejected an appeal. The SJC could not independently verify assertions about the committee’s traditional oversight. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-03_2011-04.md) |
| [2011-04](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-03_2011-04.md) | Gunn et al. v. Covenant Presbytery | Denied | Ministers challenged Covenant Presbytery’s receipt of and action on its MNA Committee report concerning the Mississippi Joint Committee on Campus Work, contending that the committee exceeded its assigned authority. The SJC denied the complaint. It held that the study naturally extended the salary inquiry, the committee had oversight of MJCCW, the recommendations sought discussion rather than structural change, and Presbytery had ruled the matters properly before it. The record could not establish claimed traditional limits on oversight, and no corrective relief issued. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-03_2011-04.md) |
| [2011-05](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-05.md) | Kim v. Korean Capital Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held Young Bae Kim's complaint against Korean Capital Presbytery administratively out of order because it was not filed with the Stated Clerk within 30 days after the presbytery's October 4, 2010 meeting, as BCO 43 requires. The adopted ruling reached no merits. The roll call adopted 18 concurring, 1 disqualified, and 5 absent; the disqualified commissioner could not certify that he had read the necessary record portions. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-05.md) |
| [2011-07](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-07.md) | Presbyterian Church in America v. Warrior Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The 39th General Assembly cited Warrior Presbytery under BCO 40 provisions for failing to answer exceptions of substance in several meeting minutes. The SJC recorded that the Presbytery had since submitted responses approved by the Presbytery and found them acceptable. The adopted ruling provides no further rationale or separate relief. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-07.md) |
| [2011-08](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-08.md) | Sherfey v. James River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order | Sherfey challenged James River Presbytery’s denial of his complaint and alleged errors in its commission’s proceedings. The SJC ruled the complaint judicially out of order with incurable defects: several grounds had not first been adjudicated by Presbytery as BCO 43-2 required, and the General Assembly filing missed BCO 43-3’s thirty-day deadline. Incorrect advice from Presbytery’s clerk did not excuse compliance. The adopted decision neither reached the merits nor ordered a remand; only a concurrence proposed returning the matter to Presbytery. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-08.md) |
| [2011-09](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-09.md) | Jennings v. North Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Annulled; Remanded | North Florida Presbytery restored a minister deposed for adultery by a 19–17 vote. The SJC sustained the complaint, holding that Presbytery had not proceeded with the required caution and that a bare majority could not demonstrate BCO 34-8’s required strongly favorable general sentiment. It declined to require reordination, calling that question unsettled and treating BCO 37-5’s restoration pronouncement as sufficient pending clarification. The SJC annulled Presbytery’s actions concerning the minister and remanded for a proper probationary and review process. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-09.md) |
| [2011-10](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-10.md) | Testa v. South Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held Frank Testa's complaint against South Florida Presbytery administratively out of order because the presbytery had not yet acted on the underlying matter, so no merits decision was reached. The case thus turns on prematurity rather than the complaint's substance; the sparse record does not describe the underlying dispute. The adopted vote was 19 concurring, 1 not qualified, and 4 absent. RE Donohoe was not qualified because he could not certify that he had read the necessary portions of the Record (OMSJC 2.3.b). | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-10.md) |
| [2011-13](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-13.md) | Spann v. Oak Mountain Presbyterian Church  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | An individual appealed directly to the SJC, alleging her church effectively excommunicated her without process, failed the Matthew 18 process, and spiritually abused her by social isolation. The SJC dismissed as premature, without reaching the merits, because there was no record of a judicial decision by the church or of an appeal to Evangel Presbytery, the next higher court; it stated she could proceed only by providing such evidence. The severity of excommunication did not excuse that prerequisite. The SJC asked Presbytery to obtain the session's written response to her letter. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__2011-13.md) |
| [2011-19](../cases/ga40_2012__stub_2011-19.md) | Testa v. Southern Florida | Administratively out of order | Mr. Frank Testa's case against Southern Florida Presbytery was found administratively out of order by the 40th General Assembly, a procedural disposition rather than a merits ruling. No separate merits opinion was published, and the source does not identify the challenged action, grounds, or rationale. It lists 2011-19 among cases found administratively out of order, while another case was found judicially out of order. | [full text](../cases/ga40_2012__stub_2011-19.md) |
| [2011-19](../cases/ga40_2012__stub_2011-19.md) | Testa v. Southern Florida | Administratively out of order | Mr. Frank Testa's case against Southern Florida Presbytery was found administratively out of order by the 40th General Assembly, a procedural disposition rather than a merits ruling. No separate merits opinion was published, and the source does not identify the challenged action, grounds, or rationale. It lists 2011-19 among cases found administratively out of order, while another case was found judicially out of order. | [disposition](../cases/ga40_2012__stub_2011-19.md) |

## 41st General Assembly (2013)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2011-06](../cases/ga41_2013__2011-06.md) | Sawyers et al. v. Missouri Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Moot | Elders complained that Missouri Presbytery erred in its BCO 31-2 investigation by finding insufficient evidence to raise a strong presumption of guilt that TE Jeffrey Meyers held Federal Vision views contrary to the Westminster Standards. The SJC answered yes: MOP erred under BCO 34-5, and the ordinary remedy was compliance with BCO 31-2. But because MOP had already tried Meyers on April 13–14, 2012, the SJC held further action moot, ordering no additional relief. Adopted by the SJC as a whole, with one dissent. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2011-06.md) |
| [2011-17](../cases/ga41_2013__2011-17.md) | Smith v. Mississippi Valley Presbytery | Partially sustained; Not sustained | Ministers challenged Jeremy Smith's excommunication, but not his deposition. The SJC rejected four allegations and most of the fifth, holding among other things that two ignored citations are only one possible form of contumacy. It sustained the complaint solely because Presbytery wrongly treated Smith's absence as a refusal to appear: a committee email was not a citation by Presbytery. The SJC reversed that finding but did not annul the excommunication, leaving Presbytery to consider whether modification was necessary. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2011-17.md) |
| [2011-18](../cases/ga41_2013__2011-18.md) | Ruff v. Nashville Presbytery | Sustained | Ruff challenged Nashville Presbytery’s investigation and its resolution of allegations against a minister through confession and admonition without formal process. The SJC sustained the complaint only on the confession and administration of censure: the investigation was adequate and BCO 38-1 was available, but the abstract confession did not acknowledge particular sins against particular people. Presbytery had to amend it, sponsor another reconciliation meeting, document satisfaction with the proceedings, or pursue the matter further, potentially through indictment. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2011-18.md) |
| [2012-01](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-01.md) | Sherfey v. James River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | The SJC dismissed a complaint against James River Presbytery without reaching the merits, holding it judicially out of order because the action complained against was not in the Record of the Case (BCO 43-1; OMSJC 10.6). The adopted ruling gives no further rationale and carried 13 concurring, 5 dissenting, 1 abstaining, 1 disqualified, and 4 absent. A dissent argued the record did contain an adjudicable Session action and that the cited OMSJC provision did not list the ground used; a separate written objection challenged a panel member's participation. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-01.md) |
| [2012-02](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-02.md) | Keating v. Warrior Presbytery | Sustained | Keating challenged Warrior Presbytery’s decision to divest him of ordination without censure while he was absent. The SJC sustained his appeal, reversed and voided the divestiture, and remanded because BCO 34-10’s required opportunity to be heard in his defense was not provided; this also amounted to hurrying before all testimony was taken. It rejected the reasonable-indulgence claim because Presbytery did not know of his postponement request. Keating remained a minister in good standing, but Presbytery could reconsider divestiture after reasonably soliciting a defense. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-02.md) |
| [2012-04](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-04.md) | Dunn v. Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held TE Dwight Dunn's complaint administratively out of order under OMSJC 9.1 and directed Presbytery to remedy the deficiencies by its next Stated Meeting before further action under OMSJC 9.2. The original complaint had been transmitted to the Faith Church Commission by the Stated Clerk, not committed by Presbytery motion as BCO 15-3 requires, and after the Commission denied it, the Commission did not seek Presbytery approval of its judgment. Because Presbytery had not acted, the SJC complaint was premature; no merits were reached. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-04.md) |
| [2012-05](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-05.md) | Hedman v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | The SJC denied RE Gerald Hedman's complaint against Pacific Northwest Presbytery, which had adopted five not-guilty judgments on charges that TE Peter Leithart's baptism, covenant, imputation, justification, and union-with-Christ views contradicted the Westminster Standards. Bound to the record and clear-error review, the SJC found no procedural violation and insufficient evidence that Presbytery clearly erred. It left the verdicts undisturbed, declined to endorse Leithart's views generally, and urged continued clarification of his non-standard terminology. A separate dissent would have found error and ordered a new trial or reference. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-05.md) |
| [2012-06](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-06.md) | Bethel v. Southeast Alabama Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held Complaint 2012-06 administratively out of order because it was not filed within thirty days (BCO 43-2; OMSJC 18.10.b) and because the complainant, a Deacon who was not a commissioner to Presbytery at the time of the challenged action, lacked standing (BCO 43-1). No merits review appears; the adopted vote was 18 concurring, 2 not qualified, and 4 absent. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-06.md) |
| [2012-09](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-09.md) | Bennett v. Missouri Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held TE M. Jay Bennett’s complaint against Missouri Presbytery administratively out of order for lack of standing. Bennett was a PCA member when he complained to Presbytery on April 16, 2012, but was received by the OPC on April 28, 2012; when he filed with the SJC on August 16, 2012, he was no longer a PCA member. The SJC reached no merits issue. The vote was 15 concurring, 2 dissenting, 1 disqualified, 1 not qualified, 1 recused, and 4 absent; no dissent rationale is stated. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-09.md) |
| [2012-10](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-10.md) | PCA v. Korean Capital Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The 40th General Assembly cited Korean Capital Presbytery for failing to respond to substantive exceptions in its October 6, 2008 minutes. The SJC noted that the Presbytery had submitted responses approved by the Presbytery under BCO 10-4 and found those responses acceptable. The record does not state a dismissal, acquittal, or other formal merits disposition; the matter is an administrative BCO 40-5 citation resolved by acceptance of the Presbytery's responses. | [full text](../cases/ga41_2013__2012-10.md) |
| 2012-08 | Case 2012-08: is with the whole SJC (at the time of writing this report); and 55 | out_of_order |  | _decided at 43rd GA_ · [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2012-08.md) |

## 42nd General Assembly (2014)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2011-11](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) | Hahn v. Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery (Cases 2011-11, 2011-12, 2011-15, 2011-16) | Denied; Affirmed | Hahn challenged Presbytery’s ruling that CTKPC Session lawfully excluded him from that congregation’s worship until a psychiatric evaluation addressed safety concerns. The SJC denied the complaint. Applying BCO 39-3.3, it found no clear error in the lower courts’ credibility and discretionary judgments, given Session’s familiarity with the people and events. It characterized the restriction as informal pastoral safety guidance, not judicial discipline: it did not bar sacraments, worship elsewhere, or transfer, and attendance could resume upon medical assurance. The conditional local restriction therefore remained undisturbed. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) |
| [2011-12](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) | Hahn v. Philadelphia Metro West. (also Cases 2011-11 and 2011-15) | Denied; Affirmed | Hahn appealed Presbytery’s guilty judgment and indefinite suspension from the sacraments, alleging trial irregularities, denial of a requested amendment, haste, and prejudice. The SJC denied the appeal on all three charges: false and unsubstantiated accusations, disobedience and disrespect toward church authorities, and a bitter and malignant spirit. Applying BCO 39-3.3, it deferred to Presbytery’s familiarity with the parties, witnesses, and extensive trial record and found no clear error, even though contrary evidence may have existed. The judgment and censure therefore remained undisturbed. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) |
| [2011-15](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) | Case 2011-15: Hahn v. Philadelphia Metro West in M42GA, 2014 Houston GA. 586 | Denied; Affirmed | Hahn challenged Presbytery’s refusal to institute process against three church members he had accused. The SJC denied his complaint because Presbytery found that BCO 31-8 applied to Hahn’s conduct and that the evidence did not create a strong presumption of guilt. Deferring to Presbytery absent clear error, the SJC left its refusal to appoint a prosecutor and commence process undisturbed. A concurrence agreed with the result but disputed the majority’s suggestion that filing charges generally requires process. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) |
| [2011-16](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) | Steven Hahn v. Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery (duplicate of Case 2011-15) | Denied | Hahn challenged Presbytery’s refusal to appoint a prosecutor and institute process against Lisa Ridenour, RE Glen Ridenour, and TE Eric Huber. The SJC denied the complaint because Presbytery found BCO 31-8 applicable to Hahn’s accusations and found insufficient evidence of a strong presumption of guilt; the record showed no clear error. A separate concurrence agreed with the result but cautioned against implying that filing charges generally compels process, emphasizing a church court’s broader discretion to investigate and decline prosecution. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-11_2011-12_2011-15_2011-16.md) |
| [2011-14](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-14.md) | Reese and Bech v. Philadelphia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | Philadelphia Presbytery declared Reese and Bech's complaint out of order because some supporting reasons and requested amends allegedly violated the BCO. The SJC sustained the complaint, holding Presbytery misapplied Robert's Rules: objectionable parts do not justify rejecting an entire complaint, and a court need not adopt requested amends. It also said pertinent questions about whether a call is for the good of the Church may be asked, but agreed the amends—including removing TE Hsu—were inappropriate and could not rescind his installation. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2011-14.md) |
| [2012-03](../cases/ga42_2014__2012-03.md) | Charles Tarter v. Evangel Presbytery | Sustained; Vacated; Remanded | A teaching elder appealed his judgment and deposition for refusing Presbytery committee directives and allegedly avoiding accountability. The SJC sustained five of nine specifications, holding that the committee could not impose requirements lacking constitutional warrant or control an independent mission board; vague allegations and testimony unavailable for cross-examination also impaired the trial. Because the record did not prove an ongoing pattern of insubordination, it vacated the censure and remanded for a constitutionally compliant proceeding or dismissal. Three specifications failed, while one duplicated another. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2012-03.md) |
| [2012-07](../cases/ga42_2014__2012-07.md) | William G. Mitchell v. The Presbytery of the Ascension | Partially sustained; Vacated | A ruling elder appealed judgments concerning an alleged broken promise to take a sabbatical and deficient performance as Session clerk. Because the pivotal email was absent from the record, the SJC vacated the sabbatical-related judgment and remanded for Presbytery only to consider whether a new trial was warranted. It reversed and dismissed the clerk-duty specification because the four-month delay in submitting minutes was attributable to the Session and did not justify a trial. Only the first specification remained potentially triable. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2012-07.md) |
| [2013-01](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-01.md) | Dunn and Pesnell v. Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery | Not sustained | The complaint challenged Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery’s November 17, 2012 vote adopting its Complaint Commission’s recommendation to institute process and try TE David Swavely. The SJC did not sustain it. Although the record showed possible procedural irregularities—including unauthorized circulation of an earlier complaint—the SJC found no reversible error: presbyteries receive deference on factual records and broad latitude to judge the sufficiency of investigations. The recommendation to proceed to trial stands, subject to later parliamentary action. The SJC did not decide Swavely’s guilt. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-01.md) |
| [2013-02](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-02.md) | Jackson v. Northwest Georgia | Administratively out of order | The 42nd General Assembly (2014) found Case 2013-02, a complaint by RE Warren Jackson against Northwest Georgia Presbytery, Administratively Out of Order. No merits opinion was published, and the surviving record gives no facts, grounds, adopted reasoning, or relief. The same disposition was entered in companion Case 2013-05, while Case 2013-09 was withdrawn as prematurely filed. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-02.md) |
| [2013-04](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-04.md) | the Session of Hope Community Church v. Central Carolina Presbytery | Sustained | Hope Community Church’s Session challenged Presbytery’s requirement that a multi-site congregation eventually particularize its second worship site. The SJC sustained the complaint, annulled the requirement, and struck it from the Presbytery Manual because BCO 12-5e specifically entrusts the time and place of worship to the Session, which Presbytery’s general church-enlargement authority could not supersede. The narrow ruling did not resolve broader concerns about multi-site churches or eliminate Presbytery’s review of a second site’s location under BCO 13-9(g). | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-04.md) |
| [2013-05](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-05.md) | Steven Hahn v. Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery | Administratively out of order | Mr. Stephen Hahn asked the Standing Judicial Commission to assume original jurisdiction over Philadelphia Metro West Presbytery. The SJC found the request administratively out of order and so did not reach a merits review. The report states no reason for the ruling and identifies no underlying dispute, leaving only the disposition itself. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-05.md) |
| [2013-07](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-07.md) | Session of First Presbyterian Church North Port v. Presbytery of Southwest Florida | Sustained; Remanded | The SJC sustained the Session's complaint against Presbytery. Presbytery had ruled a member's original complaint administratively out of order as untimely but then remanded the matter to the Session; the SJC held that once out of order, no complaint remained to remand. It also held Presbytery could not direct or instruct the Session to initiate formal process without BCO 40-5's required citation step, so those directions were null and void. The member remained under the Session's jurisdiction; the civil trespass order was not ecclesiastical discipline and did not alter membership. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-07.md) |
| [2013-09](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-09.md) | Marshall v. Pacific | Withdrawn | The 42nd Assembly (2014) disposed of Mr. G. Rick Marshall's appeal against Pacific Presbytery by withdrawal. No separate merits opinion was published, so the source does not disclose the challenged conduct, adopted reasoning, or relief beyond the withdrawal. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-09.md) |
| [2013-11](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-11.md) | Session of First Presbyterian Church North Port, Florida v. Presbytery of Southwest Florida  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | A Session appealed Presbytery’s judgment finding it guilty and suspending it for refusing instructions to institute process against a member. The SJC sustained the appeal, reversed the judgment in whole, and dismissed the charges. Once Presbytery denied the member’s complaint, that matter should have ended; moreover, Presbytery did not follow BCO 40-4’s required first step before invoking BCO 40-5 review and control. The delay, prejudice, and missing-witness specifications were moot. The decision did not fault Presbytery’s underlying concern. | [full text](../cases/ga42_2014__2013-11.md) |
| [2013-02](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-02.md) | Jackson v. Northwest Georgia | Administratively out of order | The 42nd General Assembly (2014) found Case 2013-02, a complaint by RE Warren Jackson against Northwest Georgia Presbytery, Administratively Out of Order. No merits opinion was published, and the surviving record gives no facts, grounds, adopted reasoning, or relief. The same disposition was entered in companion Case 2013-05, while Case 2013-09 was withdrawn as prematurely filed. | [disposition](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-02.md) |
| [2013-09](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-09.md) | Marshall v. Pacific | Withdrawn | The 42nd Assembly (2014) disposed of Mr. G. Rick Marshall's appeal against Pacific Presbytery by withdrawal. No separate merits opinion was published, so the source does not disclose the challenged conduct, adopted reasoning, or relief beyond the withdrawal. | [disposition](../cases/ga42_2014__stub_2013-09.md) |

## 43rd General Assembly (2015)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2012-08](../cases/ga43_2015__2012-08.md) | Art Sartorius et al. v. Siouxlands Presbytery | Partially sustained; Remanded | Teaching and ruling elders challenged Presbytery’s approval of TE Gregory Lawrence’s acquittal on five doctrinal charges. The SJC sustained the complaint on procedural grounds: the trial commission relied on his post-indictment exculpatory writings while he declined to testify, depriving the prosecution of cross-examination. The SJC ordered a new trial on the same indictment under specified evidentiary procedures. Because this was a complaint rather than an appeal, it did not reverse the acquittals into findings of guilt or decide the doctrinal merits. | [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2012-08.md) |
| [2013-03](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-03.md) | Marshall v. Pacific Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order | Marshall complained that Pacific Presbytery wrongly refused to dismiss a judicial case despite alleged procedural irregularities in the indictment and process. The SJC held the complaint judicially out of order: objections arising in a judicial case were to be preserved for appeal under BCO 32-14 and 42-3, and Marshall’s pending appeal independently barred a complaint under BCO 43-1. It granted no complaint relief. Separate opinions disputed the first rationale but agreed that the pending appeal barred the complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-03.md) |
| [2013-06](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-06.md) | Gonzales v. Great Lakes Presbytery | Sustained | A minister appealed Great Lakes Presbytery’s divestiture of his office without censure under BCO 34-10. The SJC sustained his claim that Presbytery failed to make required findings that he habitually neglected official functions and that any neglect arose only from lack of acceptance to the Church. It reversed and annulled the divestiture, leaving him a member in good standing, and required no further action. The other four specifications were moot. Presbytery remained free to pursue proper Chapter 31 process or a procedurally compliant BCO 34-10 divestiture. | [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-06.md) |
| [2013-08](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-08.md) | Jackson v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | Jackson challenged Northwest Georgia Presbytery’s failure to act on his BCO 40-5 letter alleging delinquency by Midway’s Session. The SJC found his complaint administratively out of order and dismissed it because he was not a commissioner at Presbytery’s January 2013 meeting; originating a BCO 40-5 report did not confer standing. Separately, the SJC accepted jurisdiction over Presbytery’s failure to respond and remitted the matter for constitutional disposition, without deciding whether the Session committed the alleged delinquencies. | [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-08.md) |
| [2013-10](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-10.md) | Latimer v. Chicago Metro Presbytery | Sustained | A presbytery deposed a minister for violating his seventh vow by pursuing an unbiblical divorce after he filed a civil petition. The SJC sustained the appeal in part: the presbytery clearly erred in finding sin in the filing, because the record did not conclusively show intent to divorce rather than protect his children, and the wife's counter-filing undermined the failure-to-withdraw inference. The SJC rejected prejudice, irregularity, and venue claims, voided the trial judgment, and noted that his expired prior suspension left him a presbytery member without call. Divorce precedents are fact-specific. | [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-10.md) |
| [2013-12](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-12.md) | Marshall v. Pacific Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | G. Rick Marshall appealed Pacific Presbytery, which failed to transmit a material part of the Record of the Case, causing irremediable injury. The SJC did not reach the merits; it suspended the lower judgment indefinitely, dismissed the appeal under BCO 42-7, rebuked Pacific Presbytery, and urged greater care in preserving, transcribing, and transmitting testimony. Dismissal therefore did not leave the challenged judgment enforceable. A separate concurrence suggested recording testimony or hiring a court reporter and questioned imprudent prosecutions. | [full text](../cases/ga43_2015__2013-12.md) |

## 44th General Assembly (2016)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2014-01](../cases/ga44_2016__2014-01.md) | Aven and Dively v. Ohio Valley Presbytery | Partially sustained | Two ministers complained that Ohio Valley Presbytery erred by judging TE Charles Hickey's difference with Larger Catechism 177—over restricting the Lord's Supper to those able to examine themselves—as more than semantic but neither hostile to the system nor vital to religion. The SJC neither sustained nor denied the complaint. Because the Record did not sufficiently address Hickey's particular expression and rationale, the Commission remanded to Presbytery for further hearing and a fuller record. It reserved the merits, including whether this formulation of paedocommunion is allowable. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2014-01.md) |
| [2015-01](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-01.md) | Sanfacon v. Philadelphia Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held that a ruling elder’s complaint was administratively out of order and could not be put in order because the original complaint was filed 78 days after the challenged Session action—18 days beyond BCO 43-2’s 60-day window. A second complaint was merely an extension of the untimely first. Even though Session and Presbytery recognized the untimeliness but addressed it anyway, the SJC voided all lower-court actions on the complaints. A cited 2001 case was similar. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-01.md) |
| [2015-02](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-02.md) | Gearhart v. Chicago Metro Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | An unsigned complaint challenging a January 21 presbytery action was sent to the PCA Clerk by someone without standing. The SJC held the complaint administratively out of order under OMSJC 9.1.a and dismissed it: the minister with standing failed to notify the Clerk within BCO 43-3’s 30-day window, and his later e-mail was not an unqualified ratification. Because the complaint could not be put in order, the SJC reached no merits. The decision illustrates strict filing and ratification requirements. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-02.md) |
| [2015-03](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-03.md) | Flesher and Weekly v. Metro Atlanta Presbytery | Dismissed; Moot | In Complaint 2015-03, Steve Flesher and Randy Weekly challenged Metro Atlanta Presbytery, but the SJC did not reach the merits. Because the issues raised had already been adjudicated in Appeal 2015-08, the SJC declared Complaint 2015-03 moot and approved that disposition. The short decision thus records a mootness ruling based on prior appellate adjudication; the underlying presbytery action and the Appeal 2015-08 decision are not described in the supplied text. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-03.md) |
| [2015-04](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-04.md) | Thompson v. South Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | Thompson used a BCO 43 complaint to challenge his Session indictment during an ongoing disciplinary case, according to the separate concurrence. The SJC ruled the complaint judicially out of order. It held that a defendant must raise such objections during process in the court of original jurisdiction under BCO 32-14 and, if still dissatisfied, pursue them on appeal under BCO 42-3. The ruling did not decide the indictment objection’s merits. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-04.md) |
| [2015-05](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-05.md) | Application of Thompson v. South Florida | Dismissed; No final disposition in available record | A BCO 40-5 application to cite Granada Presbyterian Church for alleged grossly unconstitutional proceedings and discipline delinquencies was found administratively out of order and dismissed as prematurely filed. The SJC held that, even assuming a credible report, BCO 40-5 first required citing the offending court before South Florida Presbytery, the court of appellate jurisdiction. Because Presbytery had docketed the matter for its August 11, 2015 meeting and had not yet ruled, the SJC could not act. No merits ruling on the discipline allegations was made. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-05.md) |
| [2015-06](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-06.md) | Presbyterian Church in America v. South Florida Presbytery (Citation) | No final disposition in available record | Reviewing South Florida Presbytery’s response to a General Assembly citation, the SJC found most responses satisfactory but rejected one because it did not address BCO 32-2’s requirement to proceed once a charge is made. It approved previously unsubmitted 2009 executive-session minutes with an exception because their treatment of a teaching elder appeared to violate BCO 46-8. Presbytery also had to submit amended minutes and complete, or formally plan, overdue Session-record reviews for report to the 45th General Assembly. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-06.md) |
| [2015-07](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-07.md) | Thompson v. South Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC dismissed John B. Thompson's complaint against South Florida Presbytery as administratively out of order and prematurely filed. Because BCO 43-2 did not require the presbytery to consider the complaint until its next stated meeting, and that meeting had not yet occurred, the SJC held the case could not be put in order under the BCO or OMSJC. The unanimous officers' recommendation also called Thompson's BCO 40-5 application premature. No merits were reached. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-07.md) |
| [2015-08](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-08.md) | Hardie v. Metro Atlanta Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | TE John Hardie appealed his Metro Atlanta Presbytery conviction on three charges of breaking ordination vows and indefinite suspension, alleging seventeen procedural and evidentiary errors. Applying BCO 39-2/3 clear-error deference, the SJC denied every specification and upheld the judgment and censure. It found no clear error in the BCO 31-2 investigation, indictment/prosecutor process, commission membership, evidence rulings, scheduling, alleged prejudice, or censure. It assessed transcription costs equally while encouraging MAP to absorb them, and cautioned that an absent commissioner's vote might support a future challenge. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-08.md) |
| [2015-09](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-09.md) | Bumgarner v. Presbytery of the Mississippi Valley  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed; No final disposition in available record | A claimant sought BCO 40-5 review of Presbytery’s handling of his earlier judicial case, alleging grossly unconstitutional proceedings and delinquency in failing to investigate his 2012 deposition. The SJC found the application administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order because BCO 40-3 excludes judicial-case proceedings from review and control when notice of appeal or complaint has been given, and he had withdrawn his 2012 complaint acknowledging it could not be resubmitted. It dismissed under OMSJC 9.2(d). The concurrence added that BCO 40 cannot refile a withdrawn complaint or reverse a censure. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-09.md) |
| [2015-10](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-10.md) | Thompson v. South Florida Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | The SJC dismissed a BCO 40-5 application because the matter concerned proceedings in a judicial case in which a complaint had been filed. Under BCO 40-3, such proceedings may not be handled by review and control once notice of appeal or complaint is given to the lower court. The SJC therefore found the case administratively out of order and not capable of being put in order under OMSJC 9.1.a., and dismissed it under OMSJC 9.2(d). | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-10.md) |
| [2015-11](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-11.md) | Thompson v. South Florida Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled | After a Session excommunicated a member without trial for contumacy, the SJC sustained his complaint and annulled the contumacy judgment, excommunication, and later Session and Presbytery actions. No mutually agreed BCO 38-1 confession existed, and declining a confession meeting was not immediately censurable objective contumacy under BCO 32-6; subjective contumacy required confession or trial. He reverted to membership under pending indictment. The SJC did not find the indictment unconstitutional, decide trial guilt, or consider the unpleaded trespass warning. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-11.md) |
| [2015-12](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-12.md) | Wills v. Metro Atlanta Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | After Metro Atlanta Presbytery dissolved Grace North Atlanta, a complaint challenging that action was declared administratively out of order for lacking a signature. Wills then complained to the SJC. The SJC held the SJC complaint was not properly before it and was administratively out of order: under BCO 43-2, a complaint must first go to the court whose act is challenged, and Presbytery had not yet addressed that ruling. Wills could file with Presbytery within 30 days. No merits ruling on the dissolution. | [full text](../cases/ga44_2016__2015-12.md) |

## 45th General Assembly (2017)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2015-13](../cases/ga45_2017__2015-13.md) | Barnes v. Heartland Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | Heartland Presbytery granted a transferring OPC teaching elder an exception to WCF 19, judging his stated differences 'more than semantic but not out of accord with any fundamental.' The SJC sustained a complaint not against the merits of that judgment but against the sufficiency of the record supporting it. It held Presbytery clearly erred by failing to develop a sufficient record: the presbyters as a whole did not receive OPCUS's adverse actions, its teaching restriction, or the elder's longer paper. Remanded for those materials and release documentation to be included in the minutes. A concurrence addressed deference; four dissenters found no clear error. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2015-13.md) |
| [2016-01](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-01.md) | Aven v. Ohio Valley Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied; Remanded | An SJC complaint alleged that Ohio Valley Presbytery failed to comply with the SJC's prior remand in Case 2014-01, which required it to 'hear further' from TE Charles Hickey about his changed view on Larger Catechism 177. The SJC denied the complaint. It held that the prior decision neither sustained nor denied the original complaint and prescribed no particular examination procedure; Hickey's nine pages of additional affirmations, exegesis, and answers constituted hearing further, and he addressed the five identified areas at least in part. A concurrence believed the SJC could also have adjudicated the confessional-difference merits; a dissent would have found noncompliance and reached the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-01.md) |
| [2016-02](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-02.md) | Robertstad v. North Texas Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC found TE Arnold Robertstad's complaint against North Texas Presbytery administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order under BCO 43-3. Robertstad initially filed by e-mail—not an allowed filing—and his hard-copy filing arrived after the thirty-day deadline. The SJC reasoned that BCO 42-4/43-3 notification language concerns a lower court notifying parties, not party e-filing, and OMSJC 18.10.b bars e-mail filing. The merits were not reached; a concurrence urged later clarification by amendment, and no further proceedings are indicated. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-02.md) |
| [2016-05](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-05.md) | Troxell v. Presbytery of the Southwest  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Annulled | A complaint challenged Presbytery’s institution of two charges against a teaching elder and related pretrial actions. The SJC annulled the household-management charge because process began fifteen months after the alleged offense and the record showed no recently flagrant, newly evident, or continuing offense under BCO 32-20. It also held that declaring the elder not in good standing removed rights without process, but left his properly imposed suspension effective. It rejected objections to discussion, questioning, and the timely submission charge, which could proceed without any determination of guilt. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-05.md) |
| [2016-07](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-07.md) | Avery and Lewelling v. Nashville Presbytery | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | Avery and Lewelling complained that Nashville Presbytery wrongly denied their complaint against Shepherding Committee actions: initiating an investigation of TE Jim Bachmann and forwarding the report to the Committee on Judicial Business. The SJC dismissed. The November 10, 2015 challenge was untimely under BCO 43-2's 60-day filing rule; the February 9, 2016 challenge was not a proper complaint because it targeted committee activities, not a court's act or decision. All specifications were judicially out of order and could not be put in order. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-07.md) |
| [2016-08](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-08.md) | Doty v. Nashville Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | A complainant challenged Nashville Presbytery's handling of allegations against a teaching elder. The SJC held the matter judicially out of order and dismissed it, finding the original filing with the church session was not a BCO 43-1 complaint because the session lacked jurisdiction over the teaching elder. Presbytery properly treated the allegations as BCO 31-2/34-1 reports, and the complainant, not a Nashville member, lacked standing to complain about that new action. A nine-member dissent argued the filing of charges conferred standing under prior SJC cases. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-08.md) |
| [2016-09](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-09.md) | Fordice v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | A church member challenged Presbytery’s denial of his complaint after it resolved accusations against a minister through a BCO 38-1 confession rather than trial. The SJC held that using confession without process was not itself error, but the statement and confession failed to correlate with and address the specific allegations. It sustained the complaint, annulled the denial, and remanded for Presbytery to amend the documents, institute process, reactivate the investigative committee, or otherwise respond directly and specifically to the unresolved allegations. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-09.md) |
| [2016-12](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-12.md) | Harwell et al. v. Nashville Presbytery | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | Complainants sought review of Nashville Presbytery's August 9, 2016 actions but filed directly with the SJC instead of first with Presbytery. The SJC held the complaint judicially out of order and unable to be put in order: the initial filing was not a complaint against an act or decision (BCO 43-1) but suggested charges or an investigation request (BCO 31-2); BCO 43-2 required prior filing with Presbytery, and the sixty-day period had run. The complaint was dismissed without a merits ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-12.md) |
| [2016-14](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-14.md) | Wills v. Metro Atlanta Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Did a presbytery violate the BCO or clearly err by dissolving a congregation without its consent? The SJC said no and denied the complaint. On the constitutional issue, it held that BCO 13-9.f grants dissolution power and BCO 40-6 does not impose formal process for it. On the discretionary issue, it found no clear error in the record. The denial left dissolution in place and warned that such action still requires sufficient justification and prudent, reviewable procedure. | [full text](../cases/ga45_2017__2016-14.md) |

## 46th General Assembly (2018)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2016-06](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-06.md) | Avery v. Nashville Presbytery | administrative | The SJC declared Case 2016-06 to have been abandoned and approved this action without objection. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-06.md) |
| [2016-10](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-10.md) | Korean Northwest Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC approved a motion to accept Korean Northwest Presbytery's amended and corrected responses to exceptions on a roll-call vote; Neikirk dissented and Dowling abstained, with three members absent. The amended responses therefore stand accepted. The source supplies no underlying exception content or reasons, so the action is a procedural acceptance rather than a merits ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-10.md) |
| [2016-11](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-11.md) | Frazier v. Nashville Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | The SJC denied a complaint challenging Nashville Presbytery's decision not to indict a teaching elder for allegedly sinful teaching on homosexuality and same-sex attraction. The adopted judgment held that the 147-page record showed Presbytery fulfilled its BCO 31-2 investigatory duty and did not show it erred in judgment when it declined process; the complaint was denied 20-0-1. Concurring opinions raised concerns about committee recusal, record clarity, and exegetical uses of Scripture, but they were separate and did not alter the denial. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-11.md) |
| [2016-13](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-13.md) | Daniels et al. v. Nashville Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed; Moot | The SJC declared the complaint moot by a vote of 13-6; the substantial dissenting opinion by six members argued the Commission provided no rationale for the mootness determination and that no constitutional basis for declaring it moot was established. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-13.md) |
| [2016-15](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-15.md) | Bachmann v. Nashville Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | TE James Bachmann appealed Nashville Presbytery's decision to replace his judicial commission's recommended admonition with indefinite suspension. The SJC sustained the appeal: Presbytery violated BCO 15-3 by dividing the verdict and amending the censure, which it had to approve or disapprove as a whole without debate. Bachmann's earlier procedural challenges were not sustained; his unconditional post-trial confession waived them. The SJC rendered the commission's judgment and admonition as its own decision, remanded to Presbytery, and restored Bachmann to good standing, subject to possible pastoral oversight items 2–4. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-15.md) |
| [2016-16](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-16.md) | Sartorius v. Siouxlands Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Did a presbytery fail in its duty to condemn erroneous opinions by acquitting a minister on theological-error charges? The SJC denied the complaint, leaving the acquittal undisturbed. A complaint against a not-guilty verdict cannot provoke a retrial at the superior court; under BCO 43 the superior court may only annul or remand, not reverse, and the complainant bore the burden to show error from the record. The record contained the defendant's testimony, including recantations or clarifications, Defense Exhibit 1, and witness testimony sufficient to refrain from contradicting the trial court. The SJC expressly did not decide the defendant's actual views. A separate concurrence elaborated deference standards but did not change the judgment. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-16.md) |
| [2016-17](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-17.md) | Webster & Fowler v. Heritage Presbytery | Denied | Did Heritage Presbytery err by cautioning a session and requesting that it postpone a candidate's election or ordination while a complaint about his qualifications was pending? The SJC said no and denied the complaint. The motion was advice, not an act for or requirement imposed on the session; the session could disregard it, and BCO 43-2 does not bar presbyters from discussing matters related to a complaint not yet before them. The SJC left undecided whether such discussions would disqualify presbyters, since the Ullrey complaint never reached presbytery. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2016-17.md) |
| [2017-01](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-01.md) | Dailey v. Heritage Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Annulled | Heritage Presbytery treated a member's September 2016 filing against her session as a BCO 43-3 complaint and began adjudicating it. The SJC sustained RE Scott Dailey's complaint against that action. BCO 43-3 allows higher-court review only if the lower court denies a complaint or fails to consider it by its next stated meeting. The session timely ruled the filing did not meet BCO 43-1 and did consider it, so neither condition applied. Presbytery's post-November 12, 2016 adjudicative actions were annulled; possible underlying disciplinary matters were left to BCO 31-2 and 40-5. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-01.md) |
| [2017-02](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-02.md) | Postles v. Heritage Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | The SJC denied a complaint challenging Heritage Presbytery’s November 12, 2016 action charging a teaching elder with violating ordination vows 6 and 7 and appointing a prosecutor to investigate and, if necessary, indict. The complaint alleged the investigating committee lacked authority for judicial process, no written report existed, and the procedure and wording violated BCO 31-2. The Commission held a committee recommendation is not judicial action, no special instruction was required, the BCO 31-2 steps were followed, and the wording did not violate the accused’s rights. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-02.md) |
| [2017-03](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-03.md) | Daniels et al. v. Nashville Presbytery | Administratively out of order | The 46th General Assembly record listed Daniels et al.’s complaint against Nashville Presbytery as out of order. The sparse published record does not identify the challenged action, specify an administrative or judicial subtype, provide a reason, or decide the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-03.md) |
| [2017-04](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-04.md) | BCO 40-5 report of RE John B. Thompson | Withdrawn | The 2017-04 BCO 40-5 report of RE John B. Thompson was withdrawn in the 46th General Assembly (2018) record, and no separate merits opinion was published. The available record states no underlying facts, issues, adopted reasons, or relief; it supplies only the docket disposition of the report. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-04.md) |
| [2017-05](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-05.md) | BCO 40-5 Report of TE James Bachmann | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC docket records TE James Bachmann's BCO 40-5 report as out of order. The published record does not identify the procedural defect, describe the underlying allegations, or decide whether an important delinquency or grossly unconstitutional proceeding occurred. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-05.md) |
| [2017-06](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-06_2017-07.md) | Request for Reference from Blue Ridge Presbytery | Denied | Blue Ridge Presbytery asked the SJC to hear and decide a complaint by reference. The SJC denied the request because the record demonstrated neither presbytery-wide disqualification nor serious division, constitutional questions, or new, delicate, or difficult issues warranting reference. Presbytery therefore had to hear and decide the complaint itself. The ruling concerned only the proper court for adjudication and did not decide the complaint’s merits; companion case 2017-07 was answered by reference to this decision. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-06_2017-07.md) |
| [2017-07](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-06_2017-07.md) | Clements v. Blue Ridge Presbytery (Complaint) | Denied | Blue Ridge Presbytery sought to have the SJC decide Clements’s complaint rather than hear it locally. Answering the complaint by reference to companion case 2017-06, the SJC directed Presbytery to hear and decide it because the record did not establish bias, serious division, constitutional questions, or unusually difficult issues warranting reference under BCO 41. The ruling did not decide the complaint’s merits. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-06_2017-07.md) |
| [2017-08](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-08.md) | BCO 40-5 Report of RE John B. Thompson | Out of order (type not stated) | The SJC ruled out of order a BCO 40-5 report submitted by RE John B. Thompson, disposing of it without a separate merits opinion. The available record states no reason for the ruling and identifies no relief or further proceeding; it establishes only the out-of-order disposition, not the underlying dispute or any governing principle. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-08.md) |
| [2017-09](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-09.md) | BCO 40-5 Report of RE John B. Thompson | Out of order (type not stated) | The 46th General Assembly ruled the BCO 40-5 report of RE John B. Thompson out of order. The published record does not describe the report’s allegations, specify an administrative or judicial subtype, or provide reasons or a separate merits opinion. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-09.md) |
| [2017-10](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-10.md) | In re Korean Eastern Presbytery | Referred | The SJC found that Korean Eastern Presbytery complied with its citation by providing the materials required under BCO 40-1, then referred those materials to the Committee for Review of Presbytery Records. Compliance with the production requirement did not itself constitute substantive approval of the submitted records. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-10.md) |
| [2017-11](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-11.md) | In re Korean Southwest Presbytery | Referred | The SJC accepted Korean Southwest Presbytery's responses, approved by the Presbytery, as complying with its citation. It forwarded them to the Review of Presbytery Records Committee. The sparse record identifies no underlying citation grounds, merits analysis, or separate opinion; the practical result was a finding of citation compliance and continued records review. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-11.md) |
| [2017-12](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-12.md) | In re Platte Valley Presbytery | Referred | The SJC unanimously accepted Platte Valley Presbytery's responses, which the Presbytery had approved, as complying with the SJC's citation. It noted that those responses and the requested minutes were forwarded to the Committee on Review of Presbytery Records. The entry does not disclose the underlying citation grounds or evaluate the presbytery's records on the merits; it confirms citation compliance and sends the materials on for ordinary records review. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__2017-12.md) |
| [2017-13](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-13.md) | Tripp v. Ohio Valley Presbytery | Withdrawn | A complaint by RE Michael Tripp against Ohio Valley Presbytery ended when the SJC noted it was withdrawn. No separate merits opinion was published, and the adopted note supplies no reasons or ruling on the presbytery action. The only stated result is withdrawal; no relief was granted or denied on the merits. Because the stub does not describe the challenged conduct, the case is useful only as a short-record withdrawal status. | [full text](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-13.md) |

The sparse published record does not identify the challenged action, specify an administrative or judicial subtype, provide a reason, or decide the merits. | [disposition](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-03.md) |

Thompson was withdrawn in the 46th General Assembly (2018) record, and no separate merits opinion was published. The available record states no underlying facts, issues, adopted reasons, or relief; it supplies only the docket disposition of the report. | [disposition](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-04.md) |

The published record does not identify the procedural defect, describe the underlying allegations, or decide whether an important delinquency or grossly unconstitutional proceeding occurred. | [disposition](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-05.md) |

Thompson, disposing of it without a separate merits opinion. The available record states no reason for the ruling and identifies no relief or further proceeding; it establishes only the out-of-order disposition, not the underlying dispute or any governing principle. | [disposition](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-08.md) |

Thompson out of order. The published record does not describe the report’s allegations, specify an administrative or judicial subtype, or provide reasons or a separate merits opinion. | [disposition](../cases/ga46_2018__stub_2017-09.md) |
