## 47th General Assembly (2019)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2018-02](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-02.md) | Anna Lewis v. Presbytery of the Mississippi Valley  ·  *dissent* | Out of order (type not stated) | After her session erased her from the roll, Anna Lewis filed a BCO 40-5 report directly with the SJC, alleging the Presbytery of the Mississippi Valley failed to redress the session's alleged failure to give BCO 38-4 notice. The SJC held the filing out of order: under RAO 16-10.c and OMSJC Chapter 15, BCO Chapter 40 matters come to it only by General Assembly referral. It did not decide the merits, but noted that without proper notice Lewis remains a member unless she joined another church. Dissents disputed that limitation and cited past direct reviews. | [full text](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-02.md) |
| [2018-03](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-03.md) | Durand v. Central Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC considered whether it could receive directly a BCO 31-2 report affecting Christian character. It ruled the matter administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order because no constitutional provision authorizes such direct receipt. The order did not address the report’s substance or direct further proceedings. | [full text](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-03.md) |
| [2018-04](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-04.md) | Mapes v. Metropolitan New York Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | A complaint sought a congregational meeting to vote on leaving the PCA after the temporary session denied the request in November 2017. The SJC dismissed the complaint as moot and not judicially in order. Both parties agreed at the panel hearing that the presbytery had voted in October 2018 to dismiss the congregation pending consent under BCO 13-9(f), and consent was given at a December 2018 called meeting. No merits ruling was made. Three commissioners dissented; one was disqualified. | [full text](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-04.md) |
| [2018-05](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-05.md) | McKay v. Central Indiana Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held that TE David McKay's complaint against Central Indiana Presbytery was administratively out of order. The decisive defect was procedural: the e-mail transmitted to the lower court clerk did not satisfy BCO 43-3 because the complainant's correspondence was insufficient to constitute proper notice. The SJC therefore reached no merits issue. All 24 participating commissioners concurred, with no dissent. The ruling enforces BCO 43-3's notice requirement before a complaint can proceed. | [full text](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-05.md) |
| [2018-06](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-06.md) | Edwards v. North Texas Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held that Jared Edwards's complaint against North Texas Presbytery was administratively out of order and could not be put in order, because the case had never been filed with the presbytery or adjudicated below (BCO 42-4 & 43-2). No merits were reached. The ruling drew 23 concurrences and no dissent; TE Fowler was disqualified as a member of the presbytery party (OMSJC 2.10(d)(3)(iii)). The decision illustrates the SJC's threshold filing requirement: a complaint that bypassed the lower court cannot proceed. | [full text](../cases/ga47_2019__2018-06.md) |

## 48th General Assembly (2021)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2018-01](../cases/ga48_2021__2018-01.md) | Glen Mapes v. Metropolitan New York Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Moot; Reversed | A ruling elder appealed convictions and censures arising from his brief refusal to leave a closed Temporary Session meeting. The SJC affirmed only his conviction for failing to submit to church government, because he knowingly attended and his apology acknowledged fault; it reversed findings of dereliction, sedition, rioting, and the balance for lack of supporting evidence. It rejected his first four appeal specifications, but held suspension and deposition excessive, substituted Admonition, and restored both sacraments and office, although he belonged to no particular Session. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2018-01.md) |
| [2019-01](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-01.md) | Dodson et al. v. Ohio Presbytery | Partially sustained; Moot | Grace PCA’s Session challenged Ohio Presbytery’s reversal of excommunication for two members convicted of heresy. The SJC partially sustained the complaint: prior indefinite suspension was not constitutionally required, but Presbytery properly found excommunication too severe because the trial record contained no evidence of contumacy. Presbytery nevertheless had to impose an appropriate lesser censure or remand for one, and lacked authority to mandate transfer to another church. The unappealed heresy convictions remained, but because the members had joined another church, further proceedings were moot. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-01.md) |
| [2019-02](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-02.md) | Schrock et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | The SJC sustained a complaint against Philadelphia Presbytery for failing to judge and record TE Larry Smith’s stated difference over the continuation of prophecy and tongues beyond the apostolic era. The record showed Presbytery approved Smith’s transfer exam and defeated a motion to find his view out of accord, but it did not categorize that view in Smith’s own words. The SJC remanded for a written statement and Presbytery’s categorical judgment, without undoing Smith’s transfer or deciding whether his view was erroneous. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-02.md) |
| [2019-03](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-03.md) | Crouse v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | A serving deacon nominated for ruling elder complained that his session removed his nomination before BCO 24-1 instruction or examination. The SJC held the session erred by adding an unwritten pre-examination 'vetting' removal and that Northwest Georgia Presbytery erred in approving it: BCO 24-1's sequence—instruction and examination before the session determines eligibility—is mandatory. The ruling reserved the session's sound discretion and the treatment of frivolous or clearly disqualified nominees. The complaint was sustained and remanded for adjudication consistent with the decision. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-03.md) |
| [2019-04](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-04.md) | Williams v. Chesapeake Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Annulled | An indicted teaching elder challenged Chesapeake Presbytery’s approval of a PJC judgment and indefinite suspension after the PJC blended a BCO 32 case of process with a BCO 38-1 case without process. The SJC sustained the complaint, annulled Presbytery’s approval, and returned the matter to the PJC. Because he admitted only some indicted charges, his negotiated confession could not satisfy BCO 38-1 and the conversion prejudiced his rights. The PJC may dismiss or proceed under BCO 32; the ruling does not decide whether conversion is ever permissible. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-04.md) |
| [2019-05](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-05.md) | Goggan v. Missouri Presbytery | Denied | An excommunicated member appealed the Missouri Presbytery judicial commission's guilty verdict and excommunication, alleging specifications of error. The SJC denied the appeal and did not sustain any specification, holding only that the appellant failed to demonstrate sufficient reason to sustain the alleged errors; the excommunication thus remained in effect. The record noted Presbytery dismissed the commission without the BCO 15-3 vote to approve its report, but the adopted reasoning gave no further explanation and did not address that fact. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-05.md) |
| [2019-06](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-06.md) | Presbyterian Church in America v. Presbytery of the Mississippi Valley  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | In a BCO 40-5 matter, the SJC held that a presbytery erred in upholding a session's erasure of a member who pled not guilty to failing to submit and pursued divorce. The session wrongly equated declining divorce counsel with refusing submission, failed to assess membership duties in another branch, failed to notify her of erasure, and proceeded without process after dropping charges. The SJC sustained the challenge, directed the session to note the notice failure and remove her under BCO 38-3(a), but ordered no express reversal. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-06.md) |
| [2019-07](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-07.md) | Fozard v. North Texas Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | At issue was whether North Texas Presbytery wrongly denied a complaint challenging Fort Worth Presbyterian Church’s “Special Case Felons” integration policy as contrary to Scripture. The SJC denied the complaint, holding that the complainant did not show the Session violated Scripture or the Constitution and that BCO 39-3.3 required deference absent clear error, leaving the lower-court actions in effect. The decision, adopted 20-1, expressly did not endorse the Respondents’ Preliminary Principles arguments; a concurrence disclaimed approval of the policy, and one member dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-07.md) |
| [2019-08](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-08.md) | Ganzel v. Central Florida Presbytery | Sustained | A minister appealed a judgment finding him guilty on most charges and imposing six months’ suspension. The SJC held BCO 32-20 barred Specifications 1, 2, and 4 because Presbytery lacked extraordinary justification for prosecuting old allegations, but did not bar ongoing Specification 3. It separately found the entire process constitutionally flawed and prejudicial. The SJC reversed the whole censure, declined Presbytery’s requested harsher sentence, and concluded the matter. A concurrence disputed the majority’s BCO 32-20 interpretation while agreeing with the result. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-08.md) |
| [2019-09](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-09.md) | Mueller v. South Florida Presbytery | Denied | On complaint by RE William F. Mueller, the SJC reviewed whether the Redlands Community Church Session erred on January 14, 2019 by allegedly reaffirming its practice of fencing the Lord's Table contrary to BCO 58-4. The SJC denied the complaint: the only relevant motion failed for lack of a second, so the Session took no action and did not reaffirm anything. It noted the case could have been ruled out of order earlier and that Mueller could pursue relief under BCO 40-5; a concurrence stressed BCO 58-4 remains binding and criticized Presbytery's handling. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-09.md) |
| [2019-10](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-10_2019-12.md) | Evans and Pitts et al. v. Arizona Presbytery | Sustained | Arizona Presbytery deposed TE John Evans without process after considering investigative-committee observations he had not approved. The SJC sustained the joined complaints, annulled the deposition, and remanded. BCO 38-1 requires the accused and court to approve one full statement of facts; Evans could not approve facts presented after he left. But the provision does not expressly require a meeting or detailed discussion. The underlying matter remained unresolved: Presbytery could proceed with or without process, or dismiss it. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-10_2019-12.md) |
| [2019-12](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-10_2019-12.md) | Pitts et al. v. Arizona Presbytery | sustained | This case was joined with Case 2019-10 (Evans v. Arizona Presbytery) and decided together. RE Pitts and co-complainants challenged the same BCO 38-1 procedural errors in TE Evans's deposition, and the SJC sustained both complaints on the same grounds. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-10_2019-12.md) |
| [2019-11](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-11.md) | Crouse v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | A complaint challenging a session's refusal to advance an ordained deacon's nomination for re-election to the diaconate was denied by the SJC. Northwest Georgia Presbytery had adopted its judicial commission's judgment that the session did not err; applying BCO 39-3(3)'s deference to discretionary judgments, the SJC found no clear error, held formal charges were unnecessary and re-election was not guaranteed, and refused to attribute one elder's questionable retaliation rationale to the session. The majority encouraged prompt communication of reasons. A dissent would apply BCO 39-3.4 and sustain. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-11.md) |
| [2019-13](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-13.md) | Gendy v. Central Florida Presbytery | Sustained; Remanded | A member removed from the rolls under BCO 38-4 challenged Presbytery's ruling that she lacked standing to complain because she was no longer a member. The SJC sustained the complaint: standing under BCO 43-1 cannot be defeated by the very removal challenged. Removal under BCO 38-4 is pastoral discipline, a disciplinary case, not a merely administrative act. The SJC remanded to Presbytery to consider the complaint and take the action it should have taken—remand to the Session. The merits of the removal were not decided. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-13.md) |
| [2019-14](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-14.md) | Yoo et al. v. Korean Southwest Orange County Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | The SJC found the complaint administratively out of order and dismissed it. The complainants had not documented that they first presented the complaint to Korean Southwest Orange County Presbytery under BCO 43-2, and the SJC held that the Presbytery—not the SJC—was the proper court for the charges and specifications in the March 2, 2020 letter. The dismissal was recorded 19-0-0; no merits were reached. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2019-14.md) |
| [2020-01](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-01.md) | COMPLAINT OF MR. PETER BENYOLA VS. CENTRAL FLORIDA PRESBYTERY | dismissed | The SJC dismissed this complaint as administratively out of order on two grounds: the complaint was not timely filed within sixty days of the presbytery actions challenged (ordination/installation of teaching elders and approval of labors outside the bounds), and Mr. Benyola lacked standing as he was neither a member… | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-01.md) |
| [2020-03](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-03.md) | McWilliams v. Southwest Florida Presbytery | dismissed | The SJC dismissed this complaint as administratively out of order because TE McWilliams, who was not the original complainant, attempted to carry to the SJC a complaint that had been filed by TEs Eudaly and Light; BCO 43-3 permits only the original complainant to take a denied complaint to the next higher court. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-03.md) |
| [2020-04](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-04.md) | Marusich v. Central Indiana Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Remanded | Marusich challenged Presbytery’s receipt of a commission’s finding that accusations against a teaching elder did not establish a strong presumption of guilt, and the commission’s failure to provide minutes or a full report. Without answering either issue or identifying an error, the SJC remanded for a new BCO 31-2 investigation, a narrative of the evidence, and a recommendation for Presbytery’s consideration under regular orders. The decision expressly establishes no general rule for BCO 31-2 investigations. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-04.md) |
| [2020-11](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-11.md) | McWilliams v. Southwest Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | The complaint against Southwest Florida Presbytery ended when the SJC approved Complainant David McWilliams's request to withdraw and abandon it, by unanimous roll-call vote (24-0-0) on March 25, 2021. The adopted decision recites no underlying dispute, merits analysis, or reasons and makes no substantive ruling; the complaint stands withdrawn and abandoned. The sparse record is useful only as an example of an approved pre-decision withdrawal/abandonment. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-11.md) |
| [2020-13](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-13.md) | Benyola v. Central Florida Presbytery | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | The SJC dismissed Peter Benyola's complaint against Central Florida Presbytery as administratively out of order because he lacked standing to complain of the Presbytery's action. Benyola was neither a teaching elder member of that Presbytery nor a ruling elder commissioner representing a member church at the September 18, 2020 meeting when the Presbytery considered and denied the complaint. Citing prior SJC standing decisions, the Commission held the defect could not be cured and dismissed unanimously, 22-0-0, without reaching the merits. | [full text](../cases/ga48_2021__2020-13.md) |

## 49th General Assembly (2022)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2020-02](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-02.md) | In the matter of PCA v. Missouri Presbytery | Other | The SJC answered three presbyteries' BCO 34-1 requests concerning Missouri Presbytery by reference to two Speck complaint decisions. One had upheld Missouri's decision not to indict on four allegations; the other had partly sustained a procedural and supervisory complaint and ordered a focused new hearing. The brief ruling gave no separate judgment or reasoning on the original-jurisdiction requests and did not transfer them to another court. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-02.md) |
| [2020-05](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-05.md) | Speck v. Missouri Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained; Remanded | Speck challenged Missouri Presbytery’s approval of six theological judgments concerning teachings at Revoice 18, its rejection of his procedural point of order, and its refusal to debate gay-couple adoption. The SJC sustained only the first issue, particularly as to Judgments 2, 3, and 5: Presbytery improperly imported BCO 34-5’s ministerial-process standard into court review and then inadequately addressed identified errors affecting church peace and purity. It rejected the procedural and adoption claims and remanded for a focused new hearing under BCO 11-3, 11-4, and 13-9(f). | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-05.md) |
| [2020-06](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-06.md) | Gordon v. Southern New England Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | A church member appealed Presbytery’s affirmance of his conviction and excommunication for failing to heed Session admonition and membership vows, including prolonged absence from that church. The SJC affirmed and denied the appeal, holding that all four specifications—lack of reasonable indulgence, haste, prejudice, and injustice—were nonspecific and unsupported. It emphasized his admissions that the charges were true, the adequate timeline, and the bar on considering evidence not offered at trial. A dissent disputed his PCA membership, standing, and the Session’s jurisdiction. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-06.md) |
| [2020-07](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-09.md) | Wilbourne v. Pacific Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled | Pacific Presbytery allowed its Judicial Commission to read a six-page adverse report containing new assertions beyond TE Rankin Wilbourne's agreed BCO 38-1 confession, then ruled his sins "base and flagitious" and deposed him. The SJC sustained his complaint and annulled the deposition, holding BCO 38-1 barred the prejudicial report and BCO 34-7 did not apply without a pending trial. It recommended treating the 17-month censure as sufficient definite suspension, restoring ministerial standing without call, and closing the matter; dissolution of his call was not annulled. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-09.md) |
| [2020-08](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-09.md) | Gendall, Hightower, and Lien v. Pacific Presbytery | Sustained | The SJC sustained the complaint of TEs Gendall, Hightower, and Lien against Pacific Presbytery's handling of a BCO 38-1 confession by TE Rankin Wilbourne. Presbytery had denied the complaint. The SJC held that reading a prejudicial six-page judicial-commission report before the agreed statement of facts violated BCO 38-1 and tainted the censure. It answered the 2020-08 complaint by reference to its decision in Case 2020-07, which annulled Wilbourne's deposition and recommended treating his 17-month suspension as sufficient definite suspension. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-09.md) |
| [2020-09](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-09.md) | Oxbalt et al. v. Pacific Presbytery | Sustained; Annulled | Pacific Crossroads Church’s Session challenged Presbytery’s handling of a minister’s BCO 38-1 confession, particularly its hearing an adverse investigative report outside the agreed statement before imposing deposition. The SJC sustained Case 2020-09 by reference to its consolidated decision and annulled the deposition: BCO 38-1 permits censure to rest only on the mutually approved statement, not additional adverse material. It recommended treating the elapsed 17 months as definite suspension and closing the matter. The minister returned to good standing without call; dissolution of his call remained intact. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-09.md) |
| [2020-10](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-10.md) | Eagle v. Savannah River Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | A complaint was filed with the SJC before Savannah River Presbytery completed its hearing on it. The SJC held the complaint administratively out of order as prematurely filed and did not reach the merits. It reset the complainant's time to elevate the complaint so that timing begins on the later of notification of the SJC action or Presbytery's ruling. The SJC adopted the decision by roll call. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-10.md) |
| [2020-12](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-12.md) | Speck v. Missouri Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Ryan Speck challenged Missouri Presbytery’s BCO 31-2 investigation and its refusal to commence process against Greg Johnson on four allegations concerning same-sex attraction as sin, identity in Christ, sanctification, and fitness for office. The SJC denied the complaint in full. It found no clear procedural error and held that Johnson’s explanations and clarifications made Presbytery’s non-indictment judgments reasonable. No trial was ordered. The ruling neither adjudged Johnson guilty nor endorsed every statement or broader constitutional position concerning same-sex attraction. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-12.md) |
| [2020-14](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-14.md) | Myers v. Illiana Presbytery | denied | TE Aaron Myers appealed his conviction on charges of maltreatment of his wife and fits of anger by Illiana Presbytery; the SJC denied the appeal and upheld all four specifications of error arguments raised by the appellant, affirming the indefinite suspension from office and suspension from the sacraments. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2020-14.md) |
| [2021-01](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-01.md) | Michelson v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held that Stuart Michelson's complaint against Northwest Georgia Presbytery was administratively out of order because he filed it with the SJC before Presbytery had completed its hearing on the complaint. The SJC did not reach the merits. It reset Michelson's time to elevate the complaint to run from the later of notification of the SJC's action or Presbytery's ruling on the complaint. Approved 22-0-0. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-01.md) |
| [2021-02](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-02.md) | Tippins v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | No final disposition in available record | In this BCO 40-5 request, the SJC advised the Stated Clerk that Tippins v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery should be referred to the General Assembly's Review of Presbytery Records Committee. The SJC approved that action 19-3-0; three commissioners dissented. The sparse record supplies no underlying facts, merits analysis, or reason for the referral. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-02.md) |
| [2021-03](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-03.md) | DeJong v. Session of Village Seven Presbyterian Church  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | A ruling elder challenged Session’s elder-term proposal and its creation of Judicial and Governance Commissions. The SJC denied the first two claims: the Constitution does not regulate elder terms, and the Judicial Commission’s assigned business was sufficiently defined. It sustained the Governance Commission claim because sweeping authority and reversed delegation allowed that body to function as Session itself, contrary to BCO 15-1. Session’s third motion was annulled, but Session remained free to adopt a narrower, constitutionally compliant plan. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-03.md) |
| [2021-05](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-05.md) | Eudaly and Light v. Southwest Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held an October 2021 complaint against Southwest Florida Presbytery administratively out of order because the complainants had affiliated with another branch of the visible church on February 9, 2020, ending PCA jurisdiction over them under BCO 38-3. They therefore lacked standing. The ruling reached no merits and supplies no description of the underlying presbytery action; the vote was 22-0-0, with Lucas and White absent. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-05.md) |
| [2021-06](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-06.md) | Herron et al. v. Central Indiana Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Partially sustained | Herron and others challenged Presbytery’s decision to proceed to trial after a BCO 31-2 finding, his administrative suspension, denial of access to Presbytery records and meetings, and a public statement. The SJC sustained only the access issue: suspension from official functions did not remove his membership rights, though it did not entitle him to every investigative-committee document. Finding no clear error on the other issues, it ordered trial to proceed, temporarily stayed censure decisions, and postponed related complaints through trial and any appeal. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-06.md) |
| [2021-07](../cases/ga50_2023__stub_2021-07.md) | RE J. Lance Acree v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery | Administratively out of order | RE Acree attempted to complain directly to Tennessee Valley Presbytery about action taken at a meeting where he was not a commissioner. The SJC found the complaint administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order because BCO 43-1 gave him no standing to complain against that action. It added that Presbytery should itself have declined to adjudicate the complaint. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__stub_2021-07.md) |
| [2021-08](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-08.md) | In re Korean Southwest Orange County Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | As of March 3, 2022, Presbytery had not supplied formally adopted responses to records-review exceptions, although its clerk had prepared drafts. The SJC allowed time for adoption and filing by April 1, after which it would forward the responses for records review; failure to file would prompt a called hearing. This was an unfinished compliance process, not approval of the draft responses or a final merits decision. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-08.md) |
| [2021-09](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-09.md) | Miller v. Ohio Valley Presbytery | denied | Mr. Miller complained that the Session of Grace and Peace PCA erred in refusing to remove 14 items (stained glass windows, etchings, banners) he alleged violated the Second Commandment. The SJC denied the complaint, finding no clear error in the Session's judgment that the items were not images of the Triune God and… | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-09.md) |
| [2021-10](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-10.md) | Williams v. Chesapeake Presbytery | denied | TE Williams appealed Chesapeake Presbytery's approval of his deposition from office and suspension from the Sacraments after trial on three charges (failure to manage household, slander, inappropriate behavior). The SJC denied all five specifications of error and upheld the Presbytery's decision in whole. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-10.md) |
| [2021-12](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-12.md) | Michelson v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | The Michelsons’ complaint challenged a congregational meeting’s procedure for electing three assistant pastors as associate pastors—allegedly without a pulpit committee, ballots, counted abstentions, or protection for a substantial minority. The SJC dismissed it as not judicially in order: BCO 43-1 requires complaint against an act or decision of a court, but the complaint attacked congregational actions, while the Session’s only act was calling the meeting and was not objected to. The SJC noted other possible redress; the dissent found the Session’s call and recommendation justiciable. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-12.md) |
| [2021-13](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-13.md) | Dudt v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Not guilty | A ruling elder appealed convictions for distributing an SJC decision contrary to Session's will and for allegedly false, divisive statements in an email opposing a pastoral-election proposal. The SJC sustained the appeal, reversed Session and Presbytery in whole, rendered not guilty, removed the indefinite suspension, and restored him to office. Session could not bind conscience without scriptural authority, and no express prohibition or proof of falsity existed. Many other specifications were rejected; a concurrence cautioned that such emails are rarely wise. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2021-13.md) |
| [2022-01](../cases/ga49_2022__2022-01.md) | Eagle v. Savannah River Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order; Dismissed | The SJC dismissed Eric Eagle and others' complaint against Savannah River Presbytery as administratively out of order and untimely under BCO 43-3. The complaint was first filed prematurely in December 2020; after the SJC reset the filing clock to the later of its October 21, 2021 action or Presbytery's October 20, 2020 ruling, it was filed more than 30 days later. The merits were not reached. An attached SJC response to CCB separately defended its record-reopening in Case 2020-12 and asked CCB to deny an exception request as untimely, waived, and procedural. | [full text](../cases/ga49_2022__2022-01.md) |

## 50th General Assembly (2023)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2021-11](../cases/ga50_2023__2021-11.md) | Park et al. v. Korean Central Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Remanded | Teaching elders challenged Korean Central Presbytery’s summary censure of a minister before an indictment was prepared. The SJC sustained the complaint and annulled Presbytery’s action because discipline preceded particular charges and a specific confession, and because an independent church lacked authority to impose PCA censure. It declared the underlying matter moot because the alleged event was twenty-one years old and the victim would not cooperate, ending the proceeding without remand. The ruling addressed neither the allegations’ merits nor all old charges categorically. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2021-11.md) |
| [2021-14](../cases/ga50_2023__2021-14.md) | Herron et al. v. Central Indiana Presbytery | Other | Herron challenged Presbytery's handling of its directive that he withdraw his civil defamation lawsuit. The SJC answered the complaint by reference to its completed trial decision in 2022-10, which acquitted him on all seven charges, removed all censures and administrative suspensions, and restored him to ministerial good standing. The incorporated decision found the lawsuit's sinfulness unproved; the complaint notice supplied no separate issue-by-issue ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2021-14.md) |
| [2021-15](../cases/ga50_2023__2021-15.md) | Barber et al. v. Central Indiana Presbytery | Other | Barber and others challenged Presbytery's actions rescinding Herron's contumacy judgment. The SJC answered their complaint by reference to its completed trial decision in 2022-10, which acquitted Herron on all seven charges, removed all censures and administrative suspensions, and restored him to ministerial good standing. That decision found the lawsuit's sinfulness unproved, undermining the related contumacy charge. The complaint notice supplied no separate issue-by-issue ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2021-15.md) |
| [2022-02](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-02.md) | Herron & Baysinger v. Central Indiana Presbytery | Other | The SJC answered this Herron-related complaint by reference to its final decision in the Herron trial, which acquitted him on all seven charges, removed all censures and administrative suspensions imposed by Presbytery, and restored his good standing as a minister. The complaint notice supplies no separate ruling on its individual allegations. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-02.md) |
| [2022-03](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-03.md) | Klett et al. v. Philadelphia Presbytery | Sustained | Philadelphia Presbytery declared that TE Larry Smith's views on continuing prophecy and tongues were not out of accord or hostile to the system of doctrine. Klett et al. complained. The SJC sustained the complaint: Smith's written views affirmed continuing non-general revelation and treated prophecy and tongues as public-worship elements, contrary to WCF 1 and 21, so Presbytery erred under BCO 21-4.f. The SJC annulled Presbytery's May 15, 2021 action. No further action was possible because Smith and his congregation had left the PCA; the matter was concluded, with precedent limited to the written views reviewed. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-03.md) |
| [2022-04](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-04.md) | Sheppard v. Highlands Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | The SJC sustained TE Craig Sheppard's complaint against Highlands Presbytery. It held that Presbytery erred when its Judicial Commission found a strong presumption of guilt but, instead of instituting process under BCO 31-2, directed pastoral reconciliation and conditioned later process on its success; that motion also impaired the accused's right against self-incrimination. It further held that BCO 32-20's one-year time bar had run and that the 2022 amendment did not apply retroactively, so Presbytery is debarred from prosecuting the allegations. Four commissioners dissented, disputing the BCO 32-20 ruling. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-04.md) |
| [2022-05](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-05.md) | Crouse et al. v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated); Moot | Three Ruling Elders complained of errors in judicial process against them. The SJC found the complaint out of order and moot because the Session had dismissed every charge and ended the process, while the elders had voluntarily resigned and requested dissolution of their calls. Because these events removed the challenged action and settled the underlying dispute, the SJC did not reach the alleged procedural errors. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-05.md) |
| [2022-07](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-07.md) | Harrell et al. v. Covenant Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | The SJC sustained an appeal by seven members of a 38-member mission church who were convicted by their temporary Session of opposing the organizing minister's expected election as pastor. Reversing the Presbytery's affirmance and the guilty verdicts underlying indefinite suspensions, the SJC held the indictments fatally lacked BCO 32-5 specificity; Presbytery used the wrong standard of review; the Session had no authority to control pastoral selection; and no Fifth or Ninth Commandment offense was proved. Challenges to witness-judges, venue, and refusal to refer were rejected. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-07.md) |
| [2022-08](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-08.md) | David Snoke v. Pittsburgh Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | No final disposition in available record | The SJC held that RE David Snoke's complaint against Pittsburgh Presbytery's January 29, 2022 action was administratively out of order and could not be put in order because Snoke, not a commissioner from his church to that meeting, lacked standing to complain. Although Presbytery later voted 20-16-3 to find the complaint in order, the SJC said it should have been ruled out of order. The SJC did not reach the merits. Its vote was 20-1, with three absent. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-08.md) |
| [2022-09](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-09.md) | Benyola v. Central Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | The SJC declared Benyola's complaint against Central Florida Presbytery administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order, because Benyola was no longer a member of any PCA congregation and thus lacked standing. The decision was adopted 23-0 on a roll call, one absent, on the officers' recommendation. The record does not identify the underlying presbytery action or reach the complaint's merits, so the case's value is its threshold standing ruling, not any complaint determination. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-09.md) |
| [2022-10](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-10.md) | Presbyterian Church in America v. TE Daniel Herron | Not guilty | On a BCO 41 reference, the SJC tried a minister on seven charges arising from allegations of sexual misconduct, bullying, dishonesty, a civil defamation suit, contumacy, doctrinal error, and lack of fitness. It found him not guilty on every charge because the prosecution failed to meet its burden, removed all censures and administrative suspensions, and restored him to good standing. For the lawsuit charge, it rejected a categorical ban on civil suits between Christians and required proof that this suit was sinful. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-10.md) |
| [2022-11](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-11.md) | BCO 34-1 Petitions re: Central Indiana Presbytery | Other | The SJC had postponed BCO 34-1 petitions concerning Central Indiana Presbytery until the Herron trial concluded. After acquitting Herron on all charges and restoring him to good standing, the SJC answered and decided the petitions by reference to that final trial decision. The petitions were thus resolved through incorporation of the trial result, not transferred to another court; the trial itself arose from a distinct BCO 41 reference. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-11.md) |
| [2022-12](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-12.md) | BCO 34-1 Petitions re: Missouri Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | The SJC dismissed two BCO 34-1 overtures asking the General Assembly to assume original jurisdiction over alleged doctrinal errors by TE Greg Johnson. After Memorial Presbyterian Church withdrew from the PCA and Missouri Presbytery removed Johnson's name from its rolls under BCO 38-3a, the SJC held it lacked authority to assume original jurisdiction because the PCA no longer had jurisdiction over him. The dismissal did not resolve the doctrinal allegations; one commissioner dissented. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-12.md) |
| [2022-13](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-13.md) | Miller v. Hills & Plains Presbytery | Dismissed | Wade Miller challenged his Session’s May 19, 2021 letter standing by its decision to permit a non-church yoga class to meet on church property. The SJC held the complaint administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order: that letter triggered BCO 43-1’s 60-day deadline, but Miller filed with the Session on November 20, 2021, and no new Session action occurred within the preceding 60 days. Citing a one-day-late precedent, the SJC reached no merits. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-13.md) |
| [2022-14](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-14.md) | Oh v. Korean Southeast Presbytery | Dismissed; Withdrawn | The SJC dismissed Oh’s complaint against Korean Southeast Presbytery following his withdrawal of it. The brief decision does not identify the challenged presbytery action, address the merits, or grant substantive relief. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-14.md) |
| [2022-15](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-15.md) | Murdock v. South Florida Presbytery | No final disposition in available record | A complaint against South Florida Presbytery was held administratively out of order because its sole complainant died on November 15, 2022, and no other person joined the original complaint. The SJC adopted that disposition by a 23–0 roll-call vote. The sparse record states no underlying complaint issue, merits analysis, or further proceedings; the ruling turns on the absence of a surviving or joined complainant. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-15.md) |
| [2022-16](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-16.md) | Michelson et al. v. Northwest Georgia Presbytery | Out of order (type not stated); Dismissed; Moot | The SJC declined to reach a complaint arising from judicial process against three ruling elders (not the complainants), because the Session dismissed all charges and ended that process on July 23, 2022, and the elders resigned and their calls were dissolved. The SJC found the complaint out of order and moot, with no merits ruling, and approved the decision 23-0. The case illustrates that termination of the underlying judicial process and voluntary resignations can moot a complaint about that process. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-16.md) |
| [2022-17](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-17.md) | Peter Benyola v. Central Florida Presbytery (three complaints) | Administratively out of order | Standing to complain: The SJC found three complaints by Peter Benyola against Central Florida Presbytery administratively out of order and incapable of being put in order because Benyola was no longer a member of any PCA congregation and thus lacked standing. The SJC approved the ruling 23-0 on March 2, 2023; the supplied record does not describe the underlying allegations. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-17.md) |
| [2022-20](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-20.md) | Wilson et al. v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Dismissed | Members challenged a voting restriction imposed at a congregational meeting. The SJC dismissed the complaint as judicially out of order because BCO 43-1 permits complaints against acts or decisions of church courts, and a congregation is not such a court. It identified other possible routes through a report to Presbytery or review of church records. The adopted decision did not decide whether the voting restriction was constitutionally valid. | [full text](../cases/ga50_2023__2022-20.md) |

## 51st General Assembly (2024)

| Case | Parties / Title | Disposition | Summary | Page |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [2022-21](../cases/ga51_2024__2022-21.md) | Senters v. Savannah River Presbytery | Partially sustained; Remanded | A minister challenged Presbytery’s procedures and its classification of a transfer candidate’s views on continuing tongues as “not a difference.” The SJC rejected the procedural objections because Presbytery could disregard its committee’s recommendation and had breached no required procedure. It nevertheless held that a difference need not overtly contradict the Standards and that the candidate’s statements implicated WCF 1.1 and 1.6. It annulled the classification and remanded for clarification and a new judgment, without deciding whether the clarified view would be acceptable. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2022-21.md) |
| [2022-22](../cases/ga51_2024__2022-22.md) | Turner v. South Florida Presbytery | Partially sustained; Annulled; Remanded | Presbytery investigated a Session's handling of sexual-misconduct allegations and found it guilty of delinquencies without a trial. The SJC upheld Presbytery's authority to receive credible reports and investigate, but annulled the findings of guilt, censures, and corrective mandates because BCO 40-6 required formal process or an admission before censure. It returned the matter for records review, informal resolution, or formal process and declined interim mandates that would assume the disputed allegations were true. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2022-22.md) |
| [2022-23](../cases/ga51_2024__2022-23.md) | Woodham v. South Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | South Florida Presbytery deposed a Teaching Elder, suspended him from the sacraments, and later excommunicated him without first assigning him to a particular church under BCO 46-8. The complainant argued that omission stripped Presbytery of jurisdiction. The SJC denied the complaint: BCO 46-8 requires assignment but fixes no timeframe or mechanism; a deposed minister remains under Presbytery's jurisdiction until transfer, and BCO 37-9(a) preserves censure authority. Deferring under BCO 39-3, the SJC found no clear error in Presbytery's efforts. Two concurrences agreed in result but disputed the majority's broad reasoning and deference. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2022-23.md) |
| [2023-01](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-01.md) | Hann v. Pee Dee Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Dismissed | Kristin Hann's complaint against Pee Dee Presbytery was dismissed because the SJC held she lacked standing: she was not subject to Pee Dee Presbytery's jurisdiction, so the complaint was judicially out of order and could not be put in order. The SJC made no merits ruling and stated that the ruling did not stay Presbytery's continuing investigation or review of resulting actions. The adopted ruling was approved 17-2; Maynard and Sartorius dissented without stated reasons, four were absent, and Ross recused. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-01.md) |
| [2023-02](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-02.md) | Reynolds v. Pee Dee Presbytery | Dismissed | The SJC dismissed Mrs. Kappie Reynolds's complaint against Pee Dee Presbytery as judicially out of order. The complaint challenged a Session's reception of and action on a confession at a meeting more than six months before filing, far outside BCO 43-2's sixty-day period; the SJC held the untimeliness was not demonstrated curable. The ruling expressly reserved whether Reynolds could proceed as an injured party under BCO 31-5 and whether the Session had jurisdiction over claims outside the received confession. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-02.md) |
| [2023-04](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-04.md) | Biese et al. v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Biese and others complained that Tennessee Valley Presbytery erred by approving Redeemer Church session minutes without exception—minutes noting a youth Super Bowl party—and by denying their complaint seeking a Sabbath exception. The SJC denied the complaint: because Presbytery gave no reasons for its actions, the record was insufficient to find it erred in interpreting the Constitution. A concurrence said the denial did not decide the Sabbath merits; a dissent would have held the complaint judicially out of order for lack of SJC jurisdiction over presbytery record review. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-04.md) |
| [2023-06](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-06_2023-08.md) | Knox Baird et al. v. Grace Presbytery | Partially sustained; Denied; Moot | Session elders challenged Presbytery’s handling of a mediation complaint, investigative directives, BCO 40-5 reports, and a trial stay. The SJC held that the mediation complaint was never formally filed and became moot; official-record requests were proper, but compelling nonrecord personal communications and requiring the Session to address its congregation exceeded Presbytery’s authority. It vacated the communications directive and annulled the stay for lack of the required prior citation. It upheld committee examination of the BCO 40-5 reports and did not reach alleged partiality; the Session trials were to proceed. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-06_2023-08.md) |
| [2023-08](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-06_2023-08.md) | Knox Baird et al. v. Grace Presbytery | Partially sustained; Denied | Session elders challenged Presbytery’s handling of a withdrawn mediation complaint, document and congregational-notice directives, BCO 40-5 reports, and a stay of Session trials. The SJC sustained the complaints in the main: the mediation complaint was never formally filed and became moot; Presbytery could require official record materials but not compel personal communications or force the Session to address its congregation; and the stay lacked a prior BCO 40-5 citation. It upheld appointing a committee to examine the reports, did not reach alleged partiality, vacated the overbroad directive, annulled the stay, and returned the trials to Session. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-06_2023-08.md) |
| [2023-07](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-07.md) | Evans v. Arizona Presbytery | Sustained; Reversed | Arizona Presbytery divested a minister without censure for prolonged lack of call after he had joined an EPCEW congregation and notified Presbytery of his withdrawal. The SJC sustained his appeal and reversed the divestiture because BCO 38-3(a) had already ended Presbytery's jurisdiction while he was in good standing and under no investigation or charges. It directed Presbytery to record the irregular withdrawal, acknowledge his new membership, and remove him from its roll. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-07.md) |
| [2023-09](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-09.md) | Myers v. Illiana Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | An elder appealed Presbytery’s elevation, without additional process, of suspensions from office and the Sacraments to deposition and excommunication. The SJC granted the appeal, reversed in whole, and remanded, restoring the prior suspensions. Elevation required a hearing on incorrigibility and contumacy—with a charge, evidence, plea, and defense—but not a full de novo trial; disagreement with the original verdict alone was insufficient. The SJC left unresolved deposition from office when excommunication is absent. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-09.md) |
| [2023-10](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-10.md) | Martinez & Cook v. Pacific Presbytery | Partially sustained | Elders challenged Presbytery’s authority and vague instruction for its Shepherding Committee to “follow up” with their Session. The SJC denied the constitutional challenge because the record reasonably supported concern for the church’s peace and spiritual welfare, but sustained the challenge to Presbytery’s lack of specific direction and poor communication. The decision distinguishes prohibited mandatory visitation without cause from a reasonably justified visit, while requiring clarity about its objectives. It did not decide the underlying doxology dispute or order further relief. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-10.md) |
| [2023-11](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-11.md) | Psiaki v. Pacific Northwest Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained | The SJC sustained Psiaki’s complaint and held that the CPC Session erred by ordaining and installing officers elected at a February 5, 2023 congregational meeting where the congregation’s bylaw excluded communing members under 18 from voting. Reading BCO 6-4, 25-1, and 25-7 together, the SJC ruled that all communing members are entitled to vote and that local bylaws cannot impose an age restriction; the “good and regular standing” language addresses censure, not age. The decision did not annul the completed ordination/installation, but required future elections and installations to comply with the Constitution. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-11.md) |
| [2023-13](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-13.md) | BCO 40-5 Matter re Metropolitan NY  ·  *dissent* | Remanded | A BCO 40-5 citation arose from Metro New York Presbytery's failure to redress a session that allowed a woman to expound Scripture in place of the sermon preceding the Lord's Supper, held services without preaching, and celebrated the Supper without a preceding sermon. The SJC remitted the matter to the presbytery for constitutional resolution because the session's later no-exception response was clearly inadequate and the unapproved minutes were unclear. Presbytery must complete its work with TE Higgins and Trinity Presbyterian Church's session and report to the 51st General Assembly. A separate procedural note questioned the Assembly's direct referral but did not void it. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-13.md) |
| [2023-14](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-14.md) | BCO 40-5 Matter re NW Georgia  ·  *dissent* | Other; Denied | Presbytery approved calls and installed three associate pastors after a constitutionally deficient election. The SJC found egregious error, including slate voting, a standing rather than ballot vote, and failure to determine and record the number of voters present under the then-current rule. It nevertheless denied the requested annulment of the installations because PCA judicial proceedings supplied no precedent and the remedy's legitimacy was doubtful. The SJC stayed further proceedings and declared the report concluded. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-14.md) |
| [2023-15](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-15_2023-17.md) | Tom Turner v. South Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | Turner complained against Presbytery Judicial Commission actions deposing TE Tommy Boland and dissolving Cross Community Church’s relationship with Presbytery. The SJC ruled both complaints administratively out of order for lack of standing and incapable of being put in order. Cross Community had already disaffiliated through its first congregational motion; the second motion, which contemplated later implementation, was out of order as contingent. Because no Presbytery action was required to effect disaffiliation, Turner was no longer a PCA member. The underlying challenges were not decided. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-15_2023-17.md) |
| [2023-17](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-15_2023-17.md) | Tom Turner v. South Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Administratively out of order | Turner’s two complaints challenged the deposition of TE Tommy Boland and the dissolution of Cross Community Church’s relationship with Presbytery, although the record does not identify which challenge was Case 2023-17. In a combined ruling, the SJC held both complaints administratively out of order for lack of standing. The congregation’s first withdrawal vote immediately satisfied BCO 25-11; its second, contingent timing motion was out of order. Because the church had already left the PCA, Turner was no longer a member, and no merits relief was available. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-15_2023-17.md) |
| [2023-19](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-19.md) | Huffman v. Tennessee Valley Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Judicially out of order; Remanded | A filing challenging Presbytery’s handling of Huffman’s BCO 38-1 confession was styled as an appeal under a newly amended rule. The SJC found it judicially out of order because the case began and was nearly completed when the former rule provided review by complaint, and retroactive application would be unfair. It remanded for Presbytery to adjudicate the filing as a complaint at its next stated meeting; denial or failure to act permits review by the SJC. No merits issue was decided. | [full text](../cases/ga51_2024__2023-19.md) |

| [2023-05](../cases/ga52_2025__2023-05.md) | Hyland v. Chicago Metro Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Denied | Former church employee Emily Hyland complained to the SJC after Chicago Metro Presbytery ruled her BCO 43 complaint out of order because, as a local-church member, she was not subject to Presbytery's jurisdiction. The SJC was equally divided 12–12–0 on the proposed panel decision, so CMP's ruling stands. That proposed decision would have answered that CMP did not err: Hyland lacked standing to file a BCO 43-1 complaint with Presbytery. No amendments were made. An evenly divided SJC thus leaves the lower ruling in place without a majority rationale. | [full text](../cases/ga52_2025__2023-05.md) |

| [2025-12](../cases/ga52_2025__2025-12_2025-13.md) | Felipe Assis and Carter Brown v. South Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | Two ministers appealed Presbytery’s divestiture of their offices after their congregations left the PCA. The SJC reversed because their calls had never been formally dissolved, so they were not automatically without call, and BCO 34-10 did not apply while they remained actively engaged and accepted in ministry. The SJC could not judge their fairness and pretext claims from the record. It restored them to good standing and required Presbytery to dissolve the existing calls before pursuing lawful alternatives. | [full text](../cases/ga52_2025__2025-12_2025-13.md) |
| [2025-13](../cases/ga52_2025__2025-12_2025-13.md) | Felipe Assis and Carter Brown v. South Florida Presbytery  ·  *dissent* | Sustained; Reversed | Two ministers appealed Presbytery’s decision to divest them of office without censure after their congregations left the PCA. The SJC sustained the BCO-violation specification and reversed the divestitures: their calls had never been formally dissolved, and BCO 34-10 did not apply because they continued performing ministerial functions and remained accepted by their churches. It declined as unable to judge claims that Presbytery disregarded their emails or used the procedure as discriminatory pretext. The ministers returned to good standing; Presbytery must dissolve their existing PCA calls and then pursue a BCO-consistent option. | [full text](../cases/ga52_2025__2025-12_2025-13.md) |
